**Just as unheated iron cannot burn, the body, senses, life airs and intelligence move to their objects only when endowed with consciousness by the Lord. The jīva is the seer during waking, dreaming and sleep states in name only*****.*** **SB 6.16.24**

The commentary says, “The prāṇas (*asavaḥ*) cannot touch, by the kriya-śakti, Brahman which spreads like ether. The mind and other factors cannot know Brahman by jñāna-śakti. I offer respects to that Brahman. The cause of the mind and senses not knowing Brahman is then explained. The body, senses, mind and prāṇas, endowed with a portion of Brahman’s consciousness, move towards their various objects. Without the Lord, they are like unheated iron, which cannot burn. Just as iron becomes capable of burning by the śakti of fire, but cannot burn fire itself, the body, senses, mind and prāṇas, moved to action by jñāna and kriya śaktis of Brahman cannot touch or know Brahman.”

In the Advaita commentary on Brahma-sūtras (1.1.5) the Sāṅkhya philosophy is criticized. “Just as, because of fire, a piece of iron has the ability to burn, because of the witness which glances at pradhāṇa, pradhāna has the power to create a universe. If that is the case, since Brahman glancing at pradhāna is the cause, Brahman is the principle cause of creating the universe.”

Śruti says:

*na tatra sūryo bhāti na candra-tārakaṁ*  
*nemā vidyuto bhānti kuto ’yam agniḥ*  
*tad eva bhāntam anubhāti sarvaṁ*  
*tasya bhāsā sarvam idaṁ vibhāti*

**There, the sun, the moon, the stars, fire and lightning do not shine. They all shine only by following the shining Lord. His light illuminates all these.** ***Kaṭha Upaniṣad*** **5.15**

*ko hy evānyāt kaḥ prāṇyāt yad eṣa ākāśa ānando na syāt*

**Who could breathe without this Brahman, if this blissful Brahman were not present? Taittarīya Upaniṣad 2.7.1**

*cakṣuṣaś cakṣur uta śrotrasya śrotram*

**He is the eye of the eye, the ear of the ear. (*****Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.18*****)**

The commentary then says, “It could be proposed that the jīva knows, because it is a seer. That is not so. The jīva is a seer in name only *(draṣṭrapadeśam*) in waking, dreaming and deep sleep.

*nānyo jīvo nāmāsti nānyo’to’sti draṣṭā:*

**What is called the jīva is not independent (*****anyaḥ*****). Rather it has a similar nature to Brahman. There is no other seer than the Lord who sees all. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.17.23**

Or another meaning of the last line is “The Lord knows the jīva defined as a seer. The jīva cannot know the Lord” for it is said:

*ekam ekatarābhāve yadā nopalabhāmahe \|*  
*tritayaṁ tatra yo veda sa ātmā svāśrayāśrayaḥ \|\|*

**Since we cannot perceive one item in absence of one of the other items (subtle sense, sense deity and gross form), he who is independent of these three, is the ātmā. But the shelter of the ātmā is the Paramātmā. SB 2.10.9**

Nārada speaks to Citraketu.

## 18.

*deho 'savo 'kṣā manavo bhūta-mātrām*  
*ātmānam anyaṁ ca viduḥ paraṁ yat*  
*sarvaṁ pumān veda guṇāṁś ca taj-jño*  
*na veda sarva-jñam anantam īḍe*

**The body, life airs, senses, internal senses, gross elements and sense objects do not know themselves or other things or the jīva. The jīva knows all of these items and the guṇas which cause them. He does not really know the omniscient Lord. I worship that Lord with infinite qualities. SB .4.25**

The body, prāṇas, senses, antaḥkaraṇa *(manavaḥ*), elements (*bhūta),* tan-mātras, do not know their own svarūpas, nor the objects of their action (*anyam*), or the devatās (*param*) superior to these organs and their objects. The jīva (*pumān*) knows everything-- himself, other things like the senses, and the devatās of the organs from which arise the body, prāṇa, senses etc. And as well he knows sattva and other guṇas which are the basis of the body and senses. The jīva knows all these. I worship that Lord, with unlimited śaktis, knower of unlimited things, all the body and jīvas, whom the jīva does not know.

*kutaḥ punar gṛṇato nāma tasya*  
*mahattamaikānta-parāyaṇasya \|*  
*yo ’nanta-śaktir bhagavān ananto*  
*mahad-guṇatvād yam anantam āhuḥ \|\|*

**What doubt is there about this for one who chants the name of the unlimited Lord, who is the shelter of the greatest devotees, who has unlimited power, and who is called unlimited because he bestows his unlimited qualities to the devotee? SB 1.18.19**

*yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati tad itara itaraṁ paśyati*  
*yatra tv asya sarvam ātmaivābhūt tat kena kaṁ paśyet*

**In the universe there appears to be duality, difference from the Lord. In this world the jīva sees objects. In the spiritual world the Paramātmā of the jīva exists as everything by his svarūpa-śakti. There the jīva cannot see objects by his material senses. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.5.15**

The jīva cannot see Paramātmā and Paramātmā can see himself and everything else. *Vijñātāram are kena vijānīyāt:* by what senses can the knower be known? He is the Paramātmā (*ātmā*) of the jīva (*asya*) in whose svarūpa exist all śaktis (*yatra sarvam abhūt*). He cannot be known by any other being.

The meaning is this. In the world, the product of māyā, there seems to be difference (*yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati*). Though non-different from the Lord since its source is the Lord, the world appears completely different with insentience, contamination and temporary nature by the inconceivable śakti called māyā. It appears to have an independent existence at all times. In that world (*tat*) the jīva *(itaraḥ*) sees objects (*itaram*) because of the suitability of the sense organs and the object perceived. In the spiritual world (*yatra*), the Paramātmā of the jīva (*asya*), like the sun in relation to its ray, exists as everything, by his svarūpa-śakti. He exists there eternally, not by having attained his śakti at some time. *(yatra tv asya sarvam ātmaivābhūt*) In the spiritual world (*tat*), the jīva sees what objects (*kam*) by what senses *(kena*)? (*tat kena kaṁ paśyet*) He cannot see anything by any means. The rays cannot reveal the glorious world within the sun by their power. Light cannot burn fire. By what senses can one know Paramātmā, the knower of everything (*vijñātāram),* who has powers within his svarūpa? No senses can know him. What was mentioned concerning jñāna-śakti applies to kriya and iccha śaktis as well. Dakṣa speaks to the Lord.

## 19.

Māyā is describes as being under the Lord’s control:

*sa tvaṁ hi nitya-vijitātma-guṇaḥ sva-dhāmnā*  
*kālo vaśī-kṛta-visṛjya-visarga-śaktiḥ*  
*cakre visṛṣṭam ajayeśvara ṣoḍaśāre*  
*niṣpīḍyamānam upakarṣa vibho prapannam*

**O Lord! O supreme power! You conquer the material guṇas contained in the jīva’s intelligence at all times by your svarūpa-śakti. You are time which agitates the guṇas. You destroy ignorance in the subtle body. Please bring near you that person who has been thrown in the wheel in the wheel of sixteen spokes by ignorance and is being squeezed like a piece of sugar cane. SB 7.9.22**

*Sva-dhamnā* means “by his cit-śakti.” Kāla is the stimulator of māyā. You control the material guṇas of the jīva *(ātmā*).

*sattvaṁ rajas tama iti guṇā jīvasya naiva me*  
*citta-jā yais tu bhūtānāṁ sajjamāno nibadhyate*

**The three modes of material nature—goodness, passion and ignorance—influence the living entity but not me. Manifesting within the minds of all beings, they induce the living entity to become attached to material bodies and to become bound up. SB 11.25.12**

Prahlāda speaks to Nṛsiṁha.

## 20.

*karoti viśva-sthiti-saṁyamodayaṁ*  
*yasyepsitaṁ nepsitam īkṣitur guṇaiḥ*  
*māyā yathāyo bhramate tad-āśrayaṁ*  
*grāvṇo namas te guṇa-karma-sākṣiṇe*

**By your glance, you carry out creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe by** ***māyā,*** **though it is not desired by you, but only by the** ***jīva.*** **Just as a piece of iron moves forward because of a magnet,** ***māyā*** **moves by the influence of the Lord. I offer respects to the Lord, the witness of the** ***guṇas*** **and the** ***jīva’s karmas*****. SB 5.18.38**

The commentary says, “The Lord does not desire the creation for himself *(ikṣituḥ*), but he desires it for the jīvas. He does not desire it at all since prakṛti is not suitable to glance upon. He does not desire creation for himself. Thus māyā carries out the creation by her guṇas. Since māyā is insentient, by proximity to the Lord māyā can act. An example of the action is given. Iron, near to the magnet (*tad-āśrayam),* moves because of the magnet. I offer respects to the Lord who is the witness of the guṇas and karmas of the jīva.” The earth speaks to Varāha.

## 21.

If the Lord mixes with māyā, liberation would be impossible. If liberation cannot be achieved, then bliss cannot be achieved. Maya is described to show that the Lord does not mix with it at all.

*tvam ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣād īśvaraḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ \|*  
*māyāṁ vyudasya cic-chaktyā kaivalye sthita ātmani \|\|*

**You are the original Lord, the controller, beyond material energy. You are situated in your spiritual form, being one with your spiritual energy, and separate from the material energy. SB 1.7.23**

You yourself (*sākṣāt*) are Bhagavān, the first puruṣa (*ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ*). You are also the antaryāmī puruṣa (*īśvaraḥ*). You do not associate with prakṛti (*prakṛteḥ paraḥ*) as Bhagavān or Paramātmā.

“How can one experience the bliss of kaivalya if Brahman is defined as Bhagavān? If he is the controller of prakṛti as īśvara, how can he not associate with prakṛti?” You keep this ābhāsa-śakti far away by your fixed svarūpa-śakti, and by your svarūpa-śakti you are situated in your svarūpa *(ātmani)* which is full of its own experience of bliss (*kaivalye).*

*kālenātmānubhāvena sāmyaṁ nītāsu śaktiṣu*  
*sattvādiṣv ādi-puruṣaḥ pradhāna-puruṣeśvaraḥ*  
*parāvarāṇāṁ parama āste kaivalya-saṁjñitaḥ*  
*kevalānubhavānanda- sandoho nirupādhikaḥ*

**When he brought his energies such as sattva to equilibrium by his powerful energy of time, the Lord, who enjoys in himself, who is the controller of prakṛti and the jīvas, who is worshipped by liberated and conditioned jīvas, remained in the form of complete bliss called kaivalya, without māyā. SB 11.9.17-18**

It is also said *svayam upalabdha-nija-sukhānubhavo bhavān*: you experience happiness within yourself. (SB 6.9.33) The word *sandoha* (profusion of bliss) indicates variety, which arises because of variety in the śakti. This is the svarūpa-śakti. Prakṛti refers to the māyā composed of three guṇas. Śrīdhara Svāmī has described the three śaktis in this way.

Devahūti says:

*paraṁ pradhānaṁ puruṣaṁ mahāntaṁ*  
*kālaṁ kaviṁ tri-vṛtaṁ loka-pālam*  
*ātmānubhūtyānugata-prapañcaṁ*  
*svacchanda-śaktiṁ kapilaṁ prapadye*

**I surrender unto Kapila, whose other form is prakṛti, who is also the jīva, the mahat-tattva, time, sūtra, ahaṅkāra, the devatās of the directions, the universe inhabited by your internal energy, and the possessor of independent energies. SB 3.24.33**

I surrender to the Lord (*param)* who is in this position because he has his independent śaktis (*svacchanda-śaktim*). The śakti is described. You are prakṛti (*pradhānam*). You are the puruṣa (the controller of prakṛti). You are mahat-tattva (*mahāntam*) and time, the agitator of prakṛti and mahat-tattva. You are ahaṅkāra (*tri-vṛtam*) which produces the planets and their protectors. After describing the forms produced by māyā, the forms are described as merging into him by the cit-śakti. I offer respects to the Lord in whom the world is merged by his cit-śakti (*ātmānubhūtyā*)and who is omniscient *(kavim*), the witness of the manifestation of pradhāna.

The puruṣa is included in māyā as its controller. Factually the puruṣa is beyond māyā. Kapila says:

*anādir ātmā puruṣo nirguṇaḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ*  
*pratyag-dhāmā svayaṁ-jyotir viśvaṁ yena samanvitam*

**This ātmā is the puruṣa, who is eternal, without material qualities, since he is beyond prakṛti; who is beyond the senses; who has a spiritual abode; who is self-manifesting, and from whom this universe, owned by him, appears.**

Mahā-saṁhitā describes the three śaktis by name and nature.

*śrīr bhūr durgeti yā bhinnā jīva-māyā mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*ātma-māyā tad-icchā syāt guṇa-māyā jaḍātmikā \|\|*

**Śrī, Bhū and Durgā are different names for jīva-māyā śakti or material śakti of the Lord, for maintenance, creation and destruction of the universe. It is my svarūpa-śakti as my desire and also material śakti as matter. Mahā-saṁhitā**

Śrī is the śakti to protect the universe. Bhū is the śakti for creation of the universe. Durgā is the śakti of destruction. The śakti takes these different forms. That śakti with jīva as its object is called jīva-māyā. It is said in Padma Purāṇa:

*aham eva tridhā bhinnā tiṣṭhāmi trividhair guṇaiḥ*

**I exist in three different forms with the three guṇas.**

*tataḥ sarve’pi te devāḥ śrutvā tad-vākya-coditāḥ \|*  
*gaurīṁ lakṣmīṁ dharāṁ caiva praṇemur bhakti-tat-parāḥ \|*

**All the devatās hearing this, inspired by those words, filled with devotion, offered respects to Gaurī (Durgā), Lakṣmī (Śrī) and Dharā (Bhū).**

*eṣā māyā bhagavataḥ sarga-sthity-anta-kāriṇī*  
*tri-varṇā varṇitāsmābhiḥ kiṁ bhūyaḥ śrotum icchasi*

**I have now described māyā, the illusory energy of the Supreme Lord, which consists of the three modes of material nature, and causes creation, maintenance and annihilation of the material universe. Now, what more do you wish to hear? SB 11.3.16**

Ātma-māyā refers to the svarūpa-śakti. Māyā means that by which something is measured or produced or known. Thus māyā can refer to any śakti in general.

*tamyāṁ tamovan naihāraṁ khadyotārcir ivāhani*  
*mahatītara-māyaiśyaṁ nihanty ātmani yuñjataḥ*

**As the darkness of snow on a dark night cannot cover darkness and the light of a glowworm in the light of day cannot reveal the sun, the power of an inferior person who tries to use it against a person of great power destroys his own ability. SB 10.13.45**

Brahmā’s statement agrees with this. There are differences in the śakti. Very little śakti may produce either falsity or truth and brings about defeat. That is understood in this verse. That is shown by two examples: darkness and light.

It is said in the Purāṇas that many were scattered by weapons made of māyā in battle. In that case māyā does not mean something false. One cannot become wet by illusory water in the desert.

*svarūpa-bhūtayā nitya-śaktyā māyākhyayā yutaḥ \|*  
*ato māyāmayaṁ viṣṇuṁ pravadanti sanātanam \|\|*

**The Lord is endowed with his eternal śakti called māyā arising from his svarūpa. Thus the wise call eternal Viṣṇu māyā-mayam, made of māyā. Caturveda-śikhā**

However, ātma-māyā refers to the icchā-śakti. Jñāna and kriya śaktis are included. Nighantu says that māyā means knowledge. Trikāṇḍa-śeṣa says:

*triguṇātmikātha jñānaṁ ca viṣṇu-śaktis tathaiva ca \|*  
*māyā-śabdena bhaṇyate śabda-tattvārtha-vedibhiḥ \|\|*

**Those who know the meaning of words say that māyā means prakṛti made of the three guṇas, knowledge, and the śakti of Viṣṇu.**

Viśva-prakāśa says that māyā means falsity or mercy. Śrīdhara Svāmī explains māyā as illusion or knowledge in the phrase *māyā-maye jīve*. (SB 11.24.27)

*apūrvam upetya yoga-*  
*māyā-balena munayas tad atho vikuṇṭham*

**Through the strength of māyā, the Kumāras attained Vaikuṇṭha. SB 3.1.26**

*Māyā means aṣṭāṅga-yoga in relation to the Kumaṛas. In relation to the Lord it means cit-śakti.*

**Thus it has been shown that ātam-māyā is of three types (icchā, jñāna, kriya).**

*mahāmāyety avidyeti niyati mohinīti ca*  
*prakṛtir vāsenevaṁ tavecchā ‘nanta kathyate,*

**O Ananta! Your desire is called mahāmāya, avidyā, niyati, mohinī, prakṛti and vāsana.**

Though jīva-māyā is called the desire of the Lord, that is an ābhāsa only, since it is understood that the desire to create the universe is not directly the Lord’s desire. Guṇa-māyā refers to pradhāna made of the three guṇas.

*tvam ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣād īśvaraḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ \|*  
*māyāṁ vyudasya cic-chaktyā kaivalye sthita ātmani \|\|*

**You are the original Lord, the controller, beyond material energy. You are situated in your spiritual form, being one with your spiritual energy, and separate from the material energy by your spiritual energy. SB 1.7.23**

In reference to this verse quoted earlier, there is another meaning. Māyā is forbidden in Vaikuṇṭha directly by the spiritual śakti. You are situated in your own expansion *(ātmani*), Vaikuṇṭha. How do you do that? You keep māyā far away by your cit-śakti, which always exists there.

Śukadeva forbids māyā in Vaikuṇṭha:

*pravartate yatra rajas tamas tayoḥ*  
*sattvaṁ ca miśraṁ na ca kāla-vikramaḥ \|*  
*na yatra māyā kim utāpare harer*  
*anuvratā yatra surāsurārcitāḥ \|\|*

**In Vaikuṇṭha there are no rajas or tamas, and no sattva mixed with rajas and tamas. There is no influence of time. There is no influence of māyā at all, what to speak of its products such as material elements. In Vaikuṇṭha the inhabitants are fully dedicated to the Lord and are worshipable by the devas, asuras and devotees. SB 2.9.10**

According to Padma Purāṇa mokṣa, parampadam, liṅgam, amṛtam and viṣṇu-mandiram are synonyms for Vaikuṇṭha. Arjuna speaks to the Lord.

## 22.

The qualities in the spiritual world are described as svarūpa-śakti since they are non-different from the Lord’s svarūpa. That is stated in the following:

*sa yad ajayā tv ajām anuśayīta guṇāṁś ca juṣan*  
*bhajati sarūpatāṁ tad anu mṛtyum apeta-bhagaḥ*  
*tvam uta jahāsi tām ahir iva tvacam ātta-bhago*  
*mahasi mahīyase ’ṣṭa-guṇite ’parimeya-bhagaḥ*

**The jīva contacts matter by the influence of māyā, takes on similar form due to upādhis, and enjoys material objects. He thus experiences saṁsāra. You, however, avoid the material energy in the same way that a snake does not identify with its skin. Glorious in your possession of eight mystic powers, you display unlimited greatness. SB 10.87.38**

The commentary says, “Because (*yat*) the jīva embraces avidyā (*ajām)* because of māyā, he serves the guṇas and the body and senses and mistakes them for himself. After that (*anu*) he takes on similar qualities (*sarūpatām*) and, with qualities like bliss covered (*apeta-bhagaḥ*), attains saṁsāra (*mṛtyum*). You however reject that māyā. “Māyā is situated in me. How do I give it up?” Just as a snake does not think its skin as himself by thinking of its good qualities, so you give up māyā, because you do not identify it as yourself. The Lord’s profusion of continuous bliss and knowledge is not produced by māyā. He is indifferent to it. Why? You have your eternal powers (*ātta-bhagaḥ*). You are glorious in your eight mystic powers. The powers are immeasurable (*aparimeya-bhagaḥ*). Your eight qualities of power are not limited by time and place like the powers of others. They are unlimited because they are related to your complete svarūpa. This is also mentioned previously in the same chapter. *Tvam asi yad ātmanā samavaruddha-samasta-bhagaḥ*: by your very nature you are perfectly full in all opulences. (SB 10.87.14)

Another explanation of the snake is as follows. The skin refers to an old skin. The snake readily gives up that skin. Just as the snake gives up the skin and does not go near it, you do not go near māyā.

*viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā*  
*samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam*  
*sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*  
*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi*

**Let me surrender to you, Bhagavān. You are the condensed form of pure knowledge and by you are bliss itself you have forms with many qualities. Your desires are always fulfilled. Because your svarūpa-śakti you are never touched by the material guṇas. SB 10.87.22**

The Lord says to Uddhava:

*siddhayo ’ṣṭādaśa proktā dhāraṇā yoga-pāra-gaiḥ*  
*tāsām aṣṭau mat-pradhānā daśaiva guṇa-hetavaḥ*

**The masters of the yoga system have declared that there are eighteen types of siddhi and meditation, of which eight are primary, having their shelter in me, and ten are secondary, appearing from the material mode of goodness. SB 11.15.3**

*etā me siddhayaḥ saumya aṣṭāv autpattikā matāḥ*

**These eight mystic perfections of mine are considered to be natural and unexcelled. SB 11.15.5**

Prahlāda says to the demon’s sons:

*kevalānubhavānanda- svarūpaḥ parameśvaraḥ*  
*māyayāntarhitaiśvarya īyate guṇa-sargayā*

**This Supreme Lord, though having a spiritual form of pure knowledge and bliss, is falsely seen as an entity without powers, because of māyā, which creates this world with guṇas. SB 7.6.23**

The commentary says, “If the Lord were everywhere, everyone and everything should be omniscient. In answer to this, it is said that his powers are covered by māyā, which creates the world with guṇas.”

One should understand that the Lord’s powers, like his svarūpa, are beyond māyā, though the verse says his powers are covered by māyā, which causes this world composed of guṇas since before this in SB 7.6.23 it is stated that he is the supreme Lord (*parameśvaraḥ*). Śruti says:

*ajām ekāṁ lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇāṁ*  
*bahvīḥ prajāḥ sṛjamānāṁ sarūpāḥ*  
*ajo hy eko juṣamāno 'nuśete*  
*jahaty enāṁ bhukta-bhogām ajo 'nyaḥ*

**One unborn entity, satisfied, follows another unborn entity--a female entity who is red, white and black and who produces many offspring of similar nature. Another male gives up this female who has been enjoyed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5**

What is the nature of Bhagavān? The Lord has knowledge, powers and śakti.

*te dhyāna-yogānugatā apaśyan*  
*devātma-śaktiṁ sva-guṇair nigūḍhām*

**Devoted to meditation, they saw the śakti of the Lord, hidden in its guṇas. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 1.3**

*Sva-guṇaiḥ* means “by his own nature.” *yātītagocarā vācām manasām*: his nature is beyond material words and mind (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.19.77) The śrutis speak to the Lord.

Qualities of the Lord

## 23.

*māṁ bhajanti guṇāḥ sarve nirguṇaṁ nirapekṣakam*  
*suhṛdaṁ priyam ātmānaṁ sāmyāsaṅgādayo ’guṇāḥ*

**All superior qualities, which are eternal, such as equality to all beings and attachment to my devotee, reside in me. I am beyond the material guṇas, not dependent on material qualities, and am the devotees’ friend, giving love to them. SB 11.13.40**

The commentary says, ‘What are his qualities? He is without qualities which change (*aguṇaḥ*). His qualities are eternal.”

In Nārada-pañcarātra it is said:

*namaḥ sarva-guṇātīta-ṣaḍ-guṇāyādi-vedhase*

**I offer respects to the Lord filled with all good qualities beyond material guṇas.**

Brahma-tarka says:

*guṇaiḥ sva-rūpa-bhūtais tu guṇy asau harir īśvaraḥ \|*  
*na viṣṇor na ca muktānāṁ kvāpi bhinno guṇo mataḥ \|\|*

**The Lord is called the possessor of qualities because his qualities belong to his svarūpa. The qualities of Viṣṇu and liberated devotees are not different.**

In Kālikā Purāṇa, Devī praises Viṣṇu:

*yasya brahmādayo devā munayaś ca tapa-dhanāḥ \|*  
*na vivṛṇvanti rūpāṇi varṇanīyaḥ kathaṁ sa me \|\|*  
*striyā mayā te kiṁ jñeyā nirguṇasya guṇāḥ prabho \|*  
*naiva jānanti yad rūpaṁ sendrā api surāsurāḥ \|\|*

**How can I describe the forms of the Lord, since Brahmā and other devatās and austere sages cannot describe them? How can I, a woman understand the qualities of the Lord who is beyond material guṇas, since the devatās, demons and Indra cannot understand his form.**

Haṁsa speaks to Sanaka.

## 24.

Many qualities including those listed by Haṁsa are enumerated in the First Canto:

*ete cānye ca bhagavan nityā yatra mahā-guṇāḥ \|*  
*prārthyā mahattvam icchadbhir na viyanti sma karhicit \|\|*

**In you reside the following eternal, great qualities, which do not disappear at any time. SB 1.16. 29**

The commentary says, “These qualities (*ete)* number forty-one. There are other qualities (*anye*) such as serving brāhmaṇas and being worthy of taking shelter. In the Lord these qualities are natural *(nityāḥ)* and are not destroyed.”

*kalā-muhūrtādi-mayaś ca kālo*  
*na yad-vibhūteḥ pariṇāma-hetuḥ \|\|*

**The Lord’s qualities have no cause for change from time with its divisions. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 4.1.84**

The earth speaks to Dharma.

## 25.

Varuṇa says:

*namas tubhyaṁ bhagavate brahmaṇe paramātmane*  
*na yatra śrūyate māyā loka-sṛṣṭi-vikalpanā*

**My obeisances unto Bhagavān, Paramātmā and Brahman within whom there is no trace of the illusory energy, which orchestrates only the creation of this world. SB 10.28.6**

In the Lord’s svarūpa of three forms, Bhagavān, Paramātmā and Brahman, māyā does not exist. His appearance does not arise from māyā. The reason is given. Māyā has control over creating and destroying the material world only (*loka-sṛṣṭi-viklapanā*).

In asking about bhūgola (earth with its divisions), Parīkṣit says:

*bhagavato guṇamaye sthūla-rūpa āveśitaṁ mano hy aguṇe 'pi sūkṣmatama ātma-jyotiṣi pare brahmaṇi bhagavati vāsudevākhye kṣamam āveśituṁ tad u haitad guro 'rhasy anuvarṇayitum iti.*

**O guru! The mind fixed on the Lord’s gross form made of matter (bhūgola) can absorb itself in the supreme Brahman, Bhagavān, Vāsudeva, light of the soul, who is beyond matter. Therefore, you should explain the structure of the universe. SB5.16.3**

Varuṇa speaks to the Lord.

## 26.

*tasmai namo bhagavate vāsudevāya dhīmahi \|*  
*yan-māyayā durjayayā māṁ vadanti jagad-gurum \|\|*

**I meditate on the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva, by whose insurmountable māyā people call me the guru of the universe.**

*vilajjamānayā yasya sthātum īkṣā-pathe ’muyā \|*  
*vimohitā vikatthante mamāham iti durdhiyaḥ \|\|*

**The ignorant jīvas, bewildered by māyā who is ashamed to stand in sight of the Lord, boast about “I” and “mine.” SB 2.5.12-13**

Ignorant jīvas like me are bewildered by this māyā which is ashamed to be seen by the Lord, who is without fault, filled with eternity, knowledge and bliss, while māyā is filled with faults like tamas. Brahmā speaks to Nārada.

Bhagavān’s body remains perfect though creating the world

## 27.

Having spoken of the Lord’s svarūpa of six qualities (aiśvarya etc.), now a new topic begins, to explain the perfect nature of the Lord’s form. His eternal nature with its qualities has been established by showing his control over Vaikuṇṭha which has a similar nature. Though the Lord appears in the material world, he remains perfect. That is described in three verses:

*naṣṭe loke dvi-parārdhāvasāne*  
*mahā-bhūteṣv ādi-bhūtaṁ gateṣu*  
*vyakte ’vyaktaṁ kāla-vegena yāte*  
*bhavān ekaḥ śiṣyate ’śeṣa-saṁjñaḥ*

**After the two halves of Brahmā’s life, at the time of cosmic annihilation, when everything, manifested is annihilated by the force of time, and the elements merge in prakṛti, you alone remain, with your unlimited devotees who are non-different from you. SB 10.3.25**

The Lord is called the remainder (*śeṣa-saṁjñaḥ*). The reason is given:

*yo ’yaṁ kālas tasya te ’vyakta-bandho*  
*ceṣṭām āhuś ceṣṭate yena viśvam*  
*nimeṣādir vatsarānto mahīyāṁs*  
*taṁ tveśānaṁ kṣema-dhāma prapadye*

**O inaugurator of the material energy! Time which starts with second and ends with years, which rotate to make the life time of Brahmā, and by which the universe moves, is said to be your movement. I surrender to the Supreme Lord, the abode of peace. SB 10.3.26**

O instigater of prakṛti by proximity alone (*avyakta-bandho)! Ceṣtām* means the passing of seconds. Śruti says *sarve nimeṣā jajñire vidyutaḥ puruṣād adhi*: all seconds or portions of time are born and shine from the puruṣa, Paramātmā. Time is the cause of all creation and destruction. Since time is simply the movement of his limbs, there is no creation and destruction of the Lord. And another reason is given. You are the abode of peace (*kṣema-dhāma*). I surrender to you (*tvā*), O abode of peace! Since Kṛṣṇa appeared by his own desire, he had no fear of Kaṁsa for certain. This is made clear:

*martyo mṛtyu-vyāla-bhītaḥ palāyan*  
*lokān sarvān nirbhayaṁ nādhyagacchat*  
*tvat pādābjaṁ prāpya yadṛcchayādya*  
*susthaḥ śete mṛtyur asmād apaiti*

**No one in this material world, fearful of the snake of time, has become free from fear, even by fleeing to various planets. O original person! Now that you have appeared, death is fleeing in fear of you, and the living entities, having obtained shelter at your lotus feet by your mercy, are fearless and tranquil. SB 10.3.27**

One does not attain freedom from fear by fleeing to other planets. Attaining your lotus feet, one becomes free of fear. “Lotus feet” indicates the form of the Lord. Sahasra-nāma-stotra says *amṛta-vapuḥ:* the Lord has an eternal body. Śaṅkara in his commentary says *mṛtaṁ maraṇaṁ tad-rahitaṁ vapur asyety amṛta-vapuḥ*: the Lord has a body which is devoid of death (*mṛta*). *Ādya* (original person) indicates that the Lord has no birth. Even though the Lord takes birth, he remains the original person without birth.

*āvirāsīd yathā prācyāṁ diśīndur iva puṣkalaḥ*

**The Lord appeared in the dense darkness of night, like the full moon rising on the eastern horizon. SB 10.3.8**

Śruti says:

*sa brahmaṇā sṛjati sa rudreṇa vilāpayati so’nutpattir alaya eva hariḥ paraḥ paramānanda*

**Nārāyaṇa creates through Brahmā and destroys through Śiva. The Lord is without birth or destruction and is full of the highest bliss. Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

Devakī speaks to the Lord.

## 28.

*utpatti-sthiti-laya-hetavo 'sya kalpāḥ*  
*sattvādyāḥ prakṛti-guṇā yad-īkṣayāsan*  
*yad-rūpaṁ dhruvam akṛtaṁ yad ekam ātman*  
*nānādhāt katham u ha veda tasya vartma*

**He is the cause of creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe. By his glance the guṇas of matter become capable of acting. His form is eternal, since it is not created. He is one, but manifested in many forms. How can one understand his nature? SB 5.25.9**

The form of Saṅkarṣaṇa is unlimited *(dhruvam*), without beginning or end. In describing the rules of different varṣas Śiva describes the form:

*na yasya māyā-guṇa-citta-vṛttibhir*  
*nirīkṣato hy aṇv api dṛṣṭir ajyate*  
*īśe yathā no 'jita-manyu-raṁhasāṁ*  
*kas taṁ na manyeta jigīṣur ātmanaḥ*

**What person, desiring to conquer the senses, will not worship the Lord whose vision is not affected by the actions of the mind influenced by the guṇas of māyā when he glances in order to regulate matter, though we, having the force of uncontrolled anger, are affected by when we glance at matter. SB 5.17.19**

Śukadeva describes that form:

*tasya mūla-deśe triṁśad-yojana-sahasrāntara āste yā vai kalā bhagavatas tāmasī samākhyātānanta iti sātvatīyā draṣṭṛ-dṛśyayoḥ saṅkarṣaṇam aham ity abhimāna-lakṣaṇaṁ yaṁ saṅkarṣaṇam ity ācakṣate.*

**30,000 yojanas below the planet of Pātāla lives Ananta, an expansion of the Supreme Lord, called** ***tamasī*****. The devotees call him Saṅkarṣaṇa because he draws together (*****saṅkaṛṣaṇa*****) the enjoyer and the object to be enjoyed, characterized by the conception of “I.” SB 5.25.1**

bhavānī-nāthaiḥ strī-gaṇārbuda-sahasrair avarudhyamāno bhagavataś catur-mūrter mahā-puruṣasya turīyāṁ tāmasīṁ mūrtiṁ prakṛtim ātmanaḥ saṅkarṣaṇa-saṁjñām ātma-samādhi-rūpeṇa sannidhāpyaitad abhigṛṇan bhava upadhāvati.

In Ilāvṛta-varṣa, Lord Śiva is always encircled by ten billion maidservants of goddess Durgā. Uttering the following mantra in meditation, Śiva worships Saṅkarṣaṇa, his source, the spiritual form in charge of ignorance, among the four forms of the Lord. SB 5.17.16

Though Saṅkarṣaṇa is here described as a form of ignorance (*tamasī*), it should be understood that he assists the work of tamas through his expansion Śiva.

*mūrtiṁ naḥ puru-kṛpayā babhāra sattvaṁ*  
*saṁśuddhaṁ sad-asad idaṁ vibhāti tatra*  
*yal-līlāṁ mṛga-patir ādade 'navadyām*  
*ādātuṁ svajana-manāṁsy udāra-vīryaḥ*

**By his great mercy the Lord has shown his form of pure sattva, within which the universe of cause and effect exists and by which, like a lion, performing numerous heroic deeds, he enacted spotless pastimes to attract the minds of his devotees. SB 5.25.10**

Thus all the lord’s forms are eternal.

*anādi-nidhanānanta-vapuṣe viśva-rūpiṇe*

**He is the form of the universe, with a body having no beginning or end. Padma Purāṇa**

In Skanda Purāṇa and other scriptures sometimes misleading statements are seen. These Purāṇas have stories applicable to people in tamas in certain periods of time, and at those times the glories of the Lord are covered. Such statements do not indicate the truth concerning the Lord since they are glorifications of Śiva and dry renunciation. Since they do not indicate the supreme position of the Lord such statements cannot be true. Thus the devotees should not accept such scriptures which indicate Śiva as supreme. Śiva says to Karttikeya:

*śiva-śāstre ‘pi tad grāhyāṁ viṣṇu –śāstropayogi yat*

**One should accept those statements in the scriptures glorifying Śiva, which agree with the statements in the Vaiṣṇava scriptures. Skanda Purāṇa**

In the Padma Purāṇa such works are classed as tamasic Purāṇas. This does not mean that those Purāṇas are not authoritative. Paramātmā Sandarbha, taking statements from Matsya Purāṇa, shows that those Purāṇas are filled with stories concerning ages predominated by persons in tamas and rajas. Sattvika Purāṇas indicate Viṣṇu as supreme and are for sattvika people. In particular periods Viṣṇu is directly glorified.

Thus the Purāṇas are glorified. The statements seen in the Purāṇas are not false, but the highest knowledge is obtained from the sattvika Purāṇas.

*pramāda-mohau tamaso bhavato ’jñānam eva ca \|\|*

**Knowledge arises from sattva. Greed arises from rajas. Inattention, confusion and ignorance arise from tamas. BG 14.17**

Thus one finds the best knowledge in sattvika Purāṇas. This will be explained in Paramātmā Sandarbha.

*vyāmohāya carācarasya jagatas te te purāṇāgamā*

**These Purāṇas are for bewildering the living beings of the universe. Padma Purāṇa**

The Lord criticizes their contrary statements:

*evaṁ vadanti rājarṣe ṛṣayaḥ kecanānvitāḥ*  
*yat sva-vāco virudhyeta nūnaṁ te na smaranty uta*

**Such is the account given by some sages, O wise King, but those who speak in this illogical way are contradicting themselves, having forgotten their own previous statements. SB 10.77.30**

This will be established in Kṛṣṇa Sandarbha. The words of the earth in SB 1.16.27 indicate the Lord’s opinion about his qualities. It is shown there that his qualities like truth, purity, mercy, tolerance, beauty, and strength are natural and eternal in him. Devakī says:

*naṣṭe loke dvi-parārdhāvasāne*  
*mahā-bhūteṣv ādi-bhūtaṁ gateṣu*  
*vyakte ’vyaktaṁ kāla-vegena yāte*  
*bhavān ekaḥ śiṣyate ’śeṣa-saṁjñaḥ*

**After the two halves of Brahmā’s life, at the time of cosmic annihilation, when everything, manifested is annihilated by the force of time, and the elements merge in prakṛti, you alone remain, with your unlimited devotees who are non-different from you. SB 10.3.25**

Thus the statement *yad rūpaṁ dhruvam akṛtam* is true. Śukadeva speaks.

Powers of Bhagavān

## 29.

The Lord’s powers are described:

*na cāntar na bahir yasya na pūrvaṁ nāpi cāparam*  
*pūrvāparaṁ bahiś cāntar jagato yo jagac ca yaḥ*  
*taṁ matvātmajam avyaktaṁ martya-liṅgam adhokṣajam*  
*gopikolūkhale dāmnā babandha prākṛtaṁ yathā*

**Thinking of the invisible Lord--who has no inside or outside, has nothing previous or after him, who existed in the past and will exist in the future, inside and outside of the universe, who is the universe-- as her son, appearing with a human form though he was beyond material senses-- she tied him to the mortar like a normal child with rope. SB 10.9.13-14**

The commentary says, “Binding means to use a rope to encompass, so that the interior object is covered. First holding the rope in front of an object which has a prior and subsequent time, one then surrounds the object. But this could not happened with Kṛṣṇa. That is explained in the verse. Kṛṣṇa has no prior and subsequent time, no inside or outside. One must bind an object with something which can pervade that object. But this situation was the opposite. The universe (and the rope) had a prior and later time, an inside and outside whereas Kṛṣṇa did not. Moreover since he is non-different from the universe one cannot bind him: he is the universe. Thinking the Lord to be a human form and her son, she bound him.”

Since Kṛṣṇa is the universe, without the cause who is Kṛṣṇa, the effect, the universe, cannot exist. How can he bound by a rope which is a small portion of his śakti of the universe, which is non-different from him? A ray of light cannot burn fire.

Yaśodā thought of Kṛṣṇa as a human form (*martya-liṅgam*). The intention is this, according to the commentator. “How could she bind up something all-pervading? No one can bind up the universe and Goloka.” He had a human form. How can that form be all-pervading? “He is *adhokṣajam:* he is that person to whom sense knowledge *(akṣajam*) is inferior (*adhaḥ*).” This means he is beyond all sense knowledge, inconceivable to proof by material perception. Though he has a human form he is still all powerful. Some say that because he is beyond material perception, he is invisible. But that is not accepted.

“Since he was all powerful, though having a human form, why did not his mother not see this?” She thought of him as her son. She had natural prema rasa called vatsālya for him. By her bliss she covered the realization of his greatness. Her greatness was her lack of awareness of his powers. This greatness allowed her to bind him with rope. The experience of prema rasa was the cause of his bondage with the rope.

*nemaṁ viriñco na bhavo na śrīr apy aṅga-saṁśrayā*  
*prasādaṁ lebhire gopī yat tat prāpa vimuktidāt*

**Neither Brahmā, nor Śiva, nor even the goddess of fortune, who is always the better half of the Supreme Lord, can obtain from the Supreme Lord, the deliverer from this material world, such mercy as received by mother Yaśodā. SB 10.9.20**

Since it is stated that he was *like* a human child (*prākṛtam yathā*) and he was also beyond the senses *(adhokṣajam*), he was actually the Lord, and not a human form produced by māyā.

For something accessible to material logic, one may conclude by seeing something impossible that it is māyā, an illusion. However since Kṛṣṇa by his nature is beyond material conception, it is utterly foolish to say that his human form is māyā. One can also claim that the bāḍava fire in the ocean is illusion, though one usually does not see fire in water. Śruti says *arvāg devā asya visarjanenātha ko veda yata ābabhūva*: the *devatās* are younger than the Lord; who, then, can tell whence he has come into being? (Ṛg *Veda* 3.54.5)

It is stated that he who was bound by the ropes was he who had an all-pervading form. The phrases are in apposition. One is equal to the other. Yaśodā accomplished her task because of ignorance of his all-pervading nature. His form also exists with limitation since he is endowed with hands and feet and other organs. Thus Kṛṣṇa’s form is simultaneously infinite and limited. The conclusion is that Kṛṣṇa is the abode of hundreds of mutually contrary śaktis. One also sees in this world *mahauṣadhi* (having great śakti) which counteracts the three doṣas.

His powers are described in Brahma-saṁhitā:

*panthās tu koṭi śata vatsara sampragamyo*  
*vāyor athāpi manaso muni puṅgavānām \|*  
*so ’py asti yat prapada sīmny avicintya tattve*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda, whose toes alone, possessing inconceivable powers, are attained only after a billion years by multitudes of sages who discipline the mind and breath. Srahma-saṁhitā 5.34**

Śruti says:

*asthūlo’naṇur amadhyamo madhyamo’vyāpako vyāpako harir ādir anādir aviśvo viśvaḥ saguṇo nirguṇa*

**The Lord is not gross, is not small, is not average size, but is also of average size. He is all-pervading but not spreading everywhere. He is the beginning with no beginning. He is not the universe and is the universe. He is with qualities and is without qualities. Quoted by Madhva**

*turīyam aturīyam ātmānam anātmānam ugram anagram vīram av;piraṁ mahāntam amahaṇtam; viṣnum aviṣnuṁ jvalantam ajvalantaṁ sarvato mukham asarvatomukham*

**The Lord is beyond inconceivable and conceivable, is spiritual and material, is fierce and gentle, is courageous and cowardly, is great and small. He is pervading and not pervading. He is fiery and not fiery. He has his face everywhere and nowhere.** ***Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad***

*asthūlo’naṇu-rūpo’sāv aviśvo viśva eva ca \|*  
*viruddha-dharma-rūpe’sāv aiśvaryāt puruṣottama*

**O Lord, you are without substance, with a huge form. You are not the universe. You are the universe. You exist with contrary qualities because of your powers. Brahma Purāṇa**

*paramāṇv-anta-paryanta- sahasrāṁśāṇu-mūrtaye \|*  
*jaṭharāntāyutāṁśānta- sthita-brahmāṇḍa-dhāriṇe \|\|*

**I offer respects to the Lord whose form is smaller than the smallest particle and holds unlimited universes in his stomach. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*mayā tatam idaṁ sarvaṁ jagad avyakta-mūrtinā \|*  
*mat-sthāni sarva-bhūtāni na cāhaṁ teṣv avasthitaḥ \|\|*

**I pervade this whole universe by my form which is invisible to material eyes, and simultaneously all entities are within me. But I am not in them.**

*na ca mat-sthāni bhūtāni paśya me yogam aiśvaram \|*  
*bhūta-bhṛn na ca bhūta-stho mamātmā bhūta-bhāvanaḥ \|\|*

**And the living beings are not in me. See my inconceivable power. I maintain them and protect them, but I am not in them. My mind maintains and protects them. BG 9.4.-5**

*Avyakta-mūrtinā* means “by a form which by its nature is beyond all intelligence.” Śukadeva speaks.

Bhagavān’s limited form

## 30.

Another section now starts to show that the all powerful Lord has a limited form, following realization of wise men.

*kvāhaṁ tamo-mahad-ahaṁ-kha-carāgni-vār-bhū-*  
*saṁveṣṭitāṇḍa-ghaṭa-sapta-vitasti-kāyaḥ*  
*kvedṛg-vidhāvigaṇitāṇḍa-parāṇu-caryā-*  
*vātādhva-roma-vivarasya ca te mahitvam*

**What am I, a small creature measuring seven spans of my own hand, enclosed in a potlike universe composed of material nature, the total material energy, false ego, ether, air, fire, water and earth? And what is your glory, when unlimited universes pass through the pores of your body just as particles of dust pass through the openings of a screened window when you create and destroy? SB 10.14.11**

The meaning is clear.

## 31.

*utkṣepaṇaṁ garbha-gatasya pādayoḥ*  
*kiṁ kalpate mātur adhokṣajāgase*  
*kim asti-nāsti-vyapadeśa-bhūṣitaṁ*  
*tavāsti kukṣeḥ kiyad apy anantaḥ*

**O controller of the senses! Does a mother take offense when the child within her womb kicks with his legs? And is there anything in existence—whether designated by various philosophers as real or as unreal—that is actually outside your form known as Hiraṇyagarbha antaryāmī? SB 10.14.12**

Like a mother you should forgive my offense, since I am also within your womb like all other beings.

## 32.

Particularly, I have been born from you:

*jagat-trayāntodadhi-samplavode*  
*nārāyaṇasyodara-nābhi-nālāt*  
*vinirgato ’jas tv iti vāṅ na vai mṛṣā*  
*kintv īśvara tvan na vinirgato ’smi*

**My dear Lord! It is said that Brahmā came out of the lotus in the navel on Nārāyaṇa’s belly in the water of devastation of the universe. Certainly, these words are not false. Thus am I not born from you? SB 10.14.13**

Did I not come from you? The meaning is “Yes certainly, I have come from you alone.”

## 33.

“If I were Nārāyaṇa, who lies on the ocean of devastation, then you would have arisen from me. But that is not so.” In answer to this objection Brahmā says:

*nārāyaṇas tvaṁ na hi sarva-dehinām*  
*ātmāsy adhīśākhila-loka-sākṣī*  
*nārāyaṇo ’ṅgaṁ nara-bhū-jalāyanāt*  
*tac cāpi satyaṁ na tavaiva māyā*

**O Lord, of whom Mahā-viṣṇu is your expansion! Nārāyaṇa, Mahā-viṣṇu, the source of the other puruṣas is not you. Nor is the third puruṣa, dwelling within all beings, nor is the second puruṣa, the witness of all planets. Mahā-viṣṇu, called Nārāyaṇa because he is the shelter of the Kāraṇa Ocean, which gives rise to material creation, is simply your expansion. It is not true that you are Nārāyaṇa. That knowledge is caused by your māyā. SB 10.14.14**

O original lord *(adhīśa*), superior to Nārāyaṇa who is the antaryāmī of all beings! This means” O Bhagavān!” For certain (*hi)* you are not Nārāyaṇa. Nārāyaṇa is your aṁśa (*aṅgam*). Though that is so, I have ultimately arisen from you, the aṅgī, since I have arisen from the aṅga.

“Why is he called Nārāyaṇa? Why I am different from him?” Nārāyaṇa is the antaryāmī (*ātmā*) of all beings *(dehīnām*). He is the shelter (*āyana*) of all jīvas (called *nāra*). Thus he is called Nārāyaṇa. But you are indifferent to his position as antaryāmī, since you are Bhagavān. Moreover he witnesses directly all people (*akhila-loka-sākṣī*). Thus he knows (*ayate*) all jīvas *(nāra).* Thus he is Nārāyaṇa. However you are the witness through your aṁśa, Nārāyaṇa, and not directly. Thus you are different from him.

“You have said that I am not Nārāyaṇa. Do you intend to say that I am Nārāyaṇa in another way?” In answer to this Brahmā says “O original Lord, who sets in motion Nārāyaṇa!” He is called Nārāyaṇa because of his actions of sheltering the jīvas. Since you have more power than he has as *adhīśa,* you are also Nārāyaṇa. A head of a state is a king and the leader of the kings is also a king. This means that Kṛṣṇa is directly Svayam Bhagavān. This is shown in Kṛṣṇa Sandarbha.

“The wise understand that the elements arise from the Lord (*nara*). These elements are thus are called nāra. Because he takes shelter (*ayana*) of these elements (*nāra)* is called Nārāyaṇa.”

It is said:

*āpo nārā iti prokdtā āpo vai nara-sūnavaḥ \|*  
*ayanaṁ tasya tāḥ pūrvaṁ tena nārāyaṇaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*

**The word** ***āpaḥ*** **and** ***nārā*** **are synonymous for “water” since the waters are considered the sons of Nara, the primeval male or Mahā-viṣṇu.** ***Nāra*** **means the offspring of Nara. 8 Since these waters act as the bed (*****ayana)*** **of Mahā-viṣṇu from the ancient past, he is called Nārāyaṇa.** ***Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **1.4.6**

In this way he is well known as Nārāyaṇa. Because he takes shelter of the water (*jala)*, called nāra because it arose (*bhū*) from the Lord (*nara)*, he called Nārāyaṇa. Why do you wish to reject what is well known and say something else?”

This is not true that you take shelter of the water of devastation. That knowledge is your māyā. Māyā here means the deceiving śakti. Viśva-prakāśa says that māyā means cheating and mercy. You cannot be limited by water. By your svarūpa-śakti you are not limited by limited objects.

The four verses indicate that everything in this world including Brahmā is within Kṛṣṇa.

Mantra-varṇa explains Nārāyaṇa in the same way:

*yac ca kiñcij jagat sarvaṁ dṛśyate śrūyate’pi vā \|*  
*antar bahiś ca tat sarvaṁ vyāpya nārāyaṇaḥ sthitaḥ \|\|*

**Nārāyaṇa, spread everywhere, is situated inside and outside of whatever is seen and heard in the universe.**

## 34.

Brahmā uses his experience to prove that the Lord’s form is not limited by water.

*tac cej jala-sthaṁ tava saj jagad-vapuḥ*  
*kiṁ me na dṛṣṭaṁ bhagavaṁs tadaiva*  
*kiṁ vā su-dṛṣṭaṁ hṛdi me tadaiva*  
*kiṁ no sapady eva punar vyadarśi*

**My dear Lord, if your body shelters the entire universe and lies on the water, why were you not seen by me when I searched for you? And why did you appear clearly in my heart? Why did you not then appear to my external vision? SB 10.14.15**

If it is true that your body which is the shelter of the universe (*saj-jagad-vapuḥ*) is situated in water, why did I not see you after entering the lotus stem and meditating for a hundred years? O Lord endowed with inconceivable powers *(bhagavan*)! If that body was illusory, visible only for a certain time due to illusion, why did I see that form wonderfully as a form of eternity, knowledge and bliss (*suṣṭhu*) in my heart by sudden realization in perfect samādhi? And after samādhi why did I not see that form externally? It is not true that your form is made of māyā limited by a particular time. This explanation is based on the story in the Third Canto.

## 35.

It is true that Nārāyaṇa is your aṁśa. He is not illusory like the universal form. If your form in the water is also the universe, then your form should be included in the universe. The Lord himself has shown that he, of whom Nārāyaṇa is a portion, is within the universe. This was realized by Yaśodā.

*atraiva māyā-dhamanāvatāre*  
*hy asya prapañcasya bahiḥ sphuṭasya*  
*kṛtsnasya cāntar jaṭhare jananyā*  
*māyātvam eva prakaṭī-kṛtaṁ te*

**O subduer of māyā! Appearing in this incarnation, you manifested the whole universe in your abdomen to your mother, even though it was clearly visible externally as well. SB 10.41.6**

When the avatāra named Kṛṣṇa, who extinguishes māyā (*māyā-dhamana*), appeared in this world you manifested the falsity *(māyatvam*) related to the whole universe seen outside and inside your belly to your mother. You are within the universe. Your being outside the universe is a display of māyā. Actually you, though of medium form, are spread everywhere through your inconceivable svarūpa-śakti. O destroyer of māyā (*māyā-dhamana*)!

## 36.

By your mercy you showed the whole universe within your body, giving visual proof. That is a fact. The previous meaning is repeated in order to remove other ideas about the Lord, while emphasizing the truth of the incident.

*yasya kukṣāv idaṁ sarvaṁ sātmaṁ bhāti yathā tathā*  
*tat tvayy apīha tat sarvaṁ kim idaṁ māyayā vinā*

**Just as this entire universe, including you, was exhibited within your abdomen, so it is now manifested here externally within you. How could such things happen unless arranged by your māyā? SB 10.14.17**

Just as the whole universe exists with you *(sātmam*) in your belly, the universe exists outside also in you.

The meaning is this. By showing himself in Vraja and at the same time showing that Vraja was within him, he showed that he was inside and outside Vraja. His mother thought that perhaps this was a dream.

*kiṁ svapna etad uta devamāyā*  
*kiṁ vā madīyo bata buddhi-mohaḥ*  
*atho amuṣyaiva mamārbhakasya*  
*yaḥ kaścanautpattika ātma-yogaḥ*

**Was what I saw a dream, or is it an illusory creation by some devatā? Has this been manifested by bewilderment of my intelligence, or is it some natural power of my child? SB 10.8.40**

She doubted it was a dream or devatās’ māyā consisting of an original and a false form. She concluded it was some power of her son. She thought as follows. “Announcing ‘I am all-pervading even in this particular medium sized human form by the inconceivable svarūpa-śakti,’ you have shown your special quality of having simultaneously both features.” This was her conclusion.

*gṛhṇīta yad yad upabandham amuṣya mātā*  
*śulbaṁ sutasya na tu tat tad amuṣya māti \|*  
*yaj jṛmbhato ’sya vadane bhuvanāni gopī*  
*saṁvīkṣya śaṅkita-manāḥ pratibodhitāsīt \|\|*

**Yaśodā took rope to bind him up but the rope was not sufficient to tie him. She also saw in his gaping mouth the universe, and then, with a worried mind, recovered her affection for him as her son. SB 2.7.30**

Just as the universe is within your belly and is outside here also, you are within the universe and spread over the universe as well. How can this happen except by māyā, a śakti which covers the truth? It is not possible otherwise.

## 37.

I (Brahmā) also have realized this:

*adyaiva tvad ṛte ’sya kiṁ mama na te māyātvam ādarśitam*  
*eko ’si prathamaṁ tato vraja-suhṛd-vatsāḥ samastā api*  
*tāvanto ’si catur-bhujās tad akhilaiḥ sākaṁ mayopāsitās*  
*tāvanty eva jaganty abhūs tad amitaṁ brahmādvayaṁ śiṣyate*

**Today, apart from you, the calves and cowherd boys and four-armed forms you manifested, all that I saw of the material realm and the beings worshipping you was nothing but your māyā. First you were alone, then you were all the calves and boys and then you became the four-armed forms in all the universes which were worshipped by persons all in the universe including me. Then you remained as the unlimited Brahman beyond anything else. SB 10.14.18**

Except for you not being illusory, did you not show me a display of your māyā (*māyātvam)* concerning the universe? Yes, that is what you showed. To fools this world appears external to you in your human form by your māyā. This is the meaning.

“How can my form be all-pervading?” First you were alone. Then from that form you became all the calves and boys. Now after these forms disappeared, you remain in your form which is non-dual Brahman. By seeing the creation, maintenance and destruction of unlimited material and spiritual forms, you must be the supreme Brahman. The conclusion is that you are all-pervading by being Brahman.

## 38.

“There are Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, who are causes of creation maintenance and destruction, and other avatāras during the maintenance period. Why do you call me the cause of everything?” Brahmā answers:

*ajānatāṁ tvat-padavīm anātmany*  
*ātmātmanā bhāsi vitatya māyām*  
*sṛṣṭāv ivāhaṁ jagato vidhāna*  
*iva tvam eṣo ’nta iva trinetraḥ*

**For persons ignorant of your actual position, you appear in the material world, spreading māyā. For the creation of the universe you appear like me \[Brahmā\], for its maintenance you appear like Viṣṇu, and for its annihilation you appear like Śiva. SB 10.14.19**

The word *tvam* (you) in the last line is the subject of the verb *bhāsi*. You, this form of Svayam Bhagavān, different from the guṇāvatāras, appear as Brahmā for creation, as Viṣṇu (*esaḥ*) for maintaining the universe, and as Śiva for destruction. *Esaḥ* is paired with *iva*, not *tvam.* The previous verse also indicated that except for Kṛṣṇa everything else was a show of māyā.

Therefore the meaning is this. For person who do not know your svarūpa (*tvat-padavīm*), you, the aṁśī (*ātmā*), by your aṁśa (*ātmanā*), spreading your māyā, the śakti for creation and destruction, to pradhāna, the insentient form *(anātmani*), remain separate *(bhāsi*), as if becoming Śiva for destruction (ante), and becoming Brahmā for creation—The ignorant see you as different for these actions. *Eṣaḥ iva* indicates that the actions of Viṣṇu in maintenance only appear limited.

*sṛjāmi tan-niyukto ’haṁ haro harati tad-vaśaḥ \|*  
*viśvaṁ puruṣa-rūpeṇa paripāti tri-śakti-dhṛk \|\|*

**I create under his order, and Śiva destroys under his order. Holding his three energies, he protects the universe as the Paramātmā. SB 2.6.32**

Since Viṣṇu is also Bhagavān, Viṣṇu is not indicated by the word *iva* in the same way as Brahmā and Śiva. Actually are exist in these forms but fools see them as different from you.

## 39.

Just as Kṛṣṇa appears as guṇāvatāras he appears as other avatāras:

*sureṣv ṛṣiṣv īśa tathaiva nṛṣv api*  
*tiryakṣu yādaḥsv api te ’janasya*  
*janmāsatāṁ durmada-nigrahāya*  
*prabho vidhātaḥ sad-anugrahāya ca*

**O Lord! O supreme creator! You have no material birth, yet to defeat the false pride of the faithless demons and show mercy to your devotees, you take birth among the devatās, sages, human beings, animals and even the aquatics. SB 10.14.20**

You have no birth. This means that what is called his birth is his manifesting himself only.

“O Brahmā, you think that since I am a single all-pervading form, it is impossible for me to be the location that displays many forms. How can my spiritual forms having a different nature be like appearance of material objects such as pots seen in the material world? You have been astonished by whatever powerful forms I have shown you. You seem to think that I cannot have other forms, that I am limited. Thus when my expansions as the boys and calves, and later the four armed forms appeared, you thought that such forms could never appear.”

## 40.

“All these forms can exist simultaneously because I have an eternal form of unlimited bliss. But you do not see all these forms at all times. How can you see all the forms at once?” Brahmā replies:

*ko vetti bhūman bhagavan parātman*  
*yogeśvarotīr bhavatas tri-lokyām*  
*kva vā kathaṁ vā kati vā kadeti*  
*vistārayan krīḍasi yoga-māyām*

**O unrestricted Lord! O Lord endowed with all powers! O Supersoul! O Lord spread throughout all time! Who knows where, how, how many, and when your pastimes will take place in the universe? Spreading yoga-māyā, you play. SB 10.14.21**

Using your inconceivable cit-śakti (*yoga-māyām*) you play. Who knows where, how, how many and when these pastimes *(ūtī*) will appear in the three worlds? No one knows.

*yasyāmataṁ tasya mataṁ mataṁ yasya na veda sa*

**The Lord is known to he who does not think the Lord is limited; he who thinks the Lord is limited does not know him. Kena Upaniṣad 2.3**

By addressing the Lord with four names (*bhūman, bhagavan, parātman, and yogeśvara*) related to his inconceivability, he gives four reasons for his powers.

O form which includes within it unlimited other forms (*bhūman*)! The meaning is this. The one chief form of Bhagavān simultaneously is unlimitedly other forms. Thus Akrūra said *bahu-mūrty-eka-mūrtikam*: your one form has many forms (SB 10.40.7) Śruti *says ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛśyamānam*: the one form is seen as many.

When worship matures to its fruits, at that time a person sees the Lord’s forms according to the type of worship.

*prajñāntara-pṛthaktvavad dṛṣṭiś ca tad uktim*

**Just as there is difference in knowledge, there difference in the worshipper and the result. That was stated in the scripture quoted. Brahma-sūtra 3.3.52**

Madhva in his commentary says *upāsanābhedād darśana-bhedah*: according to the type of worship the forms seen will differ. A cloth the color of a peacock feather has one main color with many subsidiary colors. Sometimes because of a particular point of view, a person will see a particular color. Kṛṣṇa is like one cloth with many colors. He shows many forms within that main form. The other forms are like the various subsidiary colors.

*maṇir yathā vibhāgena nīla-pītādibhir yutaḥ \|*  
*rūpa-bhedam avāpnoti dhyāna-bhedāt tathā vibhuḥ \|\|*

**Just as a gem shows blue, yellow and other colors at different times, the Lord takes different forms because of different types of meditation.** ***Nārada-pañcarātra***

This gem is the vaidūrya which shows various colors like blue and yellow. The word *bhūman* answers the question “Where (*kvā*)?”

Śukadeva says concnering Vāmana:

*yat tad vapur bhāti vibhūṣaṇāyudhair*  
*avyakta-cid-vyaktam adhārayad dhariḥ*  
*babhūva tenaiva sa vāmano vaṭuḥ*  
*sampaśyator divya-gatir yathā naṭaḥ*

**The Lord, whose body is eternally endowed with ornaments and weapons and is invisible to the world and who has a spiritual form, became visible. Then, in the presence of his parents, to please them, the Lord, like an actor whose actions are hard to understand, became Vāmana, a brāhmaṇa-dwarf, a brahmacārī. SB 8.18.12**

That body, full of bliss and knowledge *(cit*), which does not manifest by any means *(avyaktam*), which was endowed with ornaments and weapons, was established (*adhārayat*) in this material world visibly *(vyaktam*). With that body only (*eva*) he became Vāmana brahmacārī. The word *eva* indicates the original body did not change in any way. He appeared while his parents watched. The reason for his appearance in that body is given. He has actions which are inconceivable *(divya-gatiḥ).* He appears and disappears in various places in various conditions by himself. He is like an actor in that he takes joy that his real identity remains unknown. An actor uses his hand movements (*gatiḥ)* to create great astonishment (*divya*). In that form, without accepting false disguises, he shows various forms. *Divya-gatiḥ* can also mean an actor from Svarga. Such an actor can exactly imitate another form. Since no one except the Lord can manifest other forms perfectly, the fault of imperfection in actors in the example cannot be applied. The Lord in creating the universe by himself is similarly compared to a spider which transforms the insects it eats into thread.

*yathorṇanābhir hṛdayād ūrṇāṁ santatya vaktrataḥ*  
*tayā vihṛtya bhūyas tāṁ grasaty evaṁ maheśvaraḥ*

**Just as from within himself the spider expands thread through his mouth, plays with it for some time and eventually swallows it, similarly, the Supreme Lord produces the universe and then withdraws it. SB 11.9.21**

Brahmā explains how Kṛṣṇa has all forms:

*tvaṁ bhakti-yoga-paribhāvita-hṛt-saroja*  
*āsse śrutekṣita-patho nanu nātha puṁsām*  
*yad-yad-dhiyā ta urugāya vibhāvayanti*  
*tat-tad-vapuḥ praṇayase sad-anugrahāya*

**O Lord! You, who are approached by being heard about, seen and directly served, enter and remain in the lotus of your devotee’s hearts infused with bhakti-yoga. Much praised Lord! By your mercy, you manifest bodies appropriate to the mood they cultivate during sādhana.**

You manifest perfectly *(praṇayase*) various bodies. *Śrutekṣita-patha* (approached by hearing from scriptures) eliminates worshipping concocted forms. Kardama shows that among all the forms those which are not preferred by the devotee are not revealed.

*tāny eva te 'bhirūpāṇi rūpāṇi bhagavaṁs tava*  
*yāni yāni ca rocante sva-janānām arūpiṇaḥ*

**O Lord! These are suitable forms of you who have no material form, and which are pleasing to the devotees. SB 3.24.31**

Those forms of yours which are pleasing to the devotees are suitable forms and not others. “Other forms” refers to forms like the ugly form that the Lord manifested to Rantideva. Such forms are made of māyā:

*tasya tribhuvanādhīśāḥ phaladāḥ phalam icchatām*  
*ātmānaṁ darśayāṁ cakrur māyā viṣṇu-vinirmitāḥ*

**Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva, who can satisfy all materially ambitious men by giving them the rewards they desire, and who had first disguised themselves as a brāhmaṇa, śūdra and dog keeper, then manifested their real identities before King Rantideva. SB 9.21.15**

The commentary says, “The three lords, Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, appearing as brāhmaṇa, śūdra and dog keeper by māyā (*māyāḥ*) showed their real forms.” Why was the form shown to Rantideva not suitable? It was material. The Lord’s real form is not material *(arūpiṇaḥ*). The commentary says “Because his form is not material, it was impossible that it could be ugly.”

Now the three remaining names will be explained in terms of *katham* (how), *kati* (how many), and *kadā* (when). O Lord full of inconceivable powers *(bhagavan*)! Your inconceivable svarūpa-śakti is the only cause of your manifesting yourself in many forms. This is the answer to how the Lord manifests *(katham*). Of Paramātmā (*parātma*)! You are the source (*ātmā*) of the puruṣa and other avatāras *(para*), each having unlimited powers. The forms that manifest are beyond words and mind since they manifest unlimitedly in you. This answers the question of how many (*kati*).

O controller through your svarūpa-śakti *(yogeśvara*)! You are the controller through your svarūpa-śakti (*yoga)* which is able to manifest many forms in your one form. The meaning is this. Since you have a chief form which includes unlimited forms within it, those partial forms can manifest by your will. When there is a desire to manifest a particular partial form as a result of a particular type of worship of that form, you reveal that form. This answers the question of when (*kadā*).

## 41.

Therefore all these forms are included in the form of Kṛṣṇa. This conclusion is stated in the following:

*tasmād idaṁ jagad aśeṣam asat-svarūpaṁ*  
*svapnābham asta-dhiṣaṇaṁ puru-duḥkha-duḥkham*  
*tvayy eva nitya-sukha-bodha-tanāv anante*  
*māyāta udyad api yat sad ivāvabhāti*

**Therefore this entire universe, which like a dream is by nature unreal, nevertheless appears real, and thus it covers one’s consciousness and assails one with repeated miseries. This universe appears real because it is manifested by the potency of illusion emanating from you, whose unlimited form is full of eternal happiness and knowledge. SB 10.14.22**

Since you are the real form of all things in the material and spiritual world, therefore *(tasmāt*) this limited universe appears in you, who have an unlimited (*anante*) body *(tanau*) which gives eternal bliss (*nitya-sukha-bodha*). This universe perpetually appears and disappears (*sat udyat*). This appearance and disappearance takes place within the Lord (*yat* for *yasmin*), like a pot appearing and disappearing in earth element.

Does the Lord has transformation? No. There is no transformation of the Lord because of his inconceivable śakti (*māyātaḥ*).

*śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvād*

**The defects of the agent do not apply to Brahman because of scriptural statements, which are the only proof concerning inconceivable subjects. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.27**

Transformation is accepted (through transformation of śakti). Because of continual appearance and disappearance, the world is like a dream (*svapnābham*). It is not just imagined out of ignorance.

*vaidharmyāc ca na svapnādivat*

**One cannot claim that objects are only cognition as in dreams, since there is difference in the dream object and object of waking perception. Brahma-sūtra 2.2.29**

The world destroys jīva’s knowledge (*asta-dhiṣaṇam*) of Paramātmā and jīva because of the actions of māyā through avidyā. Because of these two factors (appearing and disappearing like a dream and ignorance of jīva and Paramātmā) the world is full of suffering, since even happiness is only an ābhāsa, another form of suffering. Without your existence, the universe is an unreal thing (*asat-svarūpam*) like the horns of a rabbit. Still, the temporary world appears to be eternal for fools. The happiness is a semblance. There appears to be knowledge, since prakṛti is constituted of mahat-tattva whose nature is knowledge, though that is material, and there appears to be happiness because the world has places like Svarga. Because it is seen that your body is limited by other elements, Though all-pervading, greater in size that all universes, the Lord appears within the material world in a medium sized form. 9 your body though unlimited, becomes limited by the svarūpa-śakti. That is the meaning of this section.

Brahmā speaks to the Lord.

## 42.

Thus the all-powerful nature of the Lord is established, since he contains everything in his medium-sized form as Kṛṣṇa. And because he shows that he is all-pervading this power is established:

*citraṁ bataitad ekena vapuṣā yugapat pṛthak*  
*gṛheṣu dvy-aṣṭa-sāhasraṁ striya eka udāvahat*

**It is quite amazing that in a single body Lord Kṛṣṇa simultaneously married sixteen thousand women, each in a separate palace. SB 10.69.2**

Oh! How amazing (*etad bata citram)!* Kṛṣṇa alone married sixteen thousand women. “How is this amazing?” He married them in all their sixteen thousand palaces. “And then?” He was present in each palace and performed the marriage rites. “It is not impossible to be present in each house one after the other.” He was simultaneously present in each house. “A great yogī can manifest many bodies at once also and accomplish this feat. What is so astonishing? Great yogīs that your respect do this.” He did this with a single body. Did he spread out that one body with many hands and feet? No.

*āsāṁ muhūrta ekasmin nānāgāreṣu yoṣitām*  
*sa-vidhaṁ jagṛhe pāṇīn anurūpaḥ sva-māyayā*

**According to proper method, in each of the palaces, with suitable form, he accepted each bride in marriage at the same time by his power of yoga-māyā. SB 3.3.8**

According to Uddhava’s words, each form was exactly like the other forms. The idea of one form extending itself is rejected by use of the word *pṛthak* (separate) in the verse spoken by Nārada. Kṛṣṇa was visible with one body of a human in each of the palaces separately and completely. Thus since one body spread simultaneously in all places with all types of activities it was most astonishing. That is the explanation of the verse. In the prose section of Fifth Canto describing the form of the Lord as ruler of the planet (SB 5.20.40) Śrīdhara Svāmī gives a similar explanation. “The Lord remains in a complete form (*samantāt*), with his one form, since he is master of the greatest powers (*mahā-vibhūti-patiḥ*).”

*atho muhūrta ekasmin nānāgāreṣu tāḥ striyaḥ*  
*yathopayeme bhagavān tāvad-rūpa-dharo ’vyayaḥ*

**Then the imperishable Lord, assuming a separate form for each bride, duly married all the princesses simultaneously, each in her own palace. SB 10.59.42**

Simultaneously appearing in each place (*tāvad-rūpa-dharaḥ*) he married all the princesses. He did not have separate forms like Nārāyaṇa. It is said:

*anekatra prakaṭatā rūpasyaikasya yaikadā \|*  
*sarvathā tat-svarūpaiva sa prakāśa itīryate \|\|*

**When one form manifests in many places and is, in all respects, the same as the original form it is called** ***prakāśa.***  **Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta 1.21**

This is different from appearance of different forms in other places. Nārada speaks.

## 43.

Thus it is said:

*ity ācarantaṁ sad-dharmān pāvanān gṛha-medhinām*  
*tam eva sarva-geheṣu santam ekaṁ dadarśa ha*

**Thus in every palace Nārada saw the Lord in his same personal form, executing the proper principles of religion that purify those engaged in household affairs. SB 10.69.41**

He himself *(eva*), not his expansion, was in all the houses. He was one form, not many forms through expansion of one body. Śruti says *ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛśyamānam*: one form appears as many. *na cāntar na bahir yasya*: he has no interior or exterior. (SB 10.9.11) Nārada saw clearly since Kṛṣṇa has all powers to pervade everywhere. Nārada did not just infer this but experienced it directly by power given by the Lord.

## 44.

Therefore it is said:

*kṛṣṇasyānanta-vīryasya yoga-māyā-mahodayam*  
*muhur dṛṣṭvā ṛṣir abhūd vismito jāta-kautukaḥ*

**Having repeatedly seen the vast mystic display of Kṛṣṇa, whose power is unlimited, the sage was amazed and filled with wonder. SB 10.69.42**

Yogamāyā mentioned in the verse is the cit-śakti which can do the impossible. Commenting on SB 3.16.37 Śrīdhara Svāmī says, “Yogamāyā used in relation to the Lord means his cit-śakti.” Seeing constantly this śakti, the sage, amazed, became wonder struck. A yogī can expand his body into many. Without doing this, Kṛṣṇa in a medium sized human form was everywhere. That was the cause of his being wonder struck. His form can also be explained in terms of *sarvataḥ pāṇi-pādaṁ:* he has his hands and feet everywhere. (BG 13.14) Philosophers also point out the following:

*na sthānato’pi parasyobhaya-liṅgaṁ sarvatra hi*

**They are not different forms though they are in different places, because the Lord can be everywhere by his inconceivable power. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.11**

There are not separate forms of the Lord *(parasya*) in different places *(sthānataḥ*) because *(hi*) he has his forms everywhere, in all beings (*sarvatra*).

*evam eva brahma ity ācakṣate*

**Thus they say that he is Brahman (expanding everywhere). sa brahma tyad ity ācakṣate ( Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.9)**

10

*eka eva paro viṣṇuḥ sarvatrāpi na saṁśayaḥ \|*  
*aiśvaryād rūpam ekaṁ ca sūryavad badhudheyate \|\|*

**The one supreme Viṣṇu is everywhere without doubt. By his power, the one form becomes many like the sun. Matsya Purāṇa**

*tam imam aham ajaṁ śarīra-bhājāṁ*  
*hṛdi hṛdi dhiṣṭhitam ātma-kalpitānām \|*  
*pratidṛśam iva naikadhārkam ekaṁ*  
*samadhigato ’smi vidhūta-bheda-mohaḥ \|\|*

**Freed of the illusion of difference in the Lord’s various forms, I have attained the Lord who is one though appearing to be many like the sun seen by many people, who is the charioteer, but who is also in my heart, the unborn and is situated in the hearts of all the jīvas, who create their own bodies. SB 1.9.42**

*na bhedād iti cen na pratyekam atad-vacanād*

**If you say the explanation is not logical because the forms are actually different, it is not so, because śruti states that all the forms are not different from the Lord. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.12**

*api caivam eke*

**Moreover, one group of Vedic scholars teaches this. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.13**

Śukadeva speaks.

## 45.

*tam imam aham ajaṁ śarīra-bhājāṁ*  
*hṛdi hṛdi dhiṣṭhitam ātma-kalpitānām \|*  
*pratidṛśam iva naikadhārkam ekaṁ*  
*samadhigato ’smi vidhūta-bheda-mohaḥ \|\|*

**Freed of the illusion of difference in the Lord’s various forms, I have attained the Lord who is one though appearing to be many like the sun seen by many people, who is the charioteer, but who is also in my heart, the unborn and is situated in the hearts of all the jīvas, who create their own bodies. SB 1.9.42**

I have attained Kṛṣṇa who is standing before me, who is situated in the hearts of all embodied jīvas in his expansion as the localized antaryāmī.

*kecit sva-dehāntar-hṛdayāvakāśe*  
*prādeśa-mātraṁ puruṣaṁ vasantam \|*  
*catur-bhujaṁ kañja-rathāṅga-śaṅkha-*  
*gadā-dharaṁ dhāraṇayā smaranti \|\|*

**Some yogīs meditate upon the Paramātmā measuring one** ***pradeśa,*** **who is residing in the heart within the body, and who holds the lotus, wheel, conch and club in his four hands. SB 2.2.8**

I have attained that one unified form, who also appears in distinct forms in each jīva as mentioned above. That form of the highest bliss spreads himself out. I understand that he appears in these places as the antaryāmī, his special form with all beings because I am purified of the illusion of seeing difference in him. By the Lord’s mercy this illusion has been removed. I was in illusion, thinking that he was various forms because it seemed impossible that he could spread his form everywhere. There is a reason for his spreading himself in all beings: the beings are manifested in the supreme shelter (*ātmani*), the basis.

An example is given. To many people situated in different places the one sun without obstacles appears in its complete form by rising above the trees and bushes, with obstacles, and appears be many in complete forms.

The example only illustrates that one form appears in various ways from various points of view. The Lord’s form actually shines by his acintya-śakti. The sun however appears variously because its expansive form is situated far away.

There is another meaning to the verse. The previously described form which is sitting in front of me, even appearing in the hearts of the embodied jīvas, has come near to me. Though he has the form different from antaryāmī, I see that form of Kṛṣṇa now in all beings since no other form can manifest in front of this powerful form of Kṛṣṇa. The example is used to show that there is no difference in the form even if it appears in different places, and is not used to show complete and incomplete forms of the Lord.

Bhīṣma, with eyes wide open (SB 1.9.30), concentrated on Kṛṣṇa with his mind words and eyes. (SB 1.9.43) Thus in the opening and closing sections of this story, the personal form of Kṛṣṇa is praised. Verse 39 should thus not be explained as impersonal Brahman spreading everywhere since simultaneously the personal form can be situated as limited (in individual bodies) and unlimited (beyond all bodies).

*bahir antaś ca bhūtānām acaraṁ caram eva ca \|*  
*sūkṣmatvāt tad avijñeyaṁ dūra-sthaṁ cāntike ca tat \|\|*

**He is outside and inside all things. He moves and does not move. He is difficult to understand because of His subtle nature. He is not perceivable, but He can be perceived. BG 13.16**

Though the Lord is all-pervading, the reality of his pastimes is established. Bhīṣma speaks to the Lord.

## 46.

In this way the Lord’s personal form has been established as eternal and all-pervading.

Śrīdhara Svāmī explains, “Saying that the Lord who does not manifest has become manifest, Brahmā thought of the Lord as eternal and all-pervading.”

*ajāta-janma-sthiti-saṁyamāyā-*  
*guṇāya nirvāṇa-sukhārṇavāya*  
*aṇor aṇimne 'parigaṇya-dhāmne*  
*mahānubhāvāya namo namas te*

**I offer repeated respects to the Lord who has no destruction of his birth and actions, who is without material qualities but full of auspicious qualities as Bhagavān, whose happiness is greater than that of liberation, who is Paramātmā, smaller than an atom, and who has an immeasurable form. SB 8.6.8**

*rūpaṁ tavaitat puruṣarṣabhejyaṁ*  
*śreyo 'rthibhir vaidika-tāntrikeṇa*  
*yogena dhātaḥ saha nas tri-lokān*  
*paśyāmy amuṣminn u ha viśva-mūrtau*

**O best of persons, O supreme director! Your form is always worthy of worship for those who desire the highest benefit through the Vedic and Pañcarātrika methods. I clearly see the three worlds in you, the form of the universe. SB 8.6.9**

He explains these verses as follows. “The Lord’s form only appears. His birth is not like our birth. He does not have a birth, maintenance and destruction. Why? He has no material guṇas (*aguṇāya*). He has a form of incomparable happiness experienced in liberation. He is smaller than the smallest (*anor aṇimne*) since he cannot be understood. His form cannot be measured. All this is not impossible because he is inconceivable to our minds (*mahānubhāvāya*). He concludes that the Lord’s form is eternal and immeasurable. O best of the puruṣas! O maintainer! This form of yours is to be worshipped always by the best methods, Vedic and tantric. This form is thus not new. ‘You devatās are famous as being worthy of worship.’ That is true. All of them are included in worship of the Lord. Oh (*u*)! Clearly (*ha*) I see us along the three worlds in you (*amuṣmin*) since you are the form of the universe. Thus your form is not limited.”

Nirvāṇa-sukhārṇavāya actually means that the Lord is an ocean of happiness compared to the happiness of mere liberation. Dhruva says:

*yā nirvṛtis tanu-bhṛtāṁ tava pāda-padma-*  
*dhyānād bhavaj-jana-kathā-śravaṇena vā syāt*  
*sā brahmaṇi sva-mahimany api nātha mā bhūt*  
*kiṁ tv antakāsi-lulitāt patatāṁ vimānāt*

**The bliss for your servant available from meditating on your lotus feet or from hearing about your pastimes from the devotees is not available in your form of greatness known as Brahman, what to speak of the happiness for those who fall from the pleasures of Svarga. SB 4.9.10**

By mentioned that the Lord is smaller than the smallest and also is immeasurable it is shown that he includes all dimensions by his acintya-śakti (*mahānubhāvāya*).

## 47.

Two verses illustrate the Lord is beyond gross and subtle.

*sa vai na devāsura-martya-tiryaṅ*  
*na strī na ṣaṇḍho na pumān na jantuḥ*  
*nāyaṁ guṇaḥ karma na san na cāsan*  
*niṣedha-śeṣo jayatād aśeṣaḥ*

**The Lord is not a devatā, demon, human, animal, female, neuter, male or any other living being. He is not the guṇas or karma. He is not gross or subtle. He remains after everything is negated. May the unlimited Lord remain glorious! SB 8.3.24**

*evaṁ gajendram upavarṇita-nirviśeṣaṁ*  
*brahmādayo vividha-liṅga-bhidābhimānāḥ*  
*naite yadopasasṛpur nikhilātmakatvāt*  
*tatrākhilāmara-mayo harir āvirāsīt*

**Thus, when the devatās who identify themselves by characteristic marks, did not come to rescue Gajendra who described the Lord beyond material perception, the Lord himself, the personification of all devatās, appeared, since he is the ātmā of all beings. SB 8.3.39**

SB 8.3.22 indicates that since the Lord is the cause of all causes, he cannot be included among the devatās. He is devoid of sattvika nature and material body. He is devoid of femaleness or maleness. He is devoid of all material qualities. Thus he is not neuter either. He is not any material creature. He is not the guṇas, the cause of all material forms and he is not karma composed of piety and sin, since he is the instigator of the guṇas and karma. What more can be said? He is not gross (*sat)* or subtle *(asat*) material since his form is self-manifesting (beyond all material manifestations). He remains when everything is negated (*niṣedha-śeṣaḥ*).
