But by māyā he becomes unlimited forms (*aśeṣaḥ*). May he manifest (*jayatāt*) in order to free me.

When the devatās such as Brahmā, who identified themselves with various forms (*vividha-liṅa-bhidābhimānāḥ*) did not come to Gajendra who had described the Lord devoid of a devatā form (*nirviśeṣam*), since he is the form happiness as Paramātmā for all of them (*akhilātmakavāt*), and more than that, by his māyā is happiness for unlimited beings *(nikhilānām*), the Lord who is all the devatās appeared.

The form that appears should correspond to the form that Gajendra described in his prayers. He had prayed for the appearance of a form beyond gross and subtle material forms. Otherwise the Lord would have appeared in his heart to accomplish his rescue, without showing a form with hands and feet. This happens by the mercy of the Lord:

*asyāpi deva vapuṣo mad-anugrahasya*  
*svecchā-mayasya tanu-bhūta-mayasya ko ’pi*  
*neśe mahi tv avasituṁ manasāntareṇa*  
*sākṣāt tavaiva kim utātma-sukhānubhūteḥ*

**Even by controlling the mind, no one can understand the greatness of this form of the Lord of the universe, which manifested in many four-armed forms, but which is actually you, the one form of Svayam Bhagavān, who gave me mercy by you own will, who spread through the universe in many spiritual forms and possess bliss which can be understood only by yourself. SB 10.14.2**

The two verses, though separated by other verses, are explained together since their meanings are continuous with each other. The first verse is spoken by Gajendra and the second is spoken by Śukadeva.

Bhagavān becomes visible by his will

## 48.

Manifestation of the Lord’s form directly to the devotee is described:

*sa tvaṁ kathaṁ mama vibho ’kṣi-pathaḥ parātmā*  
*yogeśvaraiḥ śruti-dṛśāmala-hṛd-vibhāvyaḥ*  
*sākṣād adhokṣaja uru-vyasanāndha-buddheḥ*  
*syān me ’nudṛśya iha yasya bhavāpavargaḥ*

**O almighty one, how is it that my eyes see you here before me? You are the Supreme Soul, whom the greatest devotees can fixed within their pure hearts only by employing the spiritual eye of the Vedas. Then how, O transcendental Lord, are you directly visible to me, since my intelligence has been blinded by the severe tribulations of material life? Only one who has finished his material entanglement in this world should be able to see you. SB 10.64.26**

The commentary says, “O Lord (*vibho*)! It is astonishing that you are before my eyes. He explains why it is astonishing. You are the supreme soul, whom the best of yogīs contemplate in their hearts according to Vedic injunctions. Since sense knowledge is inferior to you (*adhokṣajaḥ*), you should be seen by those who are free of the material world (*bhavāpavargaḥ*). It is thus astonishing that I, whose intelligence is blinded by suffering birth as a lizard *(uru-vyasana*), can see you.”

The cause of seeing the Lord is explained in Nārāyaṇādhyātma:

*nityāvyakto’pi bhagavān īkṣyate nija-śaktitaḥ \|*  
*tām ṛte paramātmānaṁ kaḥ paśyetāmṛtaṁ prabhum \|*

**The Lord, eternally invisible, is seen by his will. Without that, who can see the Lord, the Paramātmā?**

His mercy alone is the cause, with the enthusiasm of his śakti of mercy:

*yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyas tasyaiṣa ātmā vivṛṇute tanuṁ svām \|\|*

**The Lord reveals his form to whomsoever he chooses. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 3.2.4**

*na sandṛśe tiṣṭhati rūpam asya na cakṣuṣā paśyati rūpam asya*

**His form is not within the perception of material senses. One cannot see his form with the material eye.** ***Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad***

In Mokṣa-dharma the master of Śvetadvīpa speaks to Nārada:

*etat tvayā na vijñeyaṁ rūpavān iti dṛśyate \|*  
*icchan muhūrtān naśyeyam īśo’haṁ jagato guruḥ \|\|*  
*māyā hy eṣā mayā sṛṣṭā yan māṁ paśyasi nārada \|*  
*sarvabhūtaguṇair yuktaṁ naivaṁ tvaṁ jñātum arhasi \|\|*

**You should not think that I become visible because I accept a form. Being master and guru of the universe, by my desire I can become invisible. Nārada, that form which you see made of material elements is actually an appearance created by my māyā. You should not think that I am made of material qualities and elements*****. Mahābhārata*** **12.326.42-43**

You should not think that because I take a form I become visible. Saying that even being invisible he has a form, the lord shows that his form is spiritual, not material. The cause of showing his form is his desire, his great mercy. By my will, I become invisible (*naśyeyam*). The cause of this is his independence, being completely different from the universe. I am the Lord *(īśaḥ)* and guru of the universe. The form you think is made of material elements is an appearance made by my māyā. But you should not think that. Māyā here refers to the deceiving śakti. That is one of the meanings given in Viśva-prakāśa.

Bhīṣma says:

*prītas tato’sya bhagavān deva-devaḥ sanātanaḥ \|*  
*sākṣāt taṁ darśayāmāsa dṛśyo nānyena kenacid \|\|*

**Pleased with him, the eternal Lord, lord of the devatās, showed himself directly but no one else could see this form. Mahā-bhārata 12.323.11**

The Lord showed his form to Uparicara-vasu. Later Uparicara-vasu says:

*na śakyaḥ sa tvayā draṣṭum asmābhir vā bṛhaspate \|*  
*yasya prasādaṁ kurute sa vai taṁ draṣṭum arhati \|\|*

**O Bṛhaspati! It is not possible for you or me to see him. He can be seen by that person to whom he gives mercy. Mahā-bhārata 12.323.18**

In śruti also there are statements saying that one cannot see the qualities of the Lord with form:

*na cakṣuṣā paśyati rūpam asya*

**One cannot see his form with the material eye. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

Nṛga speaks to the Lord.

## 49.

Material forms are rejected in the Lord. Other types of forms are described.

*na vidyate yasya ca janma karma vā*  
*na nāma-rūpe guṇa-doṣa eva vā*  
*tathāpi lokāpyaya-sambhavāya yaḥ*  
*sva-māyayā tāny anukālam ṛcchati*

**He who has no birth or activities, no name or form, and no qualities or faults, eternally accepts birth and activities eternally by his svarūpa-śakti for creation and destruction. SB 8.3.8**

The meaning is this. Changing state is called transformation. The first transformation is birth (*janma).* The attempts to produce perfection in an imperfect being are called action (*karma).* The sound which somehow indicates an object so that it can be understood by the mind for ordinary transactions is called a name *(nāma*). The quality grasped by the eye is its form (*rūpa*). *Guṇa* is a particular quality causing excellence of an object containing mixture of sattva, rajas and tamas. These qualities are seen in the material world. These do not exist in him since he is always situated in his svarūpa, is perfect, beyond the mind, self manifesting and beyond prakṛti. However the Lord accepts birth, activities, name and form. The statement continues in the next verse.

*tasmai namaḥ pareśāya brahmaṇe 'nanta-śaktaye*  
*arūpāyoru-rūpāya nama āścarya-karmaṇe*

**I offer respects to Paramātmā, Brahman, with unlimited powers, who is devoid of material forms but who has many spiritual forms and astonishing activities. SB 8.3.9**

*niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyaṁ śāntam*

**The Lord is without decay, without material activities, and undisturbed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.19**

*aśabdam asparśam arūpam avyayam*

**The Lord is without sound, without touch, without form and indestructible. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.3.15**

*sarva-karmā sarva-kāmaḥ sarva-gandhaḥ sarva-rasa*

**The Lord has all activities, all desires, all fragrance and all rasa. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 3.14.4**

All qualities are actually faults (*doṣa)* since it is all unspiritual. The Lord has no fault recognized in the world.

*aiśvarya-yogād bhagavān viruddhārtho’bhidhīyate \|*  
*tathāpi doṣāḥ parame naivāhāryaḥ kathañcana \|*  
*guṇā viruddhā api tu samāhāryāś ca sarvataḥ \|\|*

**Because of his endowment of powers, Bhagavān is said to have contrary characteristics. Still, the fault of birth and transformation can never be found in the Supreme Lord. But contrary qualities are found together in the Lord. Kūrma Purāṇa**

*ayam ātmāpahata-pāpmā*

**The Lord is devoid of all sin. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.7.1**

*etaṁ saṁyad vāma ity āvakṣate etaṁ sarvāṇi vāmāni nayati eṣa u eva bhāmaṇīḥ eṣa sarveṣu vedeṣu bhāti*

**He is called “full of good qualities.” All qualities come to him. This attractive form is revealed in all the Vedas. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 4.15.2**

The phrase *sarva-gandha* means he s most fragrant. When the Bhāgavatam verse says that the Lord attains birth, activities and qualities, it does not mean that he attains the faults in those qualities, since that is contrary to śruti and the Lord is being established as the highest spiritual entity.

“It is contradictory to say that the Lord has and does not have birth, activities, etc.” The cause of having contrary natures is explained. He attains this by his māyā. This means his inconceivable svarūpa-śakti which accomplishes what cannot be accomplished. One can say that birth, activities do not exist in him since his birth and activities are different from those of prakṛti. They arise from his svarūpa. Commenting on Brahma-sūtra 1.4.15, Saṅkara says, “*Sat* is generally used to describe objects transformed by name and form. Brahma which is *sat* is also called *asat* since before creation name and form did not exist.”

Viṣṇu Purāṇa says *guṇāṁś ca doṣāṁś ca mune vyatīta*: O sage, the Lord is devoid of good qualities and bad qualities. But later it says *samasta-kalyāṇa-guṇātmako hi*: the Lord is endowed with all auspicious qualities.

*jñāna-śakti-balaiśvarya-vīrya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ \|*  
*bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ \|\|*

**The word** ***bhagavān*** **means to be endowed with unlimited knowledge, sense power, bodily strength, power of control, influence and beauty without inferior** ***guṇas.*** ***Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **6.5.79**

*yo’sau nirguṇa ity uktaḥ śāstreṣu jagad-īśvaraḥ \|*  
*prākṛtair heya-saṁyuktair guṇair hīnatvam ucyate \|\|*

**It is said in all scriptures that the Lord of the universe is without qualities. He is devoid of inferior qualities. The word “inferior” means material qualities.** ***Padma Purāṇa***

Thus *sva-māyayā* cannot mean anything except the svarūpa-śakti.

*viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā*  
*samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam*  
*sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*  
*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi \|\|*

**Let me surrender to you, Bhagavān. You are the condensed form of pure knowledge and by you are bliss itself you have forms with many qualities. Your desires are always fulfilled. Because your svarūpa-śakti you are never touched by the material guṇas. SB 10.37.22**

*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo*  
*'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who destroys of all sin, and who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions, became attracted to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and mercifully spread the Bhāgavatam which describes Kṛṣṇa and reveals the rasa of his pastimes. SB 12.12.69**

Thus māyā refers to the cit-śakti since māyā meaning “illusion” would be contrary to the intention of the speaker. Śrīdhara Svāmī explains the word yoga-māyā as cit-śakti.

Bhagavān’s Activities

“Bur attaining birth and activities means that it happened at a certain time.” He attains these eternally *(anukālam*) and never gives them up. The svarūpa-śakti produces cause and effect together.

“How can birth and activities be eternal? They are activities. Action is accomplished by a sequence of parts from beginning to end. Without that action’s very nature would be destroyed.” This is not a fault since the Lord’s forms are always eternal, the manifestations of his form are eternal, the pastimes like birth and activities are eternal, and the manifestations in the spiritual world and material world of his places and associates in unlimited universes and unlimited Vaikuṇṭhas are eternal. Though his birth and activities appear and disappear, with beginning and end, in some places portions of his birth and activities end, in other places they do not end, and in other places they begin. Since there is no break in continuity of their performance, the birth and activities exist eternally. Sometimes they are slightly different because of different details and sometimes they are exactly the same because of the same details. One form alone performs actions separately in different manifestations. This is Nārada’s intention in saying *citraṁ bataitad ekena vapuṣā*: how astonishing, in one form he performed activities in each palace. In different manifestations where the actions take place there is a different identity (of Kṛṣṇa and his associates) since separate action takes place. This being the case, the manifestation of rasa generated by the sequence of pastimes is experienced in each place.

“How can the birth and activities be one, since they begin separately in the different manifestations?” There is oneness of the same action arising at different times. Saṅkara in his commentary says:

*dvir-go-śabdobhayam uccarito na tu dvau go-śabdāv iti pratīti-nirṇītaṁ śabdaikatvam*

**When one says the word “cow” twice, one does not perceive two different words but one.**

It is correct to say that the birth and activities are eternal. In the āgamas there are prescriptions for meditating on pastimes which happened in the past. (But the pastimes are eternal.)

*paramātma-sambandhitvena nityatvāt trivikramatvādiṣv apy upasaṁhāryatvaṁ yujyate*

**It is proper to gather Vāmana and other forms in meditation since everything related to the Lord is eternal. Madhva’s bhāṣya**

*yad gataṁ bhavac ca bhaviṣyac ca*

**The Lord was present in the past, is present now and will be present in the future. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.8.3**

*Upasaṁhāryatvam* means that it is proper to gather all forms during meditation. The Lord’s birth is different from material birth. It is an appearance of the Lord, which resembles a material birth. Sometime it is not similar to a material birth also. *Ajāyamānā bahudhā vijāyate:* unborn, he is born in many ways. (Puruṣa-sūkta)

*niśīthe tama-udbhūte jāyamāne janārdane*  
*devakyāṁ deva-rūpiṇyāṁ viṣṇuḥ sarva-guhā-śayaḥ*  
*āvirāsīd yathā prācyāṁ diśīndur iva puṣkalaḥ*

**Then the Lord, complete with all his expansions, who is situated in the core of everyone’s heart, responding to the request o f his devotees, appeared from Devakī, non-different from the Lord, in the dense darkness of night, like the full moon rising on the eastern horizon. SB10.3.8**

*satyaṁ vidhātuṁ nija-bhṛtya-bhāṣitaṁ*  
*vyāptiṁ ca bhūteṣv akhileṣu cātmanaḥ*  
*adṛśyatātyadbhuta-rūpam udvahan*  
*stambhe sabhāyāṁ na mṛgaṁ na mānuṣam*

**To prove the words of his servant--that he is present in all objects--to be true, the Lord showed an astonishing form which was neither animal nor human to the assembly. SB 7.8.17**

*tasyāṁ bahu-tithe kale bhagavān madhusūdanaḥ*  
*kārdamaṁ vīryam āpanno jajñe 'gnir iva dāruṇi*

**After a long time, the Supreme Lord, Madhusūdana, who is like fire in wood, controlled by the strength of bhakti of Kardama, made his appearance. SB 3.24.6**

By the power of Kardama’s bhakti the avatāra Kapila appeared in this manner. The word *vīrya* (semen) is used only because this is well known for producing a son. The activity is different from material birth, being a pastime of the Lord’s bliss in his svarūpa. The philosophers say *lokavat tu līlā-kaivalyam:* the Lord’s pastimes without motive, similar to actions in this world. (Brahma-sūtra 2.1.33) In this world a madman out of intense bliss dances without a particular motive. Similarly the Lord acts without motive.

*sṛṣṭyādikaṁ harir naiva prayojanam apekṣya tu \|*  
*kurute kevalānandād yathā mattasya nartanam \|\|*  
*pūrṇānandasya tasyeha prayojana-matiḥ kutaḥ \|*  
*muktā avyāpta-kāmāḥ syuḥ kim utāsy akhilātmanaḥ \|\|*

**Without a goal, the Lord carries out creation out of bliss alone, like a mad man dancing. What is the question of a goal for the Lord who is complete bliss? Persons who are liberated have no desires to be fulfilled. What then to speak of desires in the Lord, the soul of all beings? Nārāyaṇa-saṁhitā**

In this example, the mad man is ignorant. One should not imply this in the Lord. Rather the example is used to show that the Lord enacts pastimes without considering his goal, by simply out of great inherent bliss. Similarly one could find fault with the example of inhaling and exhaling. This is natural, but in deep sleep the person is also unaware (whereas the Lord is always aware). Thus the Lord’s pastimes arise from the natural bliss of his svarūpa. Śruti says *devasyaiva svabhāvo’yam āpta-kāmasya kā spṛhā*: the act of creation is the nature of the Lord; for one whose desires are fulfilled at all times, what desire does he have? Māṇḍukya Upaniṣad

If the Lord’s actions in relation to creation of the universe are unmotivated, then what can one say about his actions in the Vaikuṇṭha?

*avyākṛta-vihārāya sarva-vyākṛta-siddhaye*  
*hṛṣīkeśa namas te ’stu munaye mauna-śīline*

**O Lord Hṛṣīkeśa, master of the senses, please let us offer our obeisances unto you, whose pastimes are beyond matter, whose pastimes are proclaimed everywhere, unto you who are ātmārāma but not ātmārāma. SB 10.16.47**

The attraction of Śukadeva and others for the Lord’s pastimes (without material motive) is therefore commendable.

The nature of the Lord’s birth is understood in the following verse from the chapter on the mystery of the Lord’s birth.

*evaṁ ca janmāni karmāṇi hy akartur ajanasya ca \|*  
*varṇayanti sma kavayo veda-guhyāni hṛt-pateḥ \|\|*

**Thus, rejecting the two versions of the universal form, the wise glorify the highest subject of the Vedas--the birth and birth and activities of the Supreme Lord, who resides in the hearts of all beings (unlike the universal form), who has no material birth and no material activities (unlike the jīva). SB 1.3.35**

The previous two verses are as follows:

*yatreme sad-asad-rūpe pratiṣiddhe sva-saṁvidā \|*  
*avidyayātmani kṛte iti tad brahma-darśanam \|\|*

**When the gross and subtle material forms, which are impositions on the soul in ignorance, are removed by the realization of the devotees, one can realize Brahman.**

*yady eṣoparatā devī māyā vaiśāradī matiḥ \|*  
*sampanna eveti vidur mahimni sve mahīyate \|\|*

**When māya or ignorance weakens, the Lord bestows his mercy on the jīva. The jīva becomes endowed with this treasure. The wise understand that, being situated in this glorious position, this person is worthy of worship. SB 1.3.33-34**

One attains Brahman by direct knowledge of one’s svarūpa, caused by negating the gross and subtle coverings created by ignorance. When māyā ceases, one realizes the wealth of one’s svarūpa.

The ātmārāmas (*kavayaḥ*) describe the birth and activities of the Lord in the heart, Paramātmā. When the gross and subtle coverings cease the wealth of realization of his birth and activities arises. Wealth (*sampattī)* means directly seeing. Thus the forms of the Lord are filled with the bliss of the Lord’s svarūpa since they are completely different from material forms. Thus he is described as being without action and without birth (material) and his birth and actions are hidden in the Vedas.

This is explained in Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary on the following verses of Akrūra’s praises. (SB 10.48.23-24) “Are my avatāras and their actions produced by ignorance, like shell being mistaken for silver? No, this is just your pastime.”

The Lord’s pastimes are equal to his svarūpa:

*nāma-karma-svarūpāṇi na pariccheda-gocare \|*  
*yasyākhila-pramāṇānāṁ sa viṣṇur garbhagas tava \|\|*

**The Lord’s names, activities and forms are not to be understood any proofs. That Viṣṇu has entered your womb.**

Another version has *rūpa-karma.*

Gītā also says *janma-karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ*: one should know my attractive birth and activities. (BG 4.9)

Bhagavān’s Name

The Lord’s name is not material. It is self manifesting, supported by qualities beyond material words and mind.

*aprasiddhes tad-guṇānām anāmāsau prakīrtitaḥ*

**The Lord is glorified as nameless because his qualities are not known in this world. Vāsudevādhyātma**

*anāmā so’prasiddhatvād arūpo bhūta-varjanāt*

**He is without material name because he is not known in the world. He is without material form because he is devoid of material elements. Brahmā Purāṇa**

This is not contrary to the statement in Viṣṇu Purāṇa:

*na yatra nātha vidyante nāma-jātyādi-kalpanāḥ \|*  
*tad brahma paramaṁ nityam avikāri bhavān aja \|\|*  
*na kalpanām ṛte’rthasya sarvasyādhigamo yataḥ \|*  
*tataḥ kṛṣṇācyutānanta-viṣṇu-nāmabhir īḍyase \|\|*

**You have no manufactured name or caste. You are the eternal, unchanging supreme Brahman. O unborn Lord! Since there can be no understanding of meaning without making names, you are worshipped by the names of Viṣṇu, Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta and Ananta (while rejecting manufactured names). Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.18.53-54**

In the apparent meaning the word *kalpana* (manufactured) seems useless. One could simply say the Lord has no name and caste and convey the meaning. Moreover using the words Brahman and *aja*, the names of the supreme Lord are accepted. For instance there is a statement like *ajām ekām lohita-śila-kṛṣṇām* which indicated a person without birth. Saying that Kṛṣṇa’s name is produced (*kalpanā*) would be wrong. How can someone be worshipped with a manufactured name, since anything manufactured is non-eternal. How could Kṛṣṇa be non-eternal? The meaning should be “In the Lord’s names like Kṛṣṇa and in his pastimes as a devatā, human or kṣatriya, there is nothing manufactured.”

*viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā*  
*samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam*  
*sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*  
*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi*

**Let me surrender to you, Bhagavān. You are the condensed form of pure knowledge and because you are bliss itself you have forms with many qualities. Your desires are always fulfilled. Because your svarūpa-śakti you are never touched by the material guṇas. SB 10.37.22**

According to this statement, the forms are aspects of the Lord’s eternal svarūpa-śakti. Since *(yataḥ)* material understanding cannot take place without making names etc for all objects seen, therefore (*tataḥ*), because of that, you, who are self accomplished, are praised by the sages and Vedas through famous names such as Kṛṣṇa, which are also self-accomplished, the essences of the Vedas, beyond all material proofs, while rejecting all manufactured meanings and names to designate you. You are not praised by other manufactured names. Your glory is revealed by these names with great power. Or, your glory is made evident by these names.

By these authorized names, well known in scriptures, the Lord is quickly realized. These names have power even if uttered to mean some other object (*sāṅketa).* These names are self accomplished and other names are manufactured.

Another meaning is “O Lord! You are the supreme Brahman, the sole object, to which names cannot be applied.” The Lord cannot be the subject of manufactured names because then he becomes specialized (*viśeṣeṇa karoti),* he performs actions, he changes *(vikāri*). You are thus *avikāri.* You do not manifest *(jāyate)* in that form. Thus you are called *aja.* What can be the support to create names for you? Without manufacturing names the meaning of objects cannot be understood. When what to speak of understanding you without giving a name? But since manufactured names cannot represent the essential nature of anything, you are praised by names like Kṛṣṇa, which indicate your qualities, which give all puruṣārthas just as you yourself do, even if thought of by accident. You are praised by eternally perfect śruti and Purāṇas, not by imaginary words indicating impersonalism.

The four names mentioned in the verse—Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta, Ananta and Viṣṇu-are representative of other names for it is said that names like Nārāyaṇa also have great power if uttered by accident.

Upavarṣa says *varṇa eva tu śabda*: the syllables are authorative.

*tasya ca nityatvāt*

**Jīvas and devatās are controllers because of the eternal will of the Lord. Brahma-sūtra 2.4.16**

Since the syllables are eternal, the names composed of syllables, which are the essence of the Vedas are also eternal. Brahmā says:

*teṣv akṣareṣu bhaviṣyaj-jagad-rūpaṁ prakāśayan*

**The Lord revealed the future universe within the syllables of the mantra.**

The names of the Lord are shown to be self accomplished since they are unique in causing the universe through names arising when the Lord appeared. The names have the unique quality of being non-different from the Lord’s svarūpa.

*oṁ āsya jānanto nāma cid viviktan mahas te viṣṇo sumatiṁ bhajāmahe \| oṁ tat sad*

**O Viṣṇu! Your name is spiritual amd self-revealed*****.*** **Knowing that name a little*****,*** **not completely, chanting impurely we have attained fine knowledge of you because that oṁ is self accomplished.** ***Ṛg Veda*** **1.156.3**

This is the meaning. O Viṣṇu! Your (*te)* name *(nāma*) is cit-svarūpam (*cit*) and therefore self revealing *(mahaḥ*). We know (*jānantaḥ*) the name (*asya*) slightly *(ā*) by honouring its glories through chanting. However we attain knowledge (*sumatim)* by uttering it *(vivaktan*), just by repeating the syllables because that oṁ (*tat*) is self accomplished (*sat*).

Thus it is said that the name gives liberation even by accidental utterance, just as the form of the Lord appears even through fear or hatred.

*apy anya-cittaḥ kruddho vā yaḥ sadā kīrtayed dharim \|*  
*so’pi bandha-ksayān muktiṁ labhec cedi-patir yathā \|\|*

**He who continually chants the name of the Lord inattentively or in anger, destroys bondage and attains liberation just like Śiśupāla. Padma Purāṇa**

By chanting the name once samsāra is destroyed, just as by seeing the Lord it is destroyed.

*sakṛd uccāritaṁ yena harir ity akṣara-dvayam \|*  
*baddhaḥ parikaras tena mokṣāya gamanaṁ prati \|\|*

**Whoever chants the two syllables “Hari” only once is ready for attaining liberation.** ***Skanda Purāṇa***

The name oṁ is glorified:

*om ity etat brahmaṇo nediṣṭaṁ nāma yasmād uccāryamāṇa eva saṁsāra-bhayāt tārayati tasmād ucyate tāra*

***Oṁ*** **is another name of the Brahman. Since by chanting oṁ one is freed from the fear of saṁsāra, it is called** ***tāra*** **(liberator).**

One should not think that the statement is exaggeration since that is an offense according to Padma Purāṇa (*tathārthavādo harināmni kalpanam*).

*nānuvrajati yo mohād vrajantaṁ jagad-īśvaram \|*  
*jñānāgni-dagdha-karmāpi sa bhaved brahma-rākṣasaḥ \|\|*

**He who does not follow the Lord in his chariot when he moves becomes a** ***brahma-rākṣasa*** **even though he has destroyed all karmas with the fire of knowledge. Viṣṇu-bhakti-candrodaya**

Even if a person accepts the Lord’s name again he enters saṁsāra by committing offense of thinking that the statements of scripture are exaggeration or imaginary. The name is a form of bliss, and witness in the heart of the devotee, like the form of the Lord. Śaunaka says:

*tad aśma-sāraṁ hṛdayaṁ batedaṁ*  
*yad gṛhyamāṇair hari-nāma-dheyaiḥ \|*  
*na vikriyetātha yadā vikāro*  
*netre jalaṁ gātra-ruheṣu harṣaḥ \|\|*

**That heart which does not transform on hearing the names of the Lord, even though he shows tears in the eyes and hair standing on end, is made of iron. SB 2.3.24**

In Skanda Purāṇa, Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa, the name of Kṛṣṇa is said to be his svarūpa, giving the results of all the Vedas for those who chant.

*madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ*  
*sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam \|*  
*sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā*  
*bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma \|\|*

**The name of Kṛṣṇa is sweeter than the sweetest, the most auspicious of all things auspicious, the highest fruit in the tree of all the Vedas, and is composed entirely of pure consciousness. O best of Bhṛgu’s dynasty! Heard once with faith or in negligence, it can deliver any human being.** ***Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa, Padma Purāṇa***

The name is the svarūpa of the Lord. This is made clear in Nārada-pañcarātra:

*vyaktaṁ hi bhagavān eva sākṣān-nārāyaṇaḥ svayam \|*  
*aṣṭākṣara-svarūpena mukheṣu parivartate \|\|*

**It is clear that the Lord, Nārāyaṇa is directly in the mouth in the form of the eight syllable mantra.**

Māṇḍukya Upaniṣad describes praṇava. *Oṁ ity etad akṣaram idaṁ sarvaṁ:* oṁ is everything. Chāndogya Upaniṣad *says oṁkāra evedaṁ sarvam*: oṁ is everything.

*praṇavo hy aparaṁ brahma praṇavaś ca paraṁ smṛtam \|*  
*apūrvo’nantaro’bāhyo’naparaḥ praṇavo’vyayaḥ \|\|*  
*sarvasya praṇavo hy ādir madhyam antas tathaiva ca \|*  
*evaṁ hi praṇavaṁ jñātvā vyaśnute tad-anantaram \|\|*  
*praṇavaṁ hīśvaraṁ vidyāt sarvasya hṛdaye sthitam \|*  
*sarva-vyāpinam oṅkāraṁ matvā dhīro na śocati \|\|*  
*amātro’nanta-mātraś ca dvaitasyopaśamaḥ śivaḥ \|*  
*oṅkāro vidito yena sa munir netaro janaḥ \|\|*

**There is nothing compared to oṁ. It is considered supreme Brahman. It has no beginning or end, nothing is beyond it, nothing can compare to it. It is imperishable. Oṁ is the beginning, middle and end of everything. Knowing oṁ one attains the Lord. Know that oṁ is the Lord situated in everyone’s heart. Understanding that it is all-pervading the wise man does not lament. it is without measure and of infinite measure. It is all auspicious, destroying duality. He who knows oṁ and no other person is a wise man. Māṇḍukya-kārikā 26-29**

One should not think that this is just praise of oṁ, thinking the syllable is unsuitable for the supreme Lord. Like other avatāras, oṁ is an avatāra of the Lord in the form of syllables. It is to be accepted on the strength of śruti that oṁ is non-different from the Lord. The name and the Lord designated by the name (*namī*) are non-different.

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ \|*  
*pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto 'bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ \|\|*

**The name of the Lord is cintāmaṇi. It is Kṛṣṇa himself, a form of consciousness and rasa. It is complete, pure, and eternally liberated since there is no difference between the name and Kṛṣṇa himself.**

The meaning is this. The name is cintāmaṇi since it bestows everything. Not only that. It is directly Kṛṣṇa, having consciousness like him. The reason is that it is non-different from him. “How can such a name be generated in the senses of a human?” It does not arise from the senses, since it, like the Vedas, is simply made manifest in the senses of a human by the Lord himself.

*śabda-brahma su-durbodhaṁ prāṇendriya-mano-mayam*  
*ananta-pāraṁ gambhīraṁ durvigāhyaṁ samudra-vat*

**The sound of the Vedas is very difficult to comprehend and manifests on different levels within the prāṇa, mind, intelligence and senses. This Vedic sound is unlimited and deep like the ocean. SB 11.21.36**

*mayopabṛṁhitaṁ bhūmnā brahmaṇānanta-śaktinā*  
*bhūteṣu ghoṣa-rūpeṇa biseṣūrṇeva lakṣyate*

**The Vedas, which are spread everywhere by me because I possess many forms, pervade everywhere as Brahman, and possess unlimited powers, are perceived by the wise as nāda within all beings, subtle as the threads within the lotus stalk. SB 11.21.37**

*kṣīṇāyuṣaḥ kṣīṇa-sattvān durmedhān vīkṣya kālataḥ*  
*vedān brahmarṣayo vyasyan hṛdi-sthācyuta-coditāḥ*

**Observing that people in general were diminished in their life span, strength and intelligence by the influence of time, great sages, inspired by the Lord situated within their hearts, divided the Vedas. SB 12.6.47**

The commentary says, “One may worry that something arising from the power of human intelligence will not be respected. Thus the verse says that the sages were inspired by the Lord in their hearts to divide the Vedas.”

The same form (*tad-rūpeṇa*) was communicated as Bhāgavatam:

*kasmai yena vibhāsito 'yam atulo jñāna-pradīpaḥ purā*  
*tad-rūpeṇa ca nāradāya munaye kṛṣṇāya tad-rūpiṇā*  
*yogīndrāya tad-ātmanātha bhagavad-rātāya kāruṇyatas*  
*tac chuddhaṁ vimalaṁ viśokam amṛtaṁ satyaṁ paraṁ dhīmahi*

**I meditate upon that pure, spotless Nārāyaṇa, who is free from suffering and death and who previously revealed this incomparable lamp of knowledge to Brahmā. Brahmā then spoke it to the sage Nārada, who narrated it to Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana Vyāsa. Śrīla Vyāsa revealed this Bhāgavatam to the greatest of sages, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, and Śukadeva mercifully spoke it to Mahārāja Parīkṣit. SB 12.13.19**

The spiritual nature of the name is indicated in the prayers to Kṛṣṇa in Devakī’s womb:

*na nāma-rūpe guṇa-janma-karmabhir*  
*nirūpitavye tava tasya sākṣiṇaḥ*  
*mano-vacobhyām anumeya-vartmano*  
*deva kriyāyāṁ pratiyanty athāpi hi*

**O Lord! Your name and form representing your qualities, birth and activities, cannot be realized since you are the witness of all things, and can only be inferred by logic and scripture. The devotees realize you through devotional actions. SB 10.2.36**

The form of the Lord is nonmaterial. It appears by the svarūpa-śakti which is self-manifesting. That was shown previously.

*ātma-tattva-viśuddhy-arthaṁ yad āha bhagavān ṛtam \|*  
*brahmaṇe darśayan rūpam avyalīka-vratādṛtaḥ \|\|*

**Worshipped by sincere devotion, revealing his true, spiritual form, the Lord spoke to Brahmā the four essential verses of Bhāgavatam in order to give knowledge about himself. SB 2.9.4**

The commentary says, “Since it was described in the previous chapter (SB 2.8.8) that the Lord also has a form, how can bhakti to that form produce liberation? This verse answers. For the purpose giving knowledge of the tattva about jīva *(ātmanaḥ*) the Lord spoke to Brahmā about worship through meditation. He shows his eternal form filled with knowledge *(ṛtam*) because he had been served by austerity (*avyalīka-vratādṛtaḥ*). The meaning is this. By ignorance the jīva produces a false relationship with a body. The Lord simply manifests his body of knowledge by yogamāyā. There is a great difference. Thus by worshipping the Lord one can attain liberation.”

The form was established as spiritual by Vasudeva in SB 10.3.20-21.

*sa tvaṁ tri-loka-sthitaye sva-māyayā*  
*bibharṣi śuklaṁ khalu varṇam ātmanaḥ*  
*sargāya raktaṁ rajasopabṛṁhitaṁ*  
*kṛṣṇaṁ ca varṇaṁ tamasā janātyaye*

**My Lord, your form is transcendental to the three material modes, yet for the maintenance of the three worlds, you assume the white color of Viṣṇu in goodness; for creation, which is surrounded by the quality of passion, you appear reddish; and at the end, when there is a need for annihilation, which is surrounded by ignorance, you appear blackish.**

The meaning is this. When you (*tvam*), the agent of creation, maintenance and destruction of the material world *(saḥ*), desire to dwell in the world (*triloka-sthitaye*), using māyā-śakti which takes shelter of you (*sva*), you protect the brāhmaṇas (*śukla-varṇam*) dedicated to dharma, created by you (*ātmanaḥ*). This māyā is in material sattva since it is inferior to the Lord and suitable to brāhmaṇas.

When you desire to create, using your māyā in rajas, you protect the various varṇas including brāhmaṇas who have material desires (*raktam varṇam*). When you desire to destroy the population, using māyā in tamas, you protect those engaged in sin (*kṛṣṇam varṇam*).

There is another meaning. When you desire to maintain the worlds, you maintain the pure form of Viṣṇu, without mixture of the guṇas. Viṣṇu is not associated with guṇas like Śiva and Brahmā. Śukadeva states this:

*śivaḥ śakti-yutaḥ śaśvat tri-liṅgo guṇa-saṁvṛtaḥ*  
*vaikārikas taijasaś ca tāmasaś cety ahaṁ tridhā*

**Śiva is always united with his personal energy, the material nature. Manifesting himself in three features in response to the entreaties of nature’s three modes, he thus embodies the threefold principle of material ego in goodness, passion and ignorance. SB 10.88.3**

*harir hi nirguṇaḥ sākṣāt puruṣaḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ*  
*sa sarva-dṛg upadraṣṭā taṁ bhajan nirguṇo bhavet*

**The Lord, however, has no connection with the material modes. He is the Supreme Lord, the all-seeing eternal witness, who is transcendental to material nature. One who worships him becomes similarly free from the material modes. SB 10.88.5**

*candrikā-viśada-smeraiḥ sāruṇāpāṅga-vīkṣitaiḥ*  
*svakārthānām iva rajaḥ- sattvābhyāṁ sraṣṭṛ-pālakāḥ*

**Those Viṣṇu forms, by their smiling glances, which resembled the pure light of the moon, which were also tinged with red, created and protected the desires of their own devotees, as if by the modes of passion and goodness. SB 10.13.50**

The expressions concerning sattva and rajas are figurative, not real.  *Varṇam* means form, not just color (such as white color) since the subject concerns the guṇas and indicates the forms manifested by the guṇas. The form of Viṣṇu for protection, a guṇāvatāra, is well known to be *śyāma* in complexion, and lives in the Milk Ocean. This is shown in Paramātmā Sandarbha. And the destroyer Śiva is known to be white in color, not black. These forms are described in the sandhyopāsāna by Gobhila. Brahmā is golden in complexion. Thus there is no rule for relating the complexions of the personalities to the guṇas. Baka and other entities at the lowest tamas were also blackish.

You manifest (*bibharṣi)* your form to your devotees by mercy (*sva-māyayā*). Red represents the intense passion of creation in rajas. Black represents the complete absence of the svarūpa of the Lord in tamas.

*pārthivād dāruṇo dhūmas tasmād agnis trayīmayaḥ \|*  
*tamasas tu rajas tasmāt sattvaṁ yad brahma-darśanam \|\|*

**Smoke is superior to dull wood, and fire, sacred to the Vedas, is superior to smoke. Similarly rajas is superior to tamas, and sattva is superior to rajas, since it is favorable for realizing the Lord. SB 1.2.24**

“Why do you describe through words with another meaning something which misleads people. For destroying the population, the color black is accepted by tamas. That is the meaning.” The next verse answers.

*tvam asya lokasya vibho rirakṣiṣur*  
*gṛhe ’vatīrṇo ’si mamākhileśvara*  
*rājanya-saṁjñāsura-koṭi-yūthapair*  
*nirvyūhyamānā nihaniṣyase camūḥ*

**O my Lord, proprietor of all creation! You have now appeared in my house, desiring to protect this world. You will kill all the armies that are moving all over the world along with the host of demons dressed as kṣatriya kings. SB 10.3.21**

*Nirvyūhyamānāḥ* means moving about. The meaning is this. Let us forget about other proofs concerning your śuddha-sattva nature as Brahman. Though you accept a form according to the guṇa, such as sleeping during pralaya, since pralaya is a matter of grief, you do not do that at this time. You should give happiness to the world by protecting it, by destroying the tamasic demons, but instead, on the pretext of killing them, you mercifully give them liberation beyond the guṇas. This is like on the pretext of bringing salt, bringing a horse. Saindhava means salt and horse.11 It is said:

*jaya-kāle tu sattvasya devarṣīn rajaso 'surān*  
*tamaso yakṣa-rakṣāṁsi tat-kālānuguṇo 'bhajat*

**When sattva becomes prominent the Lord favors the devatās and sages. When rajas becomes prominent he favors the demons. When tamas becomes prominent he favors the Yakṣas and Rākṣasas. The Lord acts according to the dictates of time. SB 7.1.8**

Thus black is not produced by tamas and red and white are not produced by rajas and sattva. By the last statement it is indicated that the svarūpa-śakti is the ultimate cause. Devakī indicates this with reverence:

*rūpaṁ yat tat prāhur avyaktam ādyaṁ*  
*brahma jyotir nirguṇaṁ nirvikāram*  
*sattā-mātraṁ nirviśeṣaṁ nirīhaṁ*  
*sa tvaṁ sākṣād viṣṇur adhyātma-dīpaḥ*

**That form which they say is invisible, the cause of all causes, the greatest, self-manifesting, without qualities, without change, pure existence, without variety and without action, is directly you, the Supreme Lord, who manifests our intelligence. SB 103.24**

Bhagavān’s purpose is bliss

Let us return to the subject. The Lord’s qualities are exceptional, astonishing enough to attract the ātmārāmas.

*ātmārāmāś ca munayo nirgranthā apy urukrame \|*  
*kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ \|\|*

**Some sages who are ātmārāmas, beyond the scriptures, false ego and rules, also practice unmotivated, pure bhakti to the master of pure bhakti, Kṛṣṇa, since he possesses qualities attractive to even them. SB 1.7.10**

*harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|*  
*adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|*

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam. SB 1.7.11**

*guṇāḥ sarve’pi yujyante hy aiśvaryāt puruṣottame \|*  
*doṣāḥ kathañcin naivātra yujyante paramo hi saḥ \|\|*  
*guṇa-doṣau māyayaiva kecid āhur apaṇḍitāḥ \|*  
*na tatra māyā māyī vā tadīyau tau kuto hy ataḥ \|\|*  
*tasmān na māyayā sarvaṁ sarvam aiśvarya-sambhavam \|*  
*amāyo hīśvaro yasmāt tasmāt taṁ paramaṁ viduḥ \|\|*

**By his power the Lord is endowed with all good qualities and devoid of all faults. Fools say the Lord has good and bad qualities out of bewilderment. There is no māyā or anything made of māyā in the Lord. There is thus no māyā in things related to the Lord. The world is not created by māyā. It arises by the power of the Lord. Because the Lord is beyond māyā he is called supreme by the wise. Viṣnū-dharmottara**

This completes the explanation of SB 8.3.3. Thus it is proved that the qualities and name of Lord are completely different from those in the material world.

“Accepting that the name, form and qualities arise from your svarūpa, since your svarūpa is perfect in itself, what is the purpose of your having these?” *Lokāpyaya-sambhavāya:* he accepts them for destruction and attainment. (SB 8.3.8) He accepts the forms for destroying saṁsāra for the devotees and attainment of happiness of bhakti for them. *Sambhavāya* means “for attaining.” It also means to give special happiness of bhakti to the nitya-siddhas (who have already attained him). Arjuna says:

*tathāyaṁ cāvatāras te bhuvo bhāra-jihīrṣayā \|*  
*svānāṁ cānanya-bhāvānām anudhyānāya cāsakṛt \|\|*

**You appear in this world in this way, in your spiritual form, with a desire to relieve the burden of the earth and to give continuous happiness to your dedicated devotees by letting them worship you. SB 1.7.25**

The meaning is this. Just as with the other avatāras, the puruṣāvatāras, so this avatāra, your manifestation as Kṛṣṇa, Svayam Bhagavān, appeared in order to relieve the burden of the highest bhakti *(bhuvaḥ*) and to give happiness of worshipping you constantly *(anudhyānāya)* to other devotees.

“You say that the goal of having forms is to give happiness of bhakti to devotees. Since you are full of bliss, why would you think of their happiness?” The Lord who knows everything is without fault. He would have the fault of lack of compassion in ignoring the devotees who depend only on him (*ananya-bhāvānām*). Compassion appears even in ātmārāmas.

*tathāpi doṣāḥ parame naivāhāryaḥ kathañcana \|*  
*guṇā viruddhā apy ete samāhāryāḥ samantataḥ \|\|*

**Still, the fault of birth and transformation can never be found in the Supreme Lord. But contrary qualities are found together in the Lord. Kūrma Purāṇa**

Thus this is possible for the Lord who has a variety of qualities. The śrutis glorify with wonder a portion of this quality spread elsewhere in things related to him. This quality attracts everyone from Brahmā to the lowest entity.

The Lord says:

*bhajato ’pi na vai kecid bhajanty abhajataḥ kutaḥ*  
*ātmārāmā hy āpta-kāmā akṛta-jñā guru-druhaḥ*

**Those individuals who are spiritually self-satisfied, those materially satisfied, those who are ignorant, and those who intentionally offend those who act for others’ welfare do not respond even those who love them, what to speak of those who are inimical.**

*nāhaṁ tu sakhyo bhajato ’pi jantūn*  
*bhajāmy amīṣām anuvṛtti-vṛttaye*  
*yathādhano labdha-dhane vinaṣṭe*  
*tac-cintayānyan nibhṛto na veda*

**But the reason I do not immediately reciprocate the affection of living beings even when they worship me, O gopīs, is that I want to intensify their loving devotion. They then become like a poor man who has gained some wealth and then lost it, and who thus becomes so anxious about it that he can think of nothing else. SB 10.32.19-20**

The mercy of the all powerful Lord whose goal is happiness of the devotee is the Lord’s astonishing excellent nature arising from the play of the bliss of his svarūpa. That is shown in *anukālam ṛcchatīti:* he eternally accepts birth, in the already quoted:

*na vidyate yasya ca janma karma vā*  
*na nāma-rūpe guṇa-doṣa eva vā*  
*tathāpi lokāpyaya-sambhavāya yaḥ*  
*sva-māyayā tāny anukālam ṛcchati*

**He who has no birth or activities, no name or form, and no qualities or faults, eternally accepts birth and activities as Brahmā and Śiva at the time creation and destruction by his energies of rajas and tamas for creating and destroying the universe. SB 8.3.8**

One should not think he has any other goal in doing these actions. The goal of the Lord who is most capable is simply bliss.

*kṛpālor asamarthasya duḥkhāyaiva kṛpālutā \|*  
*samarthasya tu tasyaiva sukhāyaiva kṛpālutā \|\|*

**For a person who cannot show compassion a show of compassion causes him suffering. For a person capable of compassion showing compassion makes him happy.**

Gajendra speaks to the Lord.

## 50.

Verses describing the Lord as not having hands or feet are in reference to the Lord with an infinite form of self-manifesting bliss.

*tvam akaraṇaḥ sva-rāḍ akhila-kāraka-śakti-dharas*  
*tava balim udvahanti samadanty ajayānimiṣāḥ*  
*varṣa-bhujo ’khila-kṣiti-pater iva viśva-sṛjo*  
*vidadhati yatra ye tv adhikṛtā bhavataś cakitāḥ*

**Though you have no material senses, you are the self-effulgent sustainer of everyone’s sensory powers. The devatās and material nature herself offer you tribute, while also enjoying the tribute offered them by their worshipers, just as subordinate rulers of various districts in a kingdom offer tribute to their lord, the ultimate proprietor of the land, while also enjoying the tribute paid them by their own subjects. In this way the universal creators faithfully execute their assigned services out of fear of you. SB 10.87.28**

The meaning is this. Just as an axe impelled by the power of the agent performs its task of cutting, and is different from the agent, the sense (*karaṇa*) is a substance which has accepted the function of a sense organ. It does not have this function as its nature. Similarly an object is capable of burning another object because it accepts the power of burning. It is a secondary feature of a substance. The word *svarāṭ* means ultimately the svarūpa-śakti which is operates by itself (*svena* from *sva*). Since the jīva is conscious and the hands, feet and other senses are unconscious, the jīva’s senses are śaktis different from jīva and dependent on the jīva. The jīva, because he is attached to the senses, is called *sa-karaṇa.* You, the antaryāmī, because you are not attached to the senses and are not dependent on them, are called *akaraṇa* since you preside (*rājase =rāṭ*) by your svarūpa-śakti (*sva*). Even at the time of final devastation the Lord’s senses remain:

*striya uragendra-bhoga-bhuja-daṇḍa-viṣakta-dhiyo*  
*vayam api te samāḥ sama-dṛśo ’ṅghri-saroja-sudhāḥ*

**We śrutis, who generally see you as all-pervading, will achieve the same nectar from your lotus feet that your consorts are able to relish because of their loving attraction to your mighty, serpentine arms, for you look upon us and your consorts in the same way. SB 10.87.23**

The learned śrutis describe his various spiritual senses including his arms which support them, but also describe the Lord as *akaraṇa* (without senses), since he does not have senses like the jīva. Why? He exists by his svarūpa which manifests his senses through his svarūpa-śakti (*sva-rāṭ*). They cannot be called senses like those of the jīva since they arise from his svarūpa. How could you manifest yourself independently (*svarāṭ*) by conditional objects?

*ānanda-mātram ajaraṁ purāṇam ekaṁ santaṁ bahudhā dṛśyamānaṁ neha nānāsti kiñcana*

**The Lord is complete bliss, without old age, is the oldest, and a single being, though seen in many forms. There is no variety at all. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.19**

*nirdoṣa-pūrṇa-guṇa-vigraha ātma-tantro*  
*niścetanātmaka-śarīra-guṇaiś ca hīnaḥ*  
*ānanda-mātra-kara-pāda-mukhodarādiḥ*  
*sarvatra ca svagata-bheda-vivarjitātmā*

**The Lord is a form with faultless, perfect qualities. He is independent. He is devoid of the qualities of the material body. His hands, feet, face and belly are all bliss. He is devoid of internal difference everywhere. Nārada Pañcarātra**

“What is the proof of existence of these svarūpa śaktis?” You give the sense organs and their śaktis to all beings (*akhila-kāraka-śakti-dharaḥ)* since you maintain the senses in those beings*.* This means that those śaktis are self accomplished, complete and indestructible in you. *Prāṇasya prāṇam uta cakṣuṣaś cakṣuḥ:* you are the prāṇa of the prāṇa and the eye of the eye. (Kena Upaniṣad 1.2) *Svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca:* the Lord possesses natural śaktis of jñāna, bala and kriya. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8)

*yat-kāya eṣa bhuvana-traya-sanniveśo*  
*yasyendriyais tanu-bhṛtām ubhayendriyāṇi*  
*jñānaṁ svataḥ śvasanato balam oja īhā*  
*sattvādibhiḥ sthiti-layodbhava ādi-kartā*

**Within the body of Mahā-viṣṇu reside clusters of millions of universes composed of three planetary systems. By the Lord’s senses, the action and knowledge senses of the jīvas arise. From his expansion antaryāmī, the knowledge of the jīvas arises. From his prāṇa the physical and sensual strength and action of the jīvas arise. He is the final cause of creation, maintenance and destruction through the three guṇas. SB 11.4.4**

*vikaraṇatvān neti cet tad uktam*

**If one objects that the Lord cannot be the creator because he has no senses, the answer has already been stated. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.31**

*śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt*

**The defects of the agent do not apply to Brahman because of scriptural statements, which are the only proof concerning inconceivable subjects. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.27**

According to this, the śruti statements are beyond material logic. The Lord is thus endowed with senses and has no senses. *Na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate:* the Lord does not have senses or actions (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8)

Or though you bestow the śakti for the jīva’s senses you are without senses. Why? You depend on your svarūpa-śakti (*svarāṭ)*.

Because you excel all others, devatās like Indra (*animiṣāḥ*) and creators of universes like Brahmā *(viśva-srjaḥ*) carry *(vahanti*) on their exalted heads (*ut*) offerings (*balim*) to you (*tava*), along with māyā, their master (*ajayā*). She, your shadow śakti, takes the offerings to you, with your svarūpa-śakti in order to increase her own wealth. They consume *(samadanti*) the offerings made by humans.

An example is given. The rules of smaller kingdoms (*varṣa-bhujaḥ*) carry tribute in respect to your order (*vidadhati*).

*bhīṣāsmād vātaḥ pavate bhīsodeti sūryaḥ bhīṣāsmād agniś cendraś ca mṛtyur dhāvati pañcamaḥ*

**Out of fear of him the wind blows. Out of fear of him the sun rises. Out of fear of him fire, Indra and death, the fifth, run. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.3.3**

Or there is another meaning. “Give a reason why my senses like hands etc. arise from my svarūpa.” The devatās, the deities of the senses, offer tribute to you. As devatās, some are creators of universes (*viśva-sṛjaḥ*). Others cause objects to be revealed through the senses, since they are the shelters of the presiding deities of the senses. You are the shelter of all of them. Since this means that your senses are self manifested (not having devatās as controllers or creators) they must arise from your svarūpa.

“The great śakti Māyā can be the shelter of the senses and creators.” Aja also pays tribute. “Jīvas are the shelter of the presiding deities of the senses.” They are like regional controllers given power by the devatās who are given power by you, the master of the universe, for the senses, through which they can enjoy. The jīvas are not the shelter of the devatās. But you are their shelter since you give them power. The śrutis speak to the Lord.

## 51.

Therefore the Lord has no hands because he has hands different from material hands.

*tvak-śmaśru-roma-nakha-keśa-pinaddham antar*  
*māṁsāsthi-rakta-kṛmi-viṭ-kapha-pitta-vātam*  
*jīvac-chavaṁ bhajati kānta-matir vimūḍhā*  
*yā te padābja-makarandam ajighratī strī*

**A woman who fails to relish the fragrance of the honey of your lotus feet becomes totally befooled, and thus she accepts as her husband or lover a living corpse covered with skin, whiskers, nails, head-hair and body-hair and filled with flesh, bones, blood, parasites, feces, mucus, bile and air. SB 10.60.45**

Thus the Lord’s hair and limbs described in the scriptures are the blissful svarūpa of the Lord. Others do not have such a svarūpa. The difference is clear. Thus Brahmā gave a benediction to Hiraṇyakaśipu which prevented death by various beings but not the Lord’s limbs (SB 7.3.37). He was given the boon of not being killed by anything with or without prāṇa. The Lord’s nails (limb), which killed him arose from hands which have life, are themselves without life.

*divyo hy amūrtaḥ puruṣah sa-bāhyābhyantaro hy ajaḥ aprāṇo hy amanāḥ śubhro 'kṣarāt parataḥ paraḥ*

**The attractive person is without form, is inside and outside, is unborn, without prāṇas, without mind, pure, and superior to the akṣara (prakṛti). Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 2.1.2**

*asya mahato bhūtasya niḥśvasitam eta dyad ṛgveda*

**From the Lord’s breathing arose the Ṛg Veda. (*****Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad*****)**

*na yasya prākṛtā mūrtir medomajjāsthi-sambhavā \|*  
*na yogitvādīśvaratvāt satya-rūpo’cyuto vibhuḥ \|\|*

**The Lord does not have a material form made of bones, fat and marrow. It does not arise from yoga practice. Because he is the supreme Lord, his form is eternal, invincible and all-pervading. Varāha Purāṇa**

His non-material form is not produced by some great yoga practice *(yogitvāt*), by some desire. It is eternal since he is the Lord. The Lord has a body. He has knowledge, desire and action since the Lord is a doer, an agent, like a potter. That body is eternal, like the Lord. Since it is the sense organ of the Lord, it has knowledge like the Lord. Thus the Lord’s body and senses are different from the jīva’s.

The phrase *jīvac-chavam* is used in the verse. The body of the jīva is by nature a corpse since it is given life by the combination with consciousness (the ātmā). The Lord’s body however is always living since it is one unit of consciousness. Thus it is different from the jīva’s body. Because it is full of eternal bliss and consciousness it is worthy of worship. Rukmiṇī speaks to the Lord.

## 52.

When the various śrutis argue about whether the Lord has name and form, the following is concluded:

*astīti nāstīti ca vastu-niṣṭhayor*  
*eka-sthayor bhinna-viruddha-dharmaṇoḥ*  
*avekṣitaṁ kiñcana yoga-sāṅkhyayoḥ*  
*samaṁ paraṁ hy anukūlaṁ bṛhat tat*

**The same supreme Brahman without contradiction is perceived by yoga and jñāna with contrary qualities because the followers are fixed in a particular faith directed to the same object. SB 6.4.32**

*Yoga* refers to scriptures which advocate worship of a gross form (*asti*). When the name and form of the Lord are mentioned in scriptures, they meditate on all material names like pot and cloth and think of Pātala as the Lord’s feet from rash imagination of things seen. Sāṅkhya refers to jñāna scriptures which advocate no form (*nāsti)*. Since the scriptures forbid names and forms, they forbid all name and form for the Lord since they think of material names and forms. These two types of worship were previously described in the same chapter. *sa sarva-nāmā sa ca viśva-rūpaḥ*: the Lord is all names and all forms in the universe by his external energy. *yad yan niruktaṁ vacasā:* his form cannot be defined by words *(nirūpitam*). (SB 6.4.28-29)

Some are fixed in these two concepts (*vastuni niṣṭhāyoḥ*). This produces quarrel. The two parties have contrary qualities (*viruddha-dharmanoḥ*) because of different concepts (*bhinna*) of *asti* and *nāsti.*

“They are arguing about different objects.” No. The two have the same object (*eka-sthayoḥ*). In this quarrel, the object perceived in agreement (*samam*) should be very favorable for both. What is the agreement? It is different *(param*), since it does not have name and form and also has name and form. That object which simultaneously possesses and does not possess name and form is different.

This should be said. Scriptures should cancel each other by advocating name and form or forbidding name and form in one object. Based on the previous reasoning that the supreme Lord had name and form and did not have name and form, the solution is that the Lord has non-material name and form. This agrees with the conclusions of both types of scripture. They simply quarrel over their positions.

Dhruva has stated the position of no conflict:

*tiryaṅ-naga-dvija-sarīsṛpa-deva-daitya-*  
*martyādibhiḥ paricitaṁ sad-asad-viśeṣam*  
*rūpaṁ sthaviṣṭham aja te mahad-ādy-anekaṁ*  
*nātaḥ paraṁ parama vedmi na yatra vādaḥ*

**O unborn Lord! Previously I knew the variety of gross and subtle elements manifested as animals, plants, birds, snakes, devatās, demons, humans and total universal form composed of mahat-tattva and other elements. O Supreme! But I did not know what was superior to that material form, and did not know the form of Brahman in which sound does not exist. SB 4.9.13**

He mentions form and sound in relation to the Lord. *Bhūpa rūpam arūpaṁ ca paraṁ cāparam eva*: O king, the Lord has form and no form, has four arms and does not have four arms. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.7.47*) Param* means a form having four arms. This will be shown later. I did not know till this time (*tan na vedmi*).

## is it 52 ??

*yo 'nugrahārthaṁ bhajatāṁ pāda-mūlam*  
*anāma-rūpo bhagavān anantaḥ*  
*nāmāni rūpāṇi ca janma-karmabhir*  
*bheje sa mahyaṁ paramaḥ prasīdatu*

**May the unlimited Supreme Lord who, without material name and form, manifests names and forms with his birth and activities in this world to show mercy to those who worship his lotus feet, be pleased with me! SB 6.4.33**

He who does not have name and form manifests *(bheje*) names and forms along with birth and activities. Not to have name and form would be a fault. That is expressed by the word *ananta* (unlimited). If he does not have name and form, it would mean he does not have śaktis. The Pracetas say *na hy antas tvad-vibhūtīnāṁ so 'nanta iti gīyase*: since there is no end to your splendor (names and forms), you are called Ananta, the unlimited. (SB 6.30.31) The cause of his manifesting name and form is his śaktis. Bhagavān means possessing auspicious śaktis. The word *paramaḥ* excludes śaktis made of material māyā.  *Paramaḥ* means he in whom Lakṣmī (*mā*) exists as the para-śakti. If his śaktis were material, it would be a fault to his supreme position.

*tasmān na māyayā sarvaṁ sarvam aiśvarya-sambhavam \|*  
*amāyo hīśvaro yasmāt tasmāt taṁ paramaṁ viduḥ \|\|*

**Therefore everything connected with the Lord is not produced by māyā. Everything arises by his spiritual powers. Since the Lord is without māyā he is known as the supreme.**

“The proof of whether the Lord has names and forms or does not have names and forms is the existence of worshippers. Who are the worshippers?” I manifest my forms and names for giving mercy to those who worship my feet. I reveal these not to the followers of yoga or Sāṅkhya but to the devotee. *Bhaktir evainaṁ darśayati*: bhakti reveals the Lord. (Māṭhara-śruti) Thus it is correct to say that the two parties are only interested in quarrelling. Later it will be said:

*iti stutaḥ saṁstuvataḥ sa tasminn aghamarṣaṇe*  
*prādurāsīt kuru-śreṣṭha bhagavān bhakta-vatsalaḥ*

**O Mahārāja Parīkṣit! The Supreme Lord, affectionate to his devotees, on being praised by Dakṣa, appeared at that holy place known as Aghamarṣaṇa. SB 6.4.35**

Because he shows his form to persons who worship his feet, the form must have existed previously to that.

*caraṇaṁ pavitraṁ vitataṁ purāṇaṁ yena pūtas tarati duṣkṛtāni:*

**We become pufified and can cross saṁsāra by that pure, eternal foot which extends itself everywhere.** ***Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad***

*Bheje* is in the past tense to indicate that his limbs are eternal. Since the Lord is *ananta* (eternal and infinite), so are his names and forms. Thus the Lord is devoid of material names and forms. Dakṣa speaks to the Lord.

Bhagavān’s forms are the highest realization

## 53.

Since forms are eternal, since they are all-pervading, since they are the shelter of everything, since they completely different from subtle and gross material objects, since they have spiritual natures, since they are self-manifesting, since they are proved by the agreement of all scriptures, the forms are proved to be the highest tattva.

This is clear from the realization of the most learned Brahmā:

*rūpaṁ yad etad avabodha-rasodayena*  
*śaśvan-nivṛtta-tamasaḥ sad-anugrahāya*  
*ādau gṛhītam avatāra-śataika-bījaṁ*  
*yan-nābhi-padma-bhavanād aham āvirāsam*

**By your mercy to the devotees, by your cit-śakti, you have shown at the beginning this form which is eternally devoid of māyā, which is the seed of countless avatāras, and from whose navel appeared the lotus from which I have appeared.**

The commentary says, “The Lord may argue, ‘You do not directly know me. What you see is made of the guṇas. Brahman without guṇas is the truth.’ In two verses Brahmā describes the form of the Lord. That form belongs to you who exclude tamas at all times (*śaśvan-nivṛtta-tamasaḥ*) by the cit-śakti (*avabodha-rasodayena*). It is revealed (*gṛhītam*) by you at your will for giving mercy to the devotees (*sad-anugrahāya*). That form is the one root of hundreds of avatāras which are śuddha-sattva. To show that the Lord is source of all avatāras, Brahmā shows that the Lord is the seed of the guṇāvatāras: I have appeared from your navel.”

*nātaḥ paraṁ parama yad bhavataḥ svarūpam*  
*ānanda-mātram avikalpam aviddha-varcaḥ*  
*paśyāmi viśva-sṛjam ekam aviśvam ātman*  
*bhūtendriyātmakam adas ta upāśrito ’smi*

**O Supreme Lord! I do not see that your form which is without duality and only bliss is different from Brahman. O form of unrestricted manifestations! I, take shelter of this chief form which creates this universe but is different from the universe and the the cause of the jīvas’ senses.**

“O supreme lord with unrestricted manifestation (*parama aviddha-varcaḥ*)! I do not see your form which is undivided (*avikalpam*) and thus only bliss (*ānanda-mātram)* to be different (*param*) from that Brahman (*tat*). Because of that *(ataḥ*) I take shelter of this form (*adaḥ*) of yours (*te*). That is proper since that is the chief form *(ekam*) among all forms to be worshipped since this form creates the universe (*viśva-sṛjam*). But it is different from the universe (*aviśvam*). Moreover it is cause of the senses of the living entities.”

*tad vā idaṁ bhuvana-maṅgala maṅgalāya*  
*dhyāne sma no darśitaṁ ta upāsakānām*  
*tasmai namo bhagavate ’nuvidhema tubhyaṁ*  
*yo ’nādṛto naraka-bhāgbhir asat-prasaṅgaiḥ*

**O lord of auspiciousness for the whole world! This personal form is for the benefit of the world, and it was shown to me, your worshipper, by meditation. Therefore I offer respects to that personal form. Let me only serve you, who are not respected by those who will go to hell, proclaiming that your personal form is false. SB 3.9.2-4**

“The Lord may argue that this form is Brahman covered with upādhis and appears after Brahman. Brahmā answers with this verse. O Lord auspicious for the universe! This form is ultimate, because this form has been shown by you *(te*) in my meditation for the auspiciousness of your worshippers. It is not proper to show something with upādhis to us, whose hearts are absorbed in your personal form (*avyakta-vartmani*). Therefore we offer respects to you repeatedly (*anuvidhema).* ‘But some people do not respect me.’ You are not respected by persons fixed in atheistic arguments (*asat-prasaṅgaiḥ*).”

It is not possible to image another meaning since Brahmā is the guru for all learned persons. The phrase *avyakta-vartma* used by Śrīdhara Svāmī appeared previously:

*sa karma-bījaṁ rajasoparaktaḥ*  
*prajāḥ sisṛkṣann iyad eva dṛṣṭvā*  
*astaud visargābhimukhas tam īḍyam*  
*avyakta-vartmany abhiveśitātmā*

**Influenced by rajas and intent on creating, desiring to create the offspring, Brahmā glanced at the lotus and the elements as the ingredients of creation. Concentrating his mind on the Lord, (*****avyakta-vartamani*****) he began to praise the Lord, worthy of worship. SB 3.8.33**

The atheists do not respect the Lord with form. They go to hell since the Lord with form has been established as supreme Brahman. Those who do not respect the Lord with his form, having ideas contrary to the learned with realization, are considered atheists. Thus Śrīdhara Svāmī calls them *nirīśvara.* The next verse shows why they are condemned.

*ye tu tvadīya-caraṇāmbuja-kośa-gandhaṁ*  
*jighranti karṇa-vivaraiḥ śruti-vāta-nītam*  
*bhaktyā gṛhīta-caraṇaḥ parayā ca teṣāṁ*  
*nāpaiṣi nātha hṛdayāmburuhāt sva-puṁsām*

**O master! You do not give up the lotus hearts of your devotees who smell through their ears the fragrance of your lotus bud feet brought by the wind of the Vedas, and take your lotus feet as the greatest treasure through prema-bhakti. SB 3.9.5**

The word *tu* (but) contrasts the devotees, fixed on the personal form with the persons averse to the personal form of the Lord mentioned in the previous verse. The devotees smell the fragrance which is proved by the Vedas (*śruti-vāta-nītam*). The devotees accept your feet as the greatest treasure with bhakti. This indicated the excellence of the Lord’s form through realization. Brahmā speaks to Nārāyaṇa.

## 54.

The body of Ṛṣabha, perceived as an āveśāvatāra, Rūpa Gosvāmī in Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta does not mention Ṛṣabha as a saktyaveśa form. 12 is also accepted as spiritual.

*idaṁ śarīraṁ mama durvibhāvyaṁ*  
*sattvaṁ hi me hṛdayaṁ yatra dharmaḥ*  
*pṛṣṭhe kṛto me yad adharma ārād*  
*ato hi mām ṛṣabhaṁ prāhur āryāḥ*

**My body is difficult to understand since it is spiritual. Where there is bhakti*****,*** **there my mind resides. Whatever is opposite to bhakti is behind me, far away. Thus the respectable people call me the best, or Ṛṣabha. SB 5.5.19**

This real nature of body of mine in human form is certainly *(hi)* difficult to understand. My mind resides where there are qualities of devotion since I put the opposite qualities behind me, since I am averse to lack of devotion. The disappearance of the speaker Rṣabhadeva is described according to material perception, through a disappearance pastime, to show how an ātmārāma passes away. (But the above verse should be taken into account.) *Yogināṁ sāmparāya-vidhim anuśikṣayan sva-kalevaraṁ jihāsuḥ:* teaching the *yogīs* how to give the material body, he desired to give an appearance of giving up his body (or desired to give up the material world). (SB 5.6.6) Here the word *kalevaram* means the material world, since that meaning is well known in the scriptures which teach worship.

*atha samīra-vega-vidhūta-veṇu-vikarṣaṇa-jātogra-dāvānalas tad vanam ālelihānaḥ saha tena dadāha.*

**An intense forest fire created by the rubbing of bamboos agitated by the force of the wind, devouring everything, burned up the forest along with Ṛṣabhadeva. SB 5.6.8**

(Since his body cannot be destroyed) the real meaning is “The fire burned up the forest with the assistance of Ṛṣabhadeva.” First preference is given to the meaning of *saha* (with) which emphasizes the agent. The fire burned up the gross bodies of the jīvas living in the forest. Ṛṣabha however had a spiritual body since he gave liberation to others. His disappearance was like that of the followers of Rāma:

*sa yaiḥ spṛṣṭo 'bhidṛṣṭo vā saṁviṣṭo 'nugato 'pi vā*  
*kosalās te yayuḥ sthāna yatra gacchanti yoginaḥ*

**The inhabitants of Kosala, who touched, saw, sat together with Rāma in friendship and followed Rāma, went to Vaikuṇṭha where the devotees go. SB 9.11.22**

Thus his disappearance was similar to other entities disappearing in fire. Ṛṣabhadeva’s appearance is described in the third chapter: he did not take birth. Ṛṣabhadeva speaks to his sons.

## 55.

If Ṛṣabhadeva’s body is spiritual, what can one say of the body of Svayam Bhagavān?

*muni-gaṇa-nṛpa-varya-saṅkule ’ntaḥ-*  
*sadasi yudhiṣṭhira-rājasūya eṣām \|*  
*arhaṇam upapeda īkṣaṇīyo*  
*mama dṛśi-gocara eṣa āvir ātmā \|\|*

**The lord of my life, worthy of being seen, who received the worship of all the greatest sages and kings in the assembly during the rājasūya sacrifice of Yudhiṣṭhira, has become visible to my eyes. SB 1.9.41**

The commentary says, “The soul of the universe (*eṣaḥ ātmā*), coming in the path of my vision, manifested himself. What good fortune!” Bhīṣma speaks to the Lord.

## 56.

Also Devakī says:

*rūpaṁ yat tat prāhur avyaktam ādyaṁ*  
*brahma jyotir nirguṇaṁ nirvikāram*  
*sattā-mātraṁ nirviśeṣaṁ nirīhaṁ*  
*sa tvaṁ sākṣād viṣṇur adhyātma-dīpaḥ*

**That form which they say is invisible, the cause of all causes, the greatest, self-manifesting, without qualities, without change, pure existence, without variety and without action, is directly you, the Supreme Lord, who manifests our intelligence. SB 10.3.24**

The Vedas speak about the nature of this entity *(yat tat rūpam*). What is that entity? He is avyakta etc. That entity with amazing qualities is you, Viṣṇu, visible to my eyes *(sākṣāt*).

Veda-vyāsa says to the Lord:

*tvām ahaṁ draṣṭum icchāmi cakṣurbhyāṁ madhusūdana \|*  
*yat tat satyaṁ paraṁ brahma jagad-yoniṁ jagat-patim \|*  
*vadanti veda-śirasaś cākṣuṣaṁ nātha me’stu tad \|\|*

**O Madhusūdana! I desire to see you with my two eyes. O Lord! May he whom Upaniṣads describe as the eternal existence, highest Brahman, the source of the universe, and the lord of the universe, be visible to my eyes. Padma Purāṇa 4.73.12-3**

The reason the Lord became visible to Devakī is stated. He is *adhyātma-dīpah*: he manifests himself, at the same time manifesting the jīva along with its causes and effects. Thus there is no need to fear for your safety. (You have a form but you are still the Lord.) Śrīdhara Svāmī also emphasizes the form of the Lord in this verse. Fearing for the safety of the Lord without form would be meaningless. Devakī speaks to the Lord.

## 57.

The aṁśas of the Lord share the same quality:

*satya-jñānānantānanda- mātraika-rasa-mūrtayaḥ*  
*aspṛṣṭa-bhūri-māhātmyā api hy upaniṣad-dṛśām*

**The forms all had eternal, unlimited forms, full of knowledge and bliss, which were one form. Their great glory could not even to be touched by the ātmā-jñānīs. SB 10.13.54**

The commentary says, “All the forms are described with form. They were forms of truth and knowledge, forms without limit, forms of bliss. They were without distinction from each other *(eka-mātra*). They were always of one form (*eka-rasaḥ*). Or they were the form of Brahman *(mūrtayaḥ*) with truth, knowledge, infinity and bliss as one unit. Their glory could not be touched by those who had eyes of ātmā-jñāna (*upaniṣad-dṛśām*). These forms became visible.”

The word *mātra* (only) indicates their color and other attributes had the spiritual nature of the Lord’s svarūpa. The word *mūrtayaḥ* (forms) does not refer to pure ātmās according to Śrīdhara Svāmī and Śukadeva since it is difficult to derive that meaning by using indirect interpretation (lakṣaṇā). As well the words *aspṛsṭa, bhūri-māhātmyāḥ, api* and *upaniṣad-ḍrṣām* taken individually and together would be contradicted by taking *mūrtayaḥ* to mean pure ātmās. That meaning would also contradict the context of the verse.

*taṁ tv āgataṁ pratihṛtaupayikaṁ sva-pumbhis*  
*te ’cakṣatākṣa-viṣayaṁ sva-samādhi-bhāgyam*

**The sages saw the Lord surrounded by devotees carrying objects to offer him. He was previously the object of their meditation, but now appeared directly before their eyes. SB 3.15.38**

That meaning would be contrary to Śukadeva’s heart:

*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo*  
*'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi*

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who destroys of all sin, and who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions, became attracted to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and mercifully spread the Bhāgavatam which describes Kṛṣṇa and reveals the rasa of his pastimes. SB 12.12.69**

*viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā*  
*samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam*  
*sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*  
*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi*

**Let me surrender to you, Bhagavān. You are the condensed form of pure knowledge and by you are bliss itself you have forms with many qualities. Your desires are always fulfilled. Because your svarūpa-śakti you are never touched by the material guṇas. SB 10.37.22**

*svacchandopātta-dehāya viśuddha-jñāna-mūrtaye*  
*sarvasmai sarva-bījāya sarva-bhūtātmane namaḥ*

**Unto him who assumes bodies according to the desires of his devotees, unto him whose form is pure consciousness, unto him who is the universe, who is Mahā-viṣṇu and the antaryāmī, I offer my obeisances. SB 10.27.11**

*tasmād idaṁ jagad aśeṣam asat-svarūpaṁ*  
*svapnābham asta-dhiṣaṇaṁ puru-duḥkha-duḥkham*  
*tvayy eva nitya-sukha-bodha-tanāv anante*  
*māyāta udyad api yat sad ivāvabhāti*

**Therefore this entire universe, which, like a dream, is by nature unreal, nevertheless appears real, and thus it covers one’s consciousness and assails one with repeated miseries. This universe appears real because it is manifested by the potency of illusion emanating from you, whose unlimited form is full of eternal happiness and knowledge. SB 10.14.22**

These verses should not be made useless by lakṣaṇā.

*ānanda-mūrtim upaguhya dṛśātma-labdhaṁ*  
*hṛṣyat-tvaco jahur anantam arindamādhim*

**Taking him into their hearts through their eyes, they embraced him, the form of all bliss, and as their bodily hairs stood on end, O subduer of enemies, they forgot the unlimited distress caused by his absence. SB 10.41.28**

*sānaṅga-tapta-kucayor urasas tathākṣṇor*  
*jighranty ananta-caraṇena rujo mṛjantī*  
*dorbhyāṁ stanāntara-gataṁ parirabhya kāntam*  
*ānanda-mūrtim ajahād ati-dīrgha-tāpam*

**Simply by smelling the fragrance of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, Trivakrā cleansed away the burning desire Cupid had aroused in her breasts, chest and eyes. With her two arms she embraced between her breasts her lover, Kṛṣṇa, the personification of bliss, and thus she gave up her long-standing distress. SB 10.48.7**

Since she embraced that form, other meanings are refuted. (The Lord could not be without form.)

*sarve nityāḥ śāśvatāś ca dehās tasya parātmanaḥ \|*  
*heyopādeya-rahitā naiva prakṛtijāḥ kvacit \|\|*  
*paramānanda-sandohā jñāna-mātrāś ca sarvataḥ \|*  
*deha-dehi-bhidā cātra neśvare vidyate kvacit \|\|*

**The bodies of the Lord are all eternal, unchanging, and devoid of faults. They are never the product of matter. They are completely filled with the highest bliss and knowledge. There is no distinction of body and soul in the Lord. Mahā-varāha Purāṇa**

Śukadeva speaks.

## 58.

That is indicated in the following:

*kṛṣṇam enam avehi tvam ātmānam akhilātmanām*  
*jagad-dhitāya so ’py atra dehīvābhāti māyayā*

**You should know Kṛṣṇa to be the soul of all living entities. For the benefit of the whole universe, out of his causeless mercy he plays as an ordinary human being*****.*** **SB 10.14.55**

The form of Kṛṣṇa was previously described:

*naumīḍya te ’bhra-vapuṣe taḍid-ambarāya*  
*guñjāvataṁsa-paripiccha-lasan-mukhāya*  
*vanya-sraje kavala-vetra-viṣāṇa-veṇu-*  
*lakṣma-śriye mṛdu-pade paśupāṅgajāya*

**O worshipable Lord! I offer my obeisances to you to attain you, Nanda’s son, having a body the complexion of a rain cloud, wearing cloth flashing like lightning, with face shining among peacock feathers and guñja berry decorations, with colorful forest garland, and possessing soft feet, his beauty enhanced by yogurt rice, stick, horn and flute. SB 10.14.1**

O Parīkṣit! You should know Kṛṣṇa. Realize that form by knowledge attained by my mercy, not from logicians. By his mercy (*māyayā*) he plays (*ābhāti)* like a jīva (*dehī iva*) for attracting the hearts of his all people to himself (*jagad-dhitāya*). The word *iva* (like) indicates that Kṛṣṇa is not a jīva who enters a body separate from himself.

Dhruva speaks of the form of the Lord as the highest attainment.

*satyāśiṣo hi bhagavaṁs tava pāda-padmam*  
*āśīs tathānubhajataḥ puruṣārtha-mūrteḥ*  
*apy evam arya bhagavān paripāti dīnān*  
*vāśreva vatsakam anugraha-kātaro 'smān*

**O Lord! You are the very form of all human goals, the highest bliss, and are obtainable by those who worship you continually without material desire. Your lotus feet are the highest attainable blessing, surpassing material blessings like acquisition of a kingdom. SB 4.9.17**

The commentary says, “O Bhagavān! The lotus feet of you whose form is the highest bliss (*puruṣārtha-mūrteh*) give real blessing compared to attaining kingdoms (*satyāśiṣaḥ*). The attainment of the highest goal (*āśīḥ*) is certain (*hi*). Who attains you by what method? You are available for those who worship you with no desires (*anubhajataḥ).”*

Śukadeva speaks.

## 59.

The following shows that the form of the Lord is called *śabda-brahman.*

*tāvat prasanno bhagavān puṣkarākṣaḥ kṛte yuge*  
*darśayām āsa taṁ kṣattaḥ śābdaṁ brahma dadhad vapuḥ*

**O Vidura! The lotus eyed Lord, having a body made of the Vedas (*****śābdaṁ brahma*****), pleased with Kardama, showed him his form in Satya-yuga. SB 3.21.8**

The Lord revealed (*dadhat*) his body. The Lord named Śukla existed in Satya-yuga. The Lord shows that highest tattva (Brahman) explained as *śabda.* Maitreya speaks.

## 60.

It has been proved that the Lord is not material and has a form. It is not possible to prove this by logic through observed objects like pots since they are destroyed by time. After establishing the Lord’s existence devoid of six transformations, it is established that his svarūpa is complete.

*ekas tvam ātmā puruṣaḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*satyaḥ svayaṁ-jyotir ananta ādyaḥ*  
*nityo ’kṣaro ’jasra-sukho nirañjanaḥ*  
*pūrṇādvayo mukta upādhito ’mṛtaḥ*

**You are the one supreme soul, the supreme person, the oldest, the truth, self-manifested, unlimited, the beginning, eternal, indestructible, unlimited happiness, pure, full, without a second, free of upādhis and deathless. SB 10.14.23**

This form, having a complexion of a rain cloud, was already raised in the first verse. That form is one (*ekaḥ*), the supreme shelter (*ātmā*) of all others. It was already said *eko’si prathamam:*  you are the first, the only one. (SB 10.18) It will also be said *kṛṣṇam enam avehi tvam ātmānam akhilātmanām:* you should understand this Kṛṣṇa, the shelter of all souls. (SB 10.14.55) Since you are *ātmā* you are existence (*satyaḥ*).
