In certain scriptures or to some persons, not able to accept the development of qualities from the Lord’s svarūpa, I am equated with the impersonal. Or, now in the world, I exist as unqualified consciousness alone but in Vaikuṇṭha exist as the Lord with qualities. Scripture describes both conditions. This same knowledge of the Lord is also taught by the Lord in *brahmaṇo hi pratiṣṭhāham:* I am the basis of Brahman. (BG 14.27) Therefore it is called the most secret knowledge.

“After creation of the world you cannot be perceived.” After the creation (paścāt) I exist in the form of Bhagavān in Vaikuṇṭha and and as antaryāmī in the world.

*sthity-udbhava-pralaya-hetur ahetur asya*  
*yat svapna-jāgara-suṣuptiṣu sad bahiś ca*  
*dehendriyāsu-hṛdayāni caranti yena*  
*sañjīvitāni tad avehi paraṁ narendra*

**One should know Nārāyaṇa who is the cause of creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe and is without cause, who remains present during the conditions of dream, waking and deep sleep, and beyond those states as well, and who makes the body, senses, life airs and mind move and come to life. SB 11.3.35**

The above verse is same knowledge that the Lord teaches in the catuḥślokī.

“Pots and cloth seen everywhere are not your forms. Thus this means that you are not complete.” This universe (*yad etat)* is me alone. Since nothing exists except me, it is also me.

*so ’yaṁ te ’bhihitas tāta bhagavān viśva-bhāvanaḥ \|*  
*samāsena harer nānyad anyasmāt sad-asac ca yat \|\|*

**Bhagavān, maintainer of the universe, has thus been described in summary. The universe and jīvas are none other than the Lord, but arise from the Lord who is different from them. SB 2.7.50**

In this above verse the Lord teaches the same knowledge of the Lord. What exists at destruction is also me alone. The same knowledge is taught in the following:

*bhagavān eka āsedam agra ātmātmanāṁ vibhuḥ*  
*ātmecchānugatāv ātmā nānā-maty-upalakṣaṇaḥ*

**Bhagavān, who exists in the form of Paramātmā and Brahman according to the viewpoint, alone existed before the creation of the universe, when the desire to create bodies of the jīvas was absent. SB 3.5.23**

Thus what was promised “to be revealed by mercy” was to show that the Lord’s measurement (yāv*at aham)* is beyond all time and place. *Nānyad yat sad-asat param* indicating *brahmaṇo hi pratiṣṭhāham* explains *yathā bhāvam*. By indicating the Lord’s form with limbs in order to show that the Lord has extraordinary, unlimited forms, *yad-rūpam* is explained. By indicating that the Lord is the shelter of everything, to show his extraordinary, unlimited qualities, *yad-guṇam* is explained. By indicating that the Lord is the shelter of various actions such as creation, maintenance and destruction, to show his extraordinary, unlimited acitons, *yat-karmakaḥ* is explained.

## 105.

In order to show the Lord’s endowment of form and qualities, māyā’s qualities are explained as a contrast.

*ṛte’rthaṁ yat pratīyeta na pratīyeta cātmani \|*  
*tad vidyād ātmano māyāṁ yathābhāso yathā tamaḥ \|\|*

**One should understand my māyā to be anything other than me which is perceived, and which cannot be perceived without me. It is like reflected light or darkness. SB 2.9.33**

This was previously explained in Anuccheda 16. This is the meaning in summary. What is perceived elsewhere, other than seeing me, who produces the highest puruṣārtha (*artham ṛte*), and what cannot be perceived without me, know that to be the māya of me, the supreme Lord. An example is given. It is like rays of a refleciton. It is like darkness. Māyā’s resemblance to a reflection is clear. Because of seeing light one can perceive darkness: without the eye which is a form of light, one cannot see. (Similarly, without the Lord, objects do not exist.) One should know this (*vidyāt*). This is an instruction for others. You however, by śakti given by me, can directly realize it. And surpassing the vision of māyā you should realize me with form. This is an indication of realization by describing the opposite. One cannot realize the real svarūpa described in scripture by being absorbed in the effects of māyā. Thus for realization you must reject māyā. In the absense of māyā you realize prema.

## 106.

The secret of prema is described:

*yathā mahānti bhūtāni bhūteṣūccāvaceṣv anu \|*  
*praviṣṭāny apraviṣṭāni tathā teṣu na teṣv aham \|\|*

**Just as the elements enter into all beings and also remain separate, I enter into all beings and remain separate when I perform my pastimes. In pastimes related to the material world I remain detached and in pastimes related to devotees, I am attached. SB 2.9.35**

Just as the great elements are situated outside all beings and also are situated in all beings, so I, being situated in Vaikuṇṭha beyond the material world, do not enter into the world’s guṇas but still am situated in the hearts of surrendered beings. The elements do not enter all beings, but enter by their portions into all beings. However, the Lord enters his devotees by different manifestations. That is the difference between the Lord and the elements. The example is simply to show entereing and not entering. Prema bhakti of the devotees which controls the Lord is the secret.

*ānanda-cinmaya-rasa-pratibhāvitābhis*  
*tābhir ya eva nija-rūpatayā kalābhiḥ \|*  
*goloka eva nivasaty akhilātma-bhūto*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who, though regarding all the inhabitants as his very self, resides in Goloka exclusively with the young gopīs, who are embodiments of madhura-rasa. He accepts them as his wives, while they respond to his conjugal affection reciprocally. Brahma-saṁhitā 5.39**

*premāñjana-cchurita-bhakti-vilocanena*  
*santaḥ sadaiva hṛdayeṣu vilokayanti \|*  
*yaṁ śyāmasundaram acintya-guṇa-svarūpaṁ*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda of beautiful dark complexion, filled with inconceivable qualities, whom the devotees constantly see in their mind during samādhi permeated with prema. Brahma-saṁhitā 5.38**

The devotees see the Lord’s form with inconceivable qualities by eyes of bhakti which shine brightly (*cchurita*) with the ointment of prema.

*samo ’haṁ sarva-bhūteṣu na me dveṣyo ’sti na priyaḥ \|*  
*ye bhajanti tu māṁ bhaktyā mayi te teṣu cāpy aham \|\|*

**I am equal to all living beings. I do not hate anyone nor do I favor anyone. But those who worship me with devotion are in me, and I am in them. BG 9.29**

There is another meaning. Just as the elements appear situated outside all beings but also inside, I appear to the devotees inside by the mind and outside by the senses of the devotee. This self-manifesting prema filled with bliss, caused by the selfless, fully surrendered conduct of my devotees is my secret. Brahmā says:

*na bhāratī me ’ṅga mṛṣopalakṣyate*  
*na vai kvacin me manaso mṛṣā gatiḥ \|*  
*na me hṛṣīkāṇi patanty asat-pathe*  
*yan me hṛdautkaṇṭhyavatā dhṛto hariḥ \|\|*

**O Nārada! My words are never false. The working of the mind is never false. My senses do not fall onto the wrong path. This is because I hold the Lord in my heart, filled great zeal. SB 2.6.34**

Though in this other explanation the meaning is concealed, even then the conclusion is the same, since it fulfills the four promises and does not stray from them. However the phrase *na teṣu* (not in them) is extra and has no meaning since the meaning is fulfilled by the verbs entering and not entering.

Rahasya or secret means something extremely rare which is covered by other ordinary objects in order to hide it from view of indifferent or hostile persons. For instance a cintāmaṇi gem is hidden in a box. The Lord says:

*vedā brahmātma-viṣayās tri-kāṇḍa-viṣayā ime*  
*parokṣa-vādā ṛṣayaḥ parokṣaṁ mama ca priyam*

**The Vedas, divided into three divisions, ultimately reveal worship of the Lord. The Vedic sages and mantras, however, state this indirectly, since they know that I prefer this indirect method. SB 11.21.35**

Thus a valuable, rare object which should not be given everywhere is hidden or made parokṣa. *Muktiṁ dadāti karhicit sma na bhakti-yogam*: the Lord gives liberation but rarely prema-bhakti. (SB 5.6.18)

*idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma yan me bhagavatoditam \|*  
*saṅgraho ’yaṁ vibhūtīnāṁ tvam etad vipulī kuru \|\|*

**This work called Bhāgavatam, spoken to me by the Lord, is a collection of the Lord’s vibhūtis. You should distribute it everywhere.**

*yathā harau bhagavati nṛṇāṁ bhaktir bhaviṣyati \|*  
*sarvātmany akhilādhāre varṇaya \|\|*

**After making a saṅkalpa, you should speak the Bhāgavatam in such a way that devotion to Hari, attractor of the mind, Bhagavān, the blissful object of worship, the essence of all worship and the fulfiller of all desires, will appear in the people of Kali-yuga. SB 2.7.51-52**

Therefore Śrīdhara Svāmī has correctly explained bhakti as rahasya.

## 107.

How does this secret arise? The Lord teaches sādhana which is called an aṅga, as the fourth item in the list.

*etāvad eva jijñāsyaṁ tattva-jijñāsunātmanaḥ \|*  
*anvaya-vyatirekābhyāṁ yat syāt sarvatra sarvadā \|\|*

**The person desiring to know the best sādhana and goal of sādhana must learn by surrender to guru about this truth which is determined as the best by performance at all times and all places with rules and prohibitions. SB 2.9.35**

This should be learned from a guru *(jijñāsyam*) by the person with a desire to experience the secret (*tattva-jijñāsunā*) about me (*ātmanaḥ*)—prema. What is it? It is that which should always be undertaken in all places with rules and prohibitions (*anvaya-vyatirekābhyām*).

*na hy ato ’nyaḥ śivaḥ panthā viśataḥ saṁsṛtāv iha \|*  
*vāsudeve bhagavati bhakti-yogo yato bhavet \|\|*

**There is no other auspicious path for those suffering in the material world, because this path produces prema for Bhagavān Vāsudeva. SB 2.2.33**

This passage begins with what should be rejected (*vyatirekha*), followed by the positive rule:

*tasmāt sarvātmanā rājan hariḥ sarvatra sarvadā \|*  
*śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca smartavyo bhagavān nṛṇām \|\|*

**O King! Therefore, at all times and all places without restriction men should hear about, glorify and remember the Supreme Lord with full concentration of mind. SB 2.2.36**

This concluding passage shows the positive rule *(anvaya*). It should be done at all times everywhere. Thus in the catuḥślokī through teachings about knowledge of the Lord, realization, prema and bhakti, Bhagavān is taught.

The word Bhagavān is found here:

*tasmai sva-lokaṁ bhagavān sabhājitaḥ*  
*sandarśayām āsa paraṁ na yat-param \|*  
*vyapeta-saṅkleśa-vimoha-sādhvasaṁ*  
*sva-dṛṣṭavadbhir vibudhair abhiṣṭutam \|\|*

**Bhagavān, gratified by his worship, showed Brahmā Mahā-vaikuṇṭha, to which nothing is superior, which is free of suffering, confusion and fear of offenses and which is praised by the devatās who constantly see the self. SB 2.9.9**

*dadarśa tatrākhila-sātvatāṁ patiṁ*  
*śriyaḥ patiṁ yajña-patiṁ jagat-patim \|*  
*sunanda-nanda-prabalārhaṇādibhiḥ*  
*sva-pārṣadāgraiḥ parisevitaṁ vibhum \|\|*

**Brahmā saw the Lord, the Lord of all the devotees, the Lord of Lakṣmī, the Lord of sacrifice and the Lord of the universe, served by his chief followers such as Sunanda, Nanda, Prabala and Arhaṇa. SB 2.9.15**

The speaker is clearly said to be Kṛṣṇa, in accordance with śrutis and tāpanīs. The speaker is not Nārāyaṇa, his aṁśa, situated on the Garbhodaka Ocean. Therefore (because Kṛṣṇa spoke it) the Mahā-purāṇa is called Śrīmad-bhāgavatam. It is said:

*kasmai yena vibhāsito 'yam atulo jñāna-pradīpaḥ purā*  
*tad-rūpeṇa ca nāradāya munaye kṛṣṇāya tad-rūpiṇā*  
*yogīndrāya tad-ātmanātha bhagavad-rātāya kāruṇyatas*  
*tac chuddhaṁ vimalaṁ viśokam amṛtaṁ satyaṁ paraṁ dhīmahi*

**I meditate upon that pure, spotless, supreme form, who is free from suffering and death and who previously revealed this incomparable lamp of knowledge to Brahmā. Brahmā then spoke it to the sage Nārada, who narrated it to Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana Vyāsa. Śrīla Vyāsa revealed this Bhāgavatam to the greatest of sages, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, and Śukadeva mercifully spoke it to Mahārāja Parīkṣit. SB 12.13.19**

Here the word *param* means the speaker Bhagavān (Kṛṣṇa.) The word *parasya* distinguishes him from Viṣṇu in the following:

*ādyo ’vatāraḥ puruṣaḥ parasya*  
*kālaḥ svabhāvaḥ sad-asan manaś ca \|*  
*dravyaṁ vikāro guṇa indriyāṇi*  
*virāṭ svarāṭ sthāsnu cariṣṇu bhūmnaḥ \|\|*

**Mahā-viṣṇu, an expansion of the supreme Lord (Kṛṣṇa), is time, svabhāva, effect and cause, mahat-tattva, the five gross elements, false ego, the three guṇas, the senses, the universal form, the totality of jīvas, the individual jīvas as non-moving and moving beings. All of these are related to the Supreme Lord. SB 2.6.42**

The word Bhagavān is used to refer to the speaker of Bhāgavatam in the following:

*idaṁ bhagavatā pūrvaṁ brahmaṇe nābhi-paṅkaje*  
*sthitāya bhava-bhītāya kāruṇyāt samprakāśitam*

**Bhagavān first revealed the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam to Brahmā. At the time, Brahmā, frightened by material existence, was sitting on the lotus flower that had grown from the Lord's navel. SB 12.13.10**

Bhagavān, after showing Mahā-vaikuṇṭha, showed the Purāṇa to Brahmā situated on the lotus. This follows the narration in the Second Canto.

The Lord speaks to Brahmā.

## 108.

Therefore the conclusion of all scripture is Bhagavān.

*sarvaiś ca vedaiḥ paramo hi devo jijñāsyo nānyo vedaiḥ prasidhyet*  
*tasmād enaṁ sarva-vedān adhītya vicārya ca jñātum icchen mumukṣuḥ*

**By all the Vedas one should learn about the supreme Lord. No other person is proved by the Vedas. Having studied about him from the Vedas, reflected on him, the person desiring libration should desire to realize him. Catur-veda-śikhā**

*yaṁ sarva-devā ānamanti mumukṣavo brahmavādinaś ca*

**All the devatās, persons desiring liberation, and proponents of Brahman respect that Lord. Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

*sarve vedā yat padam āmananti tapāmsi sarvāṇi ca yad vadanti*

**All the Vedas worship his feet. All austerities speak of him. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.3.15**

*na vedavin manute taṁ bṛhantaṁ sarvānubhūtam ātmānaṁ sāmparāye*  
*taṁ tv aupaniṣadaṁ puruṣaṁ pṛcchāmi*

**Knowers of the Veda at the time of death do not know that great Lord perceived in all beings.**

I inquire about the Lord who is known in the Upaniṣads. *Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad* 3.9.26

*vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo*  
*vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham*

**I alone am known by all the Vedas. I am the maker of the Vedānta, the conclusion of the Vedas, since I alone know the Vedas. BG 15.15**

*siddhānte punar eka eva bhagavān viṣṇuḥ samastāgama*  
*-vyāpāreṣu vivecana-vyatikaraṁ nīteṣu niścīyate*

**The one lord Viṣṇu is ascertained in all philosophy, all scriptures and all conclusions of discussions. Padma Purāṇa**

*sarva-nāmābhidheyaś ca sarva-vedeḍitaś ca sa*

**He is called by all names and praised by all the Vedas. Skanda Purāṇa**

*natāḥ sma sarva-vacasāṁ pratiṣṭhā yatra śāśvatī*

**We offer respects to the Lord who is the eternal foundation of all words. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

*sarva-vedān setihāsān sa-purāṇān sa-yuktikān \|*  
*sa-pañcarātrān vijñāya viṣṇur jñeyo na cānyathā \|\|*

**Knowing all the Vedas, itihāsas, Purāṇas, all logic, and Pañcarātra scriptures, Viṣṇu alone is to be known. Brahma-tarka**

Thus all the Vedas conclude that Bhagavān is supreme. The Lord himself says:

*māṁ vidhatte ’bhidhatte māṁ vikalpyāpohyate tv aham*  
*etāvān sarva-vedārthaḥ śabda āsthāya māṁ bhidām*

**The Vedas indicate me as sacrifice and the devatās. I am the meaning of all the Vedas. What is produced and destroyed in the worlds is me alone. SB 11.21.43**

Śrutis establish (*dhatte)* me as the form of sacrifice and as the form of all the devatās (*abhidhatte*).

*tasmād vā etasmād ātmano ākāśaḥ saṁbhūtaḥ*

**From prakṛti arose ether. Taittirīya Upaniṣad**

What is produced in the world (ether etc.) and destroyed (*vikalpya apohyate*) is me. It is not separate from me since everything is part of me.

The Lord speaks.

How the scriptures can indicate Bhagavān beyond words

## 109.

It has been shown that Bhagavān is the meaning of all the Vedas. King Parīkṣit asks:

*brahman brahmaṇy anirdeśye nirguṇe guṇa-vṛttayaḥ*  
*kathaṁ caranti śrutayaḥ sākṣāt sad-asataḥ pare*

**O brāhmaṇa, how can the Vedas directly describe the Supreme Absolute Truth, who cannot be described in words? The Vedas are limited to describing the qualities of material nature, but the Supreme is devoid of these qualities, being transcendental to all material manifestations and their causes. SB 10.87.1**

The meaning is this. It is seen that the śrutis have the nature of the material guṇas (*guṇa-vṛttayaḥ)* since they are made of words. But Brahman is without material guṇas, beyond the guṇas (*nirguṇe*). Thus it cannot be indicated by words (*anirdeśye)*. It cannot be described because it is not the shelter of the actions, qualities and varieties arising as the effect of the guṇas and because Brahman cannot be known. It is beyond (*pare*) effect and cause (*sad-asataḥ*). It does not have a relationship with cause and effect.

The literal meaning of a word (mukhya-vṛtti) is produced when the word refers clearly to a particular object. An example of a secondary meaning (gauṇa-vṛtti) of word is the word “lion” in the sentence “Devattatta is a lion.” In that sentence lion means he is endowed with bravery like a lion. In the sentence “There is a village on the Gaṅgā” the phrase “on the Gaṅgā” is figurative (lakṣaṇā), since a village cannot be on the Gaṅgā. The village is on the bank of the Gaṅgā. How can the śrutis use these functions of words (literal, secondary or figurative) for Brahman, which cannot be the object of direct, secondary or figurative reference?

The śrutis, being the only means of teaching about Brahman, must be said to have the ability to express Brahman.

*śāstra-yonitvāt*

**The Lord cannot be known by reasoning because the source of understanding the Lord is the Upaniṣads. Brahma-sūtra 1.1.3**

They must do so by the direct meaning (*mukhya-vṛtti*) since the śrutis are self-evident (*svataḥ pramāṇa*). By what method do the śrutis then express Brahman, by direct expression *(mukhya-vṛtti*)? Please explain the method by your mercy. Otherwise one is forced to conclude that Brahman is beyond the scope of the śrutis because Brahman is not a real object and anything unreal is not suitable for expression in words. What to speak of Bhagavān, a manifestation superior to Brahman? How can Bhagavān be expressed in words? How did the Lord teach his two devotees (Śrutadeva and Bahulāśva, SB 10.86.59) the path of the Vedas, the self-evident proof, non-different from you, and the path dedicated to Bhagavān? It must be said that the direct meaing is the method in maintaining the sanctity of self-evident proof since figurative meaning (*lakṣaṇā*) is the source of interpretation. If *lakṣaṇā* cannot be used, how can direct meaning express Brahman?

## 110.

Śukadeva replies:

*buddhīndriya-manaḥ-prāṇān janānām asṛjat prabhuḥ*  
*mātrārthaṁ ca bhavārthaṁ ca ātmane ’kalpanāya ca*

**The Supreme Lord manifested the material intelligence, senses, mind and vital air of the living entities so that they could indulge their desires for sense gratification, take repeated births to engage in fruitive activities, become elevated in future lives and ultimately attain liberation. SB 10.87.2**

The supreme Lord created the upādhis like intelligence for various purposes for the sleeping jīvas (*janānām*). He did not create jīvas by his ignorance. This discards the vivarta-vāda notion. He created the upādhis for enjoying māyā (*mātrārtham)*. *Mātra* means measuring from the root *mī* which also means creating illusions or māyā. He created upādhis for causing action, which creates repeated birth (*bhavārtham*) and for going to other planets and enjoying there (*ātmane*) and for destroying what was produced (*akalpanāya),* producing liberation. In other words he created upādhis for artha, dharma, kāma and mokṣa.

Mokṣa in this case means the state of consciousness alone. *Yathā-varṇa-vidhānam apavargaś cāpi bhavati:* all these destinations are prescribed for the self according to the quality of their actions, as indicated in the Vedas; liberation is then achieved. SB 5.19.19

*yo 'sau bhagavati sarva-bhūtātmany anātmye 'nirukte 'nilayane paramātmani vāsudeve 'nanya-nimitta-bhakti-yoga-lakṣaṇo nānā-gati-nimittāvidyā-granthi-randhana-dvāreṇa yadā hi mahā-puruṣa-puruṣa-prasaṅgaḥ.*

**That liberation, whose essential nature is unmotivated bhakti-yoga to the Lord full of qualities, who attracts the minds of all beings, who is not the object of merging, who is not described by material words, who remains beyond destruction of the universe, who is the most excellent ātmā, who is the son of Vasudeva, takes place by destruction of the knot of ignorance which causes various material goals, when there is association with devotees of the Lord. SB 5.19.20**

This described sādhya bhakti as liberation. Thus it is understood that there are two types of liberation. The Lord created upādhis for destroying ignorance, which produces everything (*akalpanāya*) and producing liberation of either type.

This should be said. Since the Lord created upādhis for the visible entities for the purposes listed, he created the living entities as suitable vehicles of śakti to accomplish those goals. The śaktis for accomplishing artha, dharma and kāma are portions of avidyā-śakti, a function of māyā, which causes creation of various objects, since this śakti is concerned with material action and is the cause of repeated birth while misplacing one’s identity. The śakti producing mokṣa is a portion of the cit-śakti, which destroys the creations of māyā since it takes the form of bhakti and favorable jñāna.

As long as the jīvas are averse to the Lord the avidyā-śakti causing various creations remains prominent. Intelligence, mind, senses and prāṇa act in the material guṇas. They do not act directly without guṇas. That is true. But when the jīva becomes favorable to the Lord, because of the manifestation of cit-śakti in the intelligence, mind and senses, they act without the guṇas. Words also, act the same way, since they are composed of intelligence etc. The same is said at the conclusion of the chapter:

*ity etad varṇitaṁ rājan yan naḥ praśnaḥ kṛtas tvayā*  
*yathā brahmaṇy anirdeśye nīṛguṇe ’pi manaś caret*

**Thus I have replied to the question you asked me, O King, concerning how the mind can have access to the Lord, who is indescribable by material words and devoid of material qualities. SB 10.87.59**

From this it is understood that the intelligence, mind and senses are endowed with cit-śakti, share qualities of the spititual form of the Lord full of the highest bliss and are self-revealing. The same qualities apply to words.

The conclusion is that the śrutis function by taking support of the Lord’s spiritual svarūpa. Thus the Lord can be described by human words. It is said:

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api \|*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ \|\|*

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotes hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people. SB 1.5.11**

What the to speak of the words of a non-human (śruti) appearing from the breathing of the Lord directly, which are completely sheltered by the Lord. Definitely they can describe the Lord’s activities. Thus the śrutis directly function to describe the Lord. It is said *kvacid ajayātmanā ca carato ’nucaren nigamaḥ:* sometimes the Vedas serve you as you sport with your material and spiritual potencies. SB 10.87.14

Describing praṇava it is said:

*sva-dhāmno brāhmaṇaḥ sākṣād vācakaḥ paramātmanaḥ*  
*sa sarva-mantropaniṣad veda-bījaṁ sanātanam*

***Oṁ*** **indicates Brahman, which is its own shelter, and as well indicates Paramātmā and Bhagavān*****. Oṁ*** **contains all mantras and Upaniṣads and it is the eternal cause of the Vedas. SB 12.6.41**

Śruti says *om ity etad brahmaṇo nediṣṭhaṁ nāma*: *oṁ* is the name nearest to Brahman.

Nediṣṭam means “without obstacles like lakṣaṇā.” Thus the intention of Parīkṣit is “Please say how the śrutis directly express Brahman.” Fault in the śrutis is rejected later:

*dyu-pataya eva te na yayur antam anantatayā*  
*tvam api yad-antarāṇḍa-nicayā nanu sāvaraṇāḥ*  
*kha iva rajāṁsi vānti vayasā saha yac chrutayas*  
*tvayi hi phalanty atan-nirasanena bhavan-nidhanāḥ*

**Because you are unlimited, neither the lords of heaven nor even you yourself can ever reach the end of your glories. The countless universes, each enveloped in its shell, are compelled by the wheel of time to wander within you, like particles of dust blowing about in the sky. The śrutis, following their method of eliminating everything separate from the Supreme, become successful by revealing you as their final conclusion. SB 10.87.41**

Though some śrutis appear to be materialistic by absorption in artha, dharma and kāma, even those śrutis ultimately conclude in favorability to the Lord. Though the śrutis, being the spiritual breathing of the Lord, ultimately give mercy to all the jīvas overcome by māyā, initially they prescribe sacrifices for sons, which are capable of producing sons, for persons desiring earthly pleasures they can see and who ignorant of what they cannot see, in order to create faith in the Lord.

Showing the temporary nature of material goals after developing faith, the śrutis promote fire sacrifices for fulfilling desirable objects with celestial bliss and astonishing variety in Svarga in the next life. Then when these people continually practice in this manner, the śrutis produce a taste for dharma. After these people have developed taste for dharma, becoming pure in heart, reflecting on the meaning of scripture, understanding that the world is temporary, and fearing rebirth, the śrutis create desire of bliss of liberation. The bliss of liberation is a form of the appearance of the para-tattva.

Sūta says:

*dharmasya hy āpavargyasya nārtho ’rthāyopakalpate \|*  
*nārthasya dharmaikāntasya kāmo lābhāya hi smṛtaḥ \|\|*

**Material results are not suitable as the goal for the person dedicated to higher spiritual goals. Attainment of material assets is not the desire of the person who is dedicated to the higher path. SB 1.2.9**

*kāmasya nendriya-prītir lābho jīveta yāvatā \|*  
*jīvasya tattva-jijñāsā nārtho yaś ceha karmabhiḥ \|\|*

**For one who desires apavarga, sense pleasure attained from enjoying sense objects is not the goal as long as one lives. The goal of life is inquiry into the highest truth. What is accomplished by prescribed duties is not the goal. SB 1.2.10**

Just as the intelligence and senses can perceive the para tattva by the appearance of the cit-śakti, by some degree of favorability to the Lord, so śruti, a form of words, can express directly the tattva without material guṇas, by a methodical appearance of the cit-śakti, since the śrutis are suitable to deal with things beyond the guṇas. Speaking of praṇava it is said:

*tato 'bhūt tri-vṛd oṁkāro yo 'vyakta-prabhavaḥ sva-rāṭ*  
*yat tal liṅgaṁ bhagavato brahmaṇaḥ paramātmanaḥ*

**From nāda arose the oṁkāra composed of three sounds and whose origin is unclear.** ***Oṁ*** **is directly the Supreme Lord and causes realization of Bhagavān, Paramātmā and Brahman. SB 12.6.39**

Tattva appears in two forms: Bhagavān and Brahman. The cit-śakti has two forms also: as self-manifesting bhakti and as self-manfiesting jñāna. The śrutis dedicated to bhakti deal with Bhagavān, and the śrutis dedicated to jñāna deal with Brahman. This is the general conclusion.

## 111.

To explain the details a story is introduced:

*sva-sṛṣṭam idam āpīya śayānaṁ saha śaktibhiḥ*  
*tad-ante bodhayāṁ cakrus tal-liṅgaiḥ śrutayaḥ param*

**After Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa withdrew the created universes along with the puruṣāvatāras, he remained inattentive to universal affairs as if sleeping. After this, the śrutis reawakened him to the job of creation using words indicating his powers. SB 10.87.12**

Withdrawing at the time of destruction the universe he had created, he was sleeping with his śaktis. Making prakṛti, the puruṣas and their portions one with himself, closing his eyes to the products , he slept. After that, at the time of the end of pralaya period, the śrutis awakened Bhagavān (*param*) with words describing him (*tat-liṅgaiḥ*) in the morning. They praised him with verses. This is Bhagavān, not the puruṣāvatāra, for it is said:

*bhagavān eka āsedam agra ātmātmanāṁ vibhuḥ*  
*ātmecchānugatāv ātmā nānā-maty-upalakṣaṇaḥ*

**Bhagavān, who exists in the form of Paramātmā and Brahman according to the viewpoint, alone existed before the creation of the universe, when the desire to create bodies of the jīvas was absent. SB 3.5.23**

At that time the puruṣāvataras had disappeared.

## 112.

An example is given to illustrate the previous verse.

*yathā śayānaṁ saṁrājaṁ vandinas tat-parākramaiḥ*  
*pratyūṣe ’bhetya su-ślokair bodhayanty anujīvinaḥ*

**The poets serving a king approach him at dawn and awaken him by reciting his heroic deeds. SB 10.87.13**

The poets of the king awaken him with verses of glorification, not describing him impersonally. The Lord is like the sleeping king. At night the king plays with his queens, giving up external duties. He sleeps with his intimate friends, since he is situated in the inner chambers. He is praised by poets on the pretext of waking him with verses reciting his glories. Similarly, Bhagavān not glancing at his duties of managing the universe, plays privately with his associates in his abobe. The poets know deeply the nature of the king (*anujīvinaḥ)*. Similarly the śrutis know the Lord.

## 113.

First the śtutis show by their description the complete tattva, the form of Bhagavān, realized by bhakti which allows direct vision of the Lord, served by qualiteis of jñāna and they include Brahman in this.

*jaya jaya jahy ajām ajita doṣa-gṛbhīta-guṇāṁ*  
*tvam asi yad ātmanā samavaruddha-samasta-bhagaḥ*  
*aga-jagad-okasām akhila-śakty-avabodhaka te*  
*kvacid ajayātmanā ca carato ’nucaren nigamaḥ*

**Victory, victory to You, O unconquerable one! By your very nature you are perfectly full in all opulences; therefore please defeat the eternal power of illusion, who assumes control over the modes of nature to create difficulties for conditioned souls. O You who awaken all the material and spiritual energies of the jīvas, sometimes the Vedas serve you as you sport with your material and spiritual potencies. SB 10.87.14**

O unconquered lord (*ajita*)! Reveal your glories (*jaya jaya*)! Jaya is repeated to show respect.

*sarveṣām apy aghavatām idam eva suniṣkṛtam*  
*nāma-vyāharaṇaṁ viṣṇor yatas tad-viṣayā matiḥ*

**Simply by chanting the holy name of Lord Viṣṇu, sinful persons attract the attention of the Supreme Lord. SB 6.2.10**

Thus by addressing the Lord with *ajita,* they invoke the Lord’s direct appearance. By addressing in this way they invoke the Lord with form, in his svarūpa. He could not be called to appear if he did not have a form. Scriptures say that the name is so powerful that even uttered in hatred or fear, or to mean something else, it has effect. Effects for these cases was previously proved by the experienced of the realization of the learned and scriptural statements. The tattva that appears to the eyes in the form of the vigraha appears to the voice as the name. Since the svarūpa of the name and the person indicated by the name are non-different, contact with the name means contact with the vigraha. Elsewhere, the śrutis describe the Lord by conventional and other uses of words (*rūḍhi*), using allusion (*saṅketa*) and objects indicated by definitions out of broad categories. The names are the direct result of the creeper of the śrutis.

O Ajita! Show your excellence (*jaya*)! This shows the śrutis using principal meaning of words (mukhya-vṛtti) of the verb *ji* (to suprass), joined with the quality of excellence. *Na te mahitvām anv āśnuvanti:* they do not know your glory. (Ṛg Veda 7.99.1.1) *Na tat samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate:* his equal or superior is not seen. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

By showing their bhakti in uttering *jaya jaya,* the śrutis understand that bhakti is the cause of the Lord appearing. “By what action will I show my excellence?” You show it by destroying māyā and giving bhakti. Conquer māyā (*ajāṁ jahi*)!

“Māyā is a śakti with the functions of vidyā and avidyā. By destroying māyā I will destroy vidyā.” Māyā has accepted the quality of vidyā, the cause of remembering you, with the fault of avidyā, the cause of jīva forgetting ātmā. By her covering, māyā creates the fault of avidyā and sometimes, somewhere, somehow, māyā leaves some jīva. There is thus fault in māyā’s quality of vidyā whose nature is making māyā leave the jīva (since māyā has covered the jīva in the first place.) Thus, better to uproot māyā and give the jīvas bhakti to your lotus feet.

Having shown that the Lord is above māyā by possession of śakti capable of destroying māyā, the śrutis indicate the eternity, knowledge and bliss nature of the Lord. They use tātparya-vṛtti This is using direct meaning of words to express a meaningful statement.17 with the aim of refuting non existence (*atat*).

*māyāṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyān mayinaṁ tu maheśvaram*

**One should know māyā, prakṛti, and the Lord, the controller of māyā. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

*ajām ekām lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇām*

**The one unborn** ***prakṛti*** **which is red, white and black. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

*sarvasyādhipatiḥ sarvasyeśānaḥ*

**The Lord is the ruler of all, the controller of all. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

*sa vā eṣa neti neti*

**He is not this, not this.**

“In praying to destroy māyā, you desire to destroy my powers which are upādhis.” Since (*yat*) you have all powers called your tripāda-vibhūti (*samavaruddha-samasta-bhagaḥ*) by your svarūpa (*ātmanā*), what use are the insignficant powers of upādhis?

In the commentary on verse 38 Śrīdhara Svāmī says, “The work of the Lord of a host of desire cows of continuous bliss and knowledge is not done by māyā. Your eight powers are not limited by time and space like others’ powers. They are unlimited since they are related to your complete svarūpa.”

The word *ātmanā* means “by your svarūpa” and *bhaga* means “qualities arising from your svarūpa.” Thus the insignificance of māyā’s qualities is implied. Words like *svarūpa* and *īśvara* can describe the Lord by conventional meaning to express the Lord’s svarūpa or the qualities arising from his svarūpa.

*yad-ātmako bhagavān tad-ātmikā vyaktiḥ*

**What constitutes the Lord becomes manifest as non-different from himself.**

*parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śruyate*

**The śaktis of the Lord are many. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

That svarūpa śakti is perceived by all. O arouser of all the śaktis of the jīvas with bodies (*okasām*) either immobile (*aga)* or moving (*jagad*). You are an ocean with waves of various śaktis for the jīvas.

There is another meaning. “By destroying māyā the śakti of the jīva, an upadhi of māyā will also be destroyed.” The answer is the same. By the svarūpa-śakti , the complete śakti which gives happiness to the jīvas will appear. In this way the śrutis indicate the taṭastha nature of the jīva.

*Ko hy evānyāt kaḥ prāṇyāt yad eṣa ākāśa ānando na syāt*

**Who could breathe if** ***ākāśa*** **(the Lord) were not bliss? Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.7**

*prāṇasya prāṇam*

**The Lord is the life of prāṇa. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.18**

*tam eva bhāntam*

**Fire shines following the shining lord. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.2.15**

*dehānte devas tārakaṁ brahma vyācaṣṭe*

**At death, the devas spoke of Brahman. Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad 1.7**

*yasya deve parā bhaktiḥ yathā deve tathā gurau \|*  
*tasyaite kathitā hyarthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ \|\|*

**For the person who has bhakti for the Lord and bhakti for guru as much as the Lord the meaning of scripture is revealed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.23**

“How do you know that my activities are not done by māyā? How do you know about my eternity knowledge and bliss and my qualities produced by my svarūpa-śakti?” We śrutis *(nigamaḥ*) in the form of sound and devatās serve (*anucaret*) you who, at the time of creation *(kvacit*) play (*carataḥ)* with māyā (*ajayā*) in the form of the puruṣa, and play eternally with your qualites arising from your svarūpa by your svarūpa-śakti, with the rasa of eternity, knowledge and bliss *(ātmanā*). Thus we know all of this. Possessive case (*carataḥ-*- of you who play) expresses the accusative case: we serve you who play.

Types of śruti statements

This is what is said. There are two types of statements in the śruti (indicating the Lord): those dealing with the three guṇas and those devoid of guṇas. There are three types that deal with the guṇas. The first type define the guṇas with the Lord as a neutral support for the material world. *Yato vā imāni bhūtāni*: all these bodies arise from the Lord. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 3.1.1) The second type shows the glories of the Lord by describing rulership of devatās in the three guṇas. *Indro yato’vasitasya raja:* Indra is king of all beings. (Ṛg Veda 1.32.15) The third type indicates the highest entity by negating the three guṇas. This has two types: by negation (*niṣedha*) and by equivalence *(sāmānyaādhikaraṇya*). An example of the first type is *asthūlam anaṇu neti netī*: the Lord is not heavy and not small; he is not this. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.7.8) An example of the second type is *sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma*: the Lord is everything. *Tat tvam asi:* you are this. In the statement *sarvaṁ khalv idaṁ brahma*, Brahman is indicated and revealed because the universe comes from him. *Sad idam* (the Lord is the universe) means that pure Brahman is the world in the sense that it is his aṁśa.

By stating the oneness of *tat* with *tvam, tat* is indicated as Brahman with *tvam* as the śakti of Brahman, possessing consciousness Without knowledge of *tat* knowledge of *tvam* becomes insignificant. That is why *tat* is used in this statement. In both cases Brahman is beyond the three guṇas.

Where the qualities of the Lord are clearly understood by negating the three guṇas, it indicates Bhagavān. Where the qualities are unclear, it indicates Brahman. In this way śruti statements dealing with the three guṇas have been explained. This explains how the śrutis serve the Lord playing with māyā (*ajayā carataḥ*).

The śrutis dealing with the Lord beyond the guṇas are of two types. Those dealing with Brahman and those dealing with Bhagavān. For instance *ānando brahma*: Brahman is bliss. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad)

*na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate*  
*na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate \|*  
*parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate*  
*svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca \|\|*

**He has no body and no senses. No one is equal to him or higher. His supreme power is manifold. His actions are naturally revealed with knowledge and strength. (This indicates Bhagavān with śaktis.) Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

This explains how the śrutis serve the Lord playing with his svarūpa-śakti (*ātmanā carataḥ*). In this way the activities of the śrutis have been accomplished. The nirguṇa statements directly serve the Lord by their nature. Statements dealing with the three guṇas serve by indicating the Lord only: in order to negate māyā, its guṇas are described; later māyā is completely rejected and the qualities of the Lord’s svarūpa are directly indicated. To suggest the unity of statements, the conclusion is indicated in the initial statement (upakrama). The conclusion (upasaṁhāra) is stated in the following. *Yac chrutayas tvayi hi phalanty atan-nirasanena bhavan-nidhanāḥ*: the *śrutis,* following their method of eliminating everything separate from the Supreme, become successful by revealing you as their final conclusion. SB 10.87.41

Madhva’s commentary quotes the following śruti. *na cakṣur na śrotraṁ na tarko na smṛtir vedo hy evainaṁ vedayati:* the eye, ear, logic, smṛti and Vedas do not reveal the Lord. *Aupaniṣadaḥ puruṣaḥ*: the Lord is revealed by the Upaniṣads. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.26)

Brahman concludes in Bhagavān

## 114.

Śrutis speak of Brahman, but even those that speak of Brahman ultimately indicate Bhagavān.

*bṛhad upalabdham etad avayanty avaśeṣatayā*  
*yata udayāstamayau vikṛter mṛd ivāvikṛtāt*  
*ata ṛṣayo dadhus tvayi mano-vacanācaritaṁ*  
*katham ayathā bhavanti bhuvi datta-padāni nṛṇām*

**The world is understood to be Brahman since it remains after destruction of the universe, just like earth remains after pots are destroyed, since śrutis accept that creation and destruction arise from Brahman. But after advocating Brahman, the śrutis maintain that the import of words and focus of the mind is in you, Bhagavān. When men place their feet on earth is it illusory? SB 10.87.15**

All this (*etat)* is understood (*upalabdhan*) to be Brahman (*bṛhat*). How is that? It is what remains of the universe (*vikṛteḥ*). How is this? It is like earth. What is seen as the remainder of pots is seen to be earth. Brahman is like that, because (*yataḥ*) the śrutis accept *(avayanti*) the creation and destruction (*udyāstamayau*) of the universe to be caused by Brahman. *Yato vā imāni bhūtāni:* from the Brahman can all beings. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad) Therefore Brahman is compared to earth.

How is it that Brahman does not change by this process? Its nature is to be without change *(avikṛtāt*). It does not change because of the acintya-śakti. *Śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt:* the defects of the agent do not apply to Brahman because of scriptural statements, which are the only proof concerning inconceivable subjects. (Brahma-sūtra 2.1.27)

Though the *bṛhat* is said to possess śakti, it is here presented as Brahman Brahman is derived from the same root as *bṛhat.*18 since it is not described in this verse with qualities of Bhagavān manifested. It is not possible to present Brahman with no śakti at all since it would become most insignificant. Thus in the context it is referred to as Brahman. Thus here in the example of earth, agency of Brahman is not established (since Brahman has no action).

But though Brahman is presented, Bhagavān is ultimately the conclusion. That is explained next. After expounding about Brahman (*ataḥ*), the Vedas (*ṛṣayaḥ*) indicate that the import of words and concentration of mind is in you, Bhagavān. Because Brahman and Bhagavān are one entity-- difference being produced by showing manifestation or non-manifestation of qualities--arthāntara-nyāsa This means illustration of a matter by giving a particular case.19 is given to illustrate this. Do men, seeing or not seeing Brahman place their feet on earth or not? Certainly their feet reach the earth.

*jñāna-yogaś ca man-niṣṭho*  
*nairguṇyo bhakti-lakṣaṇaḥ*  
*dvayor apy eka evārtho*  
*bhagavac-chabda-lakṣaṇaḥ*

**The goal of jñāna, fixed on my expansion as Brahman, beyond the guṇas, and the goal of bhakti are actually only one goal, which is known by the word Bhagavān. SB 3.32.32**

Śruti to prove this is given in Madhva’s commentary:

*hanta tam eva puruṣaṁ sarvāṇi nāmāny abhivadanti yathā nadyaḥ syandamānāḥ samudrāyaṇāḥ samudram abhiniviśanti evam evaitāni nāmāni sarvāṇi puruṣam abhiviśanti*

**They call the Lord by all names. Just as rivers, taking shelter of the ocean, flow to the ocean, all the names indicate the Lord.**

## 115.

All the scriptures describe you alone by direct words (abhidāna) or as the ultimate meaning (tātparya), either as Bhagavān or Brahman. That is true, not imaginary.

*iti tava sūrayas try-adhipate ’khila-loka-mala-*  
*kṣapaṇa-kathāmṛtābdhim avagāhya tapāṁsi jahuḥ*  
*kim uta punaḥ sva-dhāma-vidhutāśaya-kāla-guṇāḥ*  
*parama bhajanti ye padam ajasra-sukhānubhavam*

**O master of Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva! Therefore, the wise get rid of all misery by diving deep into the nectarean ocean of your topics, which wash away all the contamination of the universe. Then what to speak of those who, having by spiritual strength rid their minds of bad habits and freed themselves from time, are able to worship your true nature, O supreme one, finding within it uninterrupted bliss? SB 10.87.16**

O master of the Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva! Their source is the puruṣa called Nārāyaṇa. Nārāyaṇa’s Lord is Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa, since his svarūpa is superior. O Lord of all lords! Since (*iti*) the direct meaning and conclusion of the Vedas is you, persons who have discrimination *(sūrayaḥ*) reject those parts of the Veda which do not present you as the final goals and submerge themselves in the ocean of nectar of your glories which destroys all suffering in the world. Serving you with faith, they give up all austere actions or give up karmas because they are painful.

If the sādhakas have this nature, what to speak of person who have conquered the antaḥkaraṇa *(āśaya*), the influence of time, the cause of old age, and the three guṇas—those person who worship Brahman, your position of unlimited happiness. They submerge themselves in your topics and give up all austere karmas and also give up dedication to experiencing Brahman.

This is what is said. There are three types of people: the fool, the wise man and the successful man. Taking everything together, the Vedas indicate Bhagavān as a real entity. If he were not actually existent, then, because the Vedas would have no relationship with hims, topics of Bhagavān in the Vedas would not be famous for destroying sins of even persons devoid of philosophical knowledge (*akhila-loka-mala-kṣapaṇa*). Iron has no power to burn without contact with fire. Moreover if the Bhagavān were false, the wise would not indulge in his topics. It is like saying a barren woman is fertile. They would not give up their dharmas by becoming absorbed in his topics. It would be like giving up a tīrtha on hearing that a king is as great as the Gaṅgā.

Those who have attained success, being ātmārāmas, would not become absorbed in the Lord’s topics since they would not respect such false topics. Those who bathed in a lake of nectar would not give that up and go to a river with imaginary greater qualities.

The qualities of the Lord’s topics are described. *Hanti kaluṣaṁ śrotraṁ sa yāto hariḥ*:

the Lord, being heard about, destroys sin. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa)

*tvad avagamī na vetti bhavad-uttha-śubhāśubhayor*  
*guṇa-viguṇānvayāṁs tarhi deha-bhṛtāṁ ca giraḥ*  
*anu-yugam anv-ahaṁ sa-guṇa gīta-paramparayā*  
*śravaṇa-bhṛto yatas tvam apavarga-gatir manu-jaiḥ*

**When a person realizes you, he no longer cares about his good and bad fortune arising from past pious and sinful acts, since it is you alone who control this good and bad fortune. Such a realized devotee also disregards what ordinary living beings say about him. Every day he fills his ears with your glories, which are recited in each age by the unbroken succession of Manu’s descendants, and thus you become his ultimate goal. You are the shelter of even liberation. SB 10.87.40**

*harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|*  
*adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|*

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam. SB 1.7.11**

Therefore the natrual relationship of the Lord with the Vedas which describe his qualities is established everywhere.

*oṁ āsya jānanto nāma cit viviktana mahas te viṣno sumatiṁ bhajāmahe*

**O Viṣṇu! Your name is spiritual and self-revealed*****.*** **Knowing that name a little*****,*** **not even completely, chanting impurely, we have attained fine intelligence about you, because that name** ***oṁ*** **is perfect in itself. Ṛg Veda 1.156.3**

*yathā puṣkara-palāśam āpo na śliṣyanti evam*  
*evaṁ vidaṁ pāpaṁ karma na śliṣyati \|*

**Just as water does not stick to the lotus leaf, sins of karma do not stick to the Lord. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

*na karmaṇā lipyate pāpakena*

**He is not contaminated by sinful action. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

*tat-sukṛta-duṣkṛte vidhunute \|*

**He purifies a person of sin and piety. Kauṣītak;-brāhmaṇa Upaniṣad**

*evaṁ vāva na tapati kim ahaṁ sādhu karavaṁ kim ahaṁ pāpaṁ nākaravam*

**He does not suffer. What good did I do for him? What sin did I not do? Taittirīya Upaniṣad 9.1**

*muktā hy enam upāsata*

**Liberated souls worship the Lord. Sauparṇa-śruti**

Other verses also showing Bhagavān in statements concerning worship have already been quoted in different places. They will not been given here.

“According to you the Lord can be indicated by words. This makes the Lord material, for śrutis say:

*yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manasā saha \|*

**Words along with mind, not attaining the Lord, return. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad)**

*avacanenaiva provāca*

**He spoke without words. (quoted by Madhva)**

*yad-vācānabhyuditaṁ yena vāg abhyudyate tad eva brahma tvaṁ viddhi*

**Know that Brahma which cannot be expressed by words and by which words arise.** ***Katha Upanisād*** **1.4**

*yat śrotraṁ na śṛṇoti yena śrotram idaṁ śrutam*

**Ear does not hear him. He does not hear using an ear.**

These śruti statements show that the Lord cannot be directly described by words.”

If there is a fault in directly indicating the Lord, why does the fault not apply also to indirect indications, since both are subject to the same function of words. The Lord is not directly indicated as a material subject, but can be indicated as being indescribable.

## 116.

The śrutis summarize the meaning of the Mahāvākyas:

*dyu-pataya eva te na yayur antam anantatayā*  
*tvam api yad-antarāṇḍa-nicayā nanu sāvaraṇāḥ*  
*kha iva rajāṁsi vānti vayasā saha yac chrutayas*  
*tvayi hi phalanty atan-nirasanena bhavan-nidhanāḥ*

**Because you are unlimited, neither the lords of heaven nor even you yourself can ever reach the end of your glories. The countless universes, each enveloped in its shell, are compelled by the wheel of time to wander within you, like particles of dust blowing about in the sky. The śrutis, following their method of eliminating everything separate from the Supreme, become successful by revealing you as their final conclusion. SB 10.87.41**

The Lord’s svarūpa and guṇas are indescribable in two ways: because they are unlimited and because they are impossible to describe. First the unlimited nature is described. O Bhagavān! The devatās like Brahmā (*dyu-patayaḥ*) do not know (*na yayuḥ*) your extent *(antam*) because you are unlimited (*anantatayā*). You are not like objects which have an end. You do not have an end to yourself. But then how could I know everything and possess all śaktis? Since you do not have an end *(anantatanyā*), ignorance of a rabbit’s horn (which is imaginary) cannot destroy your omniscience and not having a rabbit’s horn cannot destroy your śakti. All possibilities exist in you.20

*yo’syādhyakṣaḥ parame vyoman \| so’ṅga veda yadi vā na vede*

**The controller of the highest heaven may know the Lord or may not know the Lord. Ṛg Veda 10.129.7**

His being without end is described. Oh (*nanu*)! Within you (*yad antra*) all the universes with seven coverings each times thicker than the last layer wander about (*vānti*) in the sky by the wheel of time (*vayasā*) like dust particles all together, not one after the other. Because unlimited universes wander about, the Lord’s svarūpa is unlimited. Because of being the shelter of their various qualities, he has unlimited qualities.

*yad ūrdhvaṁ gārgi divaḥ yad arvāk pṛthivyā yad antaraṁ dyāv āpṛthivī ime yad bhūtaṁ bhavac ca bhaviṣyac ca*

**He is above the heaven, he is below the earth, he is between heaven and earth. He is past, present and future. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.7.3**

*viṣṇor nu kaṁ vīryāṇi pravocaṁ yaṁ pārthivāni vimame rajāṁsi*  
*yo askambhayad uttaraṁ sadhasthaṁ vicakramāṇas tredhorugāya*

**I speak of the valor of Viṣṇu who created the earth and heaven, who established the worlds above and below. Ṛg Veda 1.1.54.1**

Since the śrutis conclude in you (*tvayi phalanti*), they indciate your unlimited nature through indications of one means or the other. It is said that the the bliss of brahman is a hundred times that of the Prajāpatis but then it is said yato vāca nivartante: words return from him. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad)

It is said:

*na tad īdṛg iti jñeyaṁ na vācyaṁ na ca tarkyate \|*  
*paśyanto’pi na jānanti mero rūpaṁ vipaścitaḥ \|\|*

**The Lord is not be known in this way. He cannot be described or inferred. Though the wise see Meru, they do not know it.**

In this way the Lord is designated as being beyond description.

The Lord’s svarūpa is also directly indicated. *Yat tu satyaṁ jñānam*: the Lord is truth and knowledge. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1.2) The Lord’s qualities are directly described. *Svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca:* the Lord has inherent knowledge, strength and action. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8)

The śrutis then describe the Lord’s nature. The śrutis, taking shelter of you, become successful by refuting false conceptions about you (*atat-nirasaṇena*). Refuting the idea that you are a material entity, they take shelter of you.

Material qualities are described as follows:

*buddhir jñānam asaṁmohaḥ kṣamā satyaṁ damaḥ śamaḥ*  
*sukhaṁ duḥkhaṁ bhavo ’bhāvo bhayaṁ cābhayam eva ca \|\|*  
*ahiṁsā samatā tuṣṭis tapo dānaṁ yaśo ’yaśaḥ \|*  
*bhavanti bhāvā bhūtānāṁ matta eva pṛthag-vidhāḥ \|\|*

**Intelligence, knowledge, freedom from illusion, tolerance, truthfulness, sense control, mind control, pleasure, pain, birth, death, fear, fearlessness, non-violence, equanimity, satisfaction, austerity, charity, fame, infamy—all these various states of the living beings arise from me alone.BG 10.4-5**

*hrīr dhīr bhīr etat sarvaṁ mana eva*

**Shame, intelligence and fear are all the mind. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.5.3**

This indicates material knowledge and other elements. All that is not Brahman. That is the meaning of *neti neti:* it is not this. Material qualities are denied in *na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate:* he has no body and no senses. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

The Lord is positively defined by *yad satyam jñānam and svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca*. The śrutis conclude in you by refuting the idea that words like knowledge describing the Lord denote only material objects. Because the Lord is beyond intellect, he is not describable. But he is describable because he can be indicated by saying “He has a form something like this (being spiritual).”

One sees a manifestation of the Lord with his self-manifesting form in the pure heart, just as from saying “You are the tenth person” you receive some material knowledge. This finally means that śruti has self-revealing śakti.

*śabda-brahma paraṁ brahma mamobhe śāśvatī tanū*

**The scripture and the supreme Brahman are my two eternal forms. SB 6.16.51**

*vedasya ceśvarātmatvāt*

**The Vedas arise from the Lord*****.***  **SB 11.3.43**

*vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt svayambhūr iti śuśruma*

**The Vedas are directly Nārāyaṇa and are born from him. This we have heard from Yamarāja. SB 6.1.40**

*kiṁ vā parair īśvaraḥ sadyo hṛdy avarudhyate ’tra kṛtibhiḥ śuśrūṣubhis tat-kṣaṇāt*

**The Supreme Lord becomes immediately captured in the heart of the accomplished devotees by hearing Bhāgavatam and even by those who have suddenly developed a desire to hear it. SB 1.1.2**

*aupaniṣadaḥ puruṣaḥ*

**The Lord is known through the Upaniṣads. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.1.2**

This means the Lord is known through śruti *(aupaniṣadaḥ*) just as form is known throught the eye.

“The Lord appears by himself when his self-manifesting śakti in the form of śruti dispels notions of a material object like dispelling darkness. Thus he is not actually indicated by śruti. The Lord is like the sun which reveals itself as well as a pot when it makes its appearance. (Śruti is unnecessary.)”

This argument does not apply since śruti and the Lord are non-different. On the other hand, if it is accepted that the śakti and possessor of the śakti are different, the Lord is still indicated by śruti for he can be described as an indescribable being. Thus Garuḍa Purāṇa says:

*aprasiddher avācyaṁ tad vācyaṁ sarvāgamoktitaḥ \|*  
*atarkyaṁ tarkyam ajñeyaṁ jñeyam evaṁ paraṁ smṛtam \|\|*

**The Lord who cannot be described since he is unknown is described by the statements of scriptures. The Lord is said to be beyond reason, but subject to reasoning, and unknowable, but also capable of being known.**

*anyad eva tadvad itād atho aviditād adhi*

**The Lord is different from the known. He is different from the unknown. This we have learned. Kena Upaniṣad 1.3**

*yasmin brahmaṇi sarva-śakti-nilaye mānāni no mānināṁ*  
*niṣṭhāyai prabhavanti hanti kaluṣaṁ śrotraṁ sa yāto harir \|\|*

**Conceptions cannot be established by philosophers concerning Brahman, the abode of all śaktis, but this Lord, being heard by the ear, destroys sin. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.8.59**

“Brahman named Bhagavān, manifesting his śaktis, has a svarūpa revealed by his self-manifesting śakti, which is described in the Vedas. How is Brahman with no manifestation of śakti described in scriptures?”

In our opinion, Brahman is manifested by Bhagavān’s śakti. It is said:

*madīyaṁ mahimānaṁ ca paraṁ brahmeti śabditam*  
*vetsyasy anugṛhītaṁ me sampraśnair vivṛtaṁ hṛdi*

**By my mercy, you will realize my power known as the impersonal Brahman, which will be disclosed in your heart through questions and answers. SB 8.34.38**

This verse shows that Brahman is not revealed by another entity, since Brahman and Bhagavān are the same. It is said here that even teaching about Brahman through common language is possible, by the mercy of words following śruti. That is not forbidden, since at the time of cultivation of Brahman, śabda in the form of the experience of bhakti are equally endowed with the svarūpa-śakti. Thus there is no fault.

*prakṛtiś ca prākṛtaṁ ca yan na jighranti jighranti yan na paśyanti paśyanti, yan na śṛṇvanti śṛṇvanti, yan jānanti jānanti ca*

**Those who do not smell matter or material things, can smell him. Those who do not see matter or material things can see him. Those who do not hear matter or material things can hear him. Those who do not know matter and material things can know him. Sauparṇa-śruti.**

The śrutis speak to the Lord.

Śaktis of the Lord

## 117.

The Lord’s svarūpa exists as śakti and as the possessor of śakti. It was explained that Bhagavān means “he who exists with predominance of being possessor of the śakti, which are defined as arising from his svarūpa.” Lakṣmī is she who exists with predominance of the śakti. To show this, difference of some of the unlimited śaktis are shown by different functions.

*śriyā puṣṭyā girā kāntyā kīrtyā tuṣṭyelayorjayā*  
*vidyayāvidyayā śaktyā māyayā ca niṣevitam*

**Also in attendance were the Lord’s principal internal potencies—Śrī, Puṣṭi, Gīr, Kānti, Kīrti, Tuṣṭi, Ilā and Ūrjā—as were potencies Vidyā, Avidyā and Māyā, and his internal pleasure potency, Śakti. SB 10.39.55**

Śakti refers to Mahā-lakṣmī, who arises from his svarūpa. The word śakti applies to the Lord’s internal mahā-śakti, the original shelter. Māyā is the external śakti. Śrī and others function both internally and externally. This should be understood in all cases. The first type is now discussed. Śrī is the Lord’s wealth. She is not Mahā-lakṣmī since Mahā-lakṣmī is her source. That will be discussed later. The second type is now discussed. Śrī is material wealth. She is depicted in the following:

*sva eva dhiṣṇye 'bhiratasya kiṁ tayā*

**What use do I have for her, since I have your power behind me? SB 4.20.29**

*na śrīr viraktam api māṁ vijahāti*

**Even Laksmī to whom I show no attachment, but to gain whose favor others perform vows, does not give up the dust of my feet. SB 3.16.7**

Nārada says:

*śriyam anucaratīṁ tad-arthinaś ca*  
*dvipada-patīn vibudhāṁś ca yat sva-pūrṇaḥ*  
*na bhajati nija-bhṛtya-varga-tantraḥ*  
*katham amum udvisṛjet pumān kṛta-jñaḥ*

**The Lord, being complete in his svarūpa, does not care for the sum total of all wealth, which is worshipped by those desiring all wealth, and does not care for those desiring even a little wealth, or for human kings or devatās. He is dependent on his devotees. How could the devotee reject this Lord even a little? SB 4.31.22**

The kings and devatās are listed together because they desire wealth. It is narrated that Lakṣmī, in charge of the three worlds, having been destroyed by the curse of Durvāsā, again appeared by the glance of Lakṣmī who is directly the consort of the Lord, when she appeared from the Milk ocean. Other śaktis are similar. Puṣṭi causes nourishment, Gir causes good speech, Kānti gives beauty, Kīrti gives fame, and Tuṣṭi gives satisfaction. 21 Ilā mentioned in verse 55 is Bhū. She also represents another śakti Līlā.

Vidyā of the first type (internal) causes realization of the Lord. This is a special function of the saṁvit function. Vidyā of the second type is the door to revelation of the first type of vidyā. Avidyā of the first type is the cause of forgetting the Lords’ powers. It is a function of the bliss of prema of his mother and others. *Gopī-janāvidyā-kalā-prerakaḥ*: the Lord inspires the gopīs with prema filled with ignorance of his powers. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad) This will be discussed in its proper place. The second type of avidyā is the cause of jīva’s forgetting the Lord’s svarūpa, with the special function of covering the jīva.

The word *ca* in the verse 55 indicates other śaktis of the first type: sandhinī, samvit, hlādinī, bhakti, ādhāra-śakti, mūrti , This śākti reveals the vigraha of the Lord.22 vimalā (purity), jāyā, yogā, prahvī, īśānā and anugraha (mercy). The *śaktis* are mentioned in *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* 6.19 commentary in relation to *pīṭhā-pūja*: Vimalā, Utkarṣiṇī, Jñānā, Kriyā, Yogā, Prahvī, Satyā, Īśānā and Anugrahā

23 Sandhinī is also called satyā (truth and existence) and jayā is called utkarṣiṇī (attractive). Yoga means yogamāyā. Saṁvit is the jñāna and ajñāna śakti (causing awareness of the Lord, and forgetting his powers). This is also called śuddha-sattva. Prahvī is the cause of manifesting unlimited variety. Īśānā is the cause the śakti of ruling everything.

The second type of these śaktis manifests similar items in the material world. The functions of māyā are not described since they serve material activities. In Bhāgavatam, māyā’s service is considered to be ordinary, since her service lies in the material realm, taking shelter of the puruṣāvatāra, an aṁśa of Bhagavān, at a great distance.

Thus Nārada praises Kṛṣṇa:

*viśuddha-vijñāna-ghanaṁ sva-saṁsthayā*  
*samāpta-sarvārtham amogha-vāñchitam*  
*sva-tejasā nitya-nivṛtta-māyā-*  
*guṇa-pravāhaṁ bhagavantam īmahi*

**Let me surrender to you, Bhagavān. You are the condensed form of pure knowledge and by your svarūpa-śakti you have all powers. Your desires are always fulfilled. Because your svarūpa-śakti you are never touched by the material guṇas.**

*tvām īśvaraṁ svāśrayam ātma-māyayā*  
*vinirmitāśeṣa-viśeṣa-kalpanam*  
*krīḍārtham abhyātta-manuṣya-vigrahaṁ*  
*nato ’smi dhuryaṁ yadu-vṛṣṇi-sātvatām*

**I bow down to you, the supreme controller, dependent only on yourself. By your potency you have constructed the unlimited particular arrangements of this universe. Now you have appeared as the greatest hero among the Yadus, Vṛṣṇis and Sātvatas and have chosen to participate in human warfare. SB 10.37.22-23**

The meaning of the two verses is this. I surrender to Bhagavān, who has a form (*ghanam*) which is the supreme tattva (*vijñāna*) and is pure (*viśuddham*). You have all powers *(sarvārtham*) which are nitya-siddha (*samāpta*) by your svarūpa-śakti or the form of your svarūpa. You have no desire for the actions of the material world, which are useless (*amogha-vāñchitam*). “But sometimes, though he does not desire it, he has a relationship with the world.” You are free of the influence of the guṇas eternally by the power of your svarūpa-śakti.

I offer respects to you (*tvām*), Bhagavān named Kṛṣṇa, and also to the antaryāmī puruṣa as your aṁśa (*īśvaram*). As īśvara you take shelter of yourself by your svarūpa-śakti (*svāśrayam*). You have created unlimited variety of products by māyā meant for the jīva (*ātma-māyayā*). Or, you take shelter of yourself by your svarūpa-śakti. Your māyā (*kalpanam*) has created unlimited variety (*vinirmitāśeṣa-viṣeṣa*). You have manifested your human form to your devotees (*abhyātta*) for play, by manifesting your respective śaktis simultanteously at the time of your appearance on earth, when īśvara merges into Bhagavān. You are the hero of the Yadus, Vṛṣṇis and Sātvatas: you bear the weight of their prema.

Or in verse SB 10 35.55, *śaktyā* can modify all the nouns. Then Śrī is the root form. Puṣṭi and others are her aṁśas. Vidyā is jñānam. Āvidyā is bhakti (ā means “correct”) since bhakti is called *rāja-vidyā.* Māyā is the external śakti. Her functions are another set of Śrī etc. The rest are the same.

In the category of pure bhakti, the śaktis should be considered as functions of the svarūpa-śakti. Because first there is the one tattva is eternity, knowledge and bliss, there is one śakti with three divisions. This is described by Dhruva in Viṣṇu Purāṇa:

*hlādinī sandhinī saṁvit tvayy ekā sarva-saṁsthitau \|*  
*hlāda-tāpa-karī miśra tvayi no guṇa-varjite \|\|*

**In you who are the shelter of all spiritual qualities exist the energies of hlādinī, sandhinī and saṁvit as one entity. In you who are devoid of material qualities, happiness, distress and their mixture found in the material world does not exist. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.12.68**

Śrīdhara Svāmī explains, “Hlādinī produces bliss. Sandhinī is continuity or eternity. Saṁvit is the vidyā-śakti. The one entity is the chief, fixed śakti, emanating from his svarūpa. That one śakti of three types is within you, since you are the support of all beings (*sarva-saṁsthitau*) whereas the śakti related to the three guṇas is not in you. That material śakti is happiness, distress and mixed. The producer of happiness is sattva, which gives satisfaction to the mind. That which gives suffering is tamas, by separation from objects of enjoyment. That which gives happiness and suffering is rajas, generating happiness and suffering from objects. These are not in you because you are devoid of the guṇas. It is said in Sarva-jña-sūkta:

*hlādinyā saṁvid-āśliṣṭaḥ sac-cid-ānanda īśvaraḥ \|*  
*svāvidyā-saṁvṛto jīvaḥ saṅkleśa-nikarākaraḥ \|\|*

**The Lord full of eternity, knowledge and bliss is embraced by the hlādinī and saṁvit śaktis. The jīva, the very form of suffering, is surrounded by his own ignorance.”**

In order of excellence, they are sandhinī, saṁvit and hlādinī. They are the cause of perception of all objects that exist, just as potness is the cause of perceiving all pots. Sometimes Bhagavān is mentioned as a form of *sat* or existence as in *sad eva somyedam agra āsīt*: the Lord as existence existed in the beginning. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.2.1) Sandhinī is that śakti extending to all time, place and objects, by which one maintains existence (*sattām dadhāti*). Saṁvit is that śakti by which one perceives *(saṁvedayati*). Hlādinī is that śakti by which, in an excellent form of saṁvit, one is aware of bliss.

When the root śakti becomes three, by its special function which is self-manifesting, it manifests the Lord’s svarūpa, his svarūpa-śakti or his svarūpa endowed with the svarūpa śakti. All this is viśuddha-sattvam. This manifestation does not depend on anything else. It is saṁvit because it functions as knowledge to give knowledge. It is pure because it is not touched by māyā. When the sandhinī element is predominant it becomes the ādhāra-śakti. When samvit portion is predominant it becomes ātmā-vidyā. When hlādinī, the essential element becomes predominant it becomes guhya-vidyā (secret knowledge--prema). When all three are prominent at the same time, it becomes mūrti (spiritual form). By the ādhāra-śakti, the Lord’s abode manifests. This viśuddha-sattva is different from material sattva. Mārkaṇḍeya speaks to Nārayāṇa Ṛṣi:

*yat sātvatāḥ puruṣa-rūpam uśanti sattvaṁ*  
*loko yato 'bhayam utātma-sukhaṁ na cānyat*

**Nārada recognize your form as śuddha-sattva, from which arises Vaikuṇṭha, a place which has no fear of falling and is endowed with spiritual bliss. SB 12.8.46**

Jñāna, which takes shelter of the worshipper, and which is the very form of this function, is manifested by ātma-vidyā with the other two functions (sandhinī and hlādinī), impelled by jñāna sādhana. Bhakti, where the function takes the form of prīti, is manifested by the guhya-vidyā with the other two functions, impelled by bhakti sādhana.

In Viṣṇu Purāṇa in praising Lakṣmī it is said:

*yajña-vidyā mahā-vidyā guhya-vidyā ca śobhane*  
*ātmā-vidyā ca devi tvaṁ vimukti-phala-dāyinī*

**O glorious Lakṣṁī! You are karma-yoga, aṣṭāṅga-yoga, bhakti-yoga and jñāna-yoga. You give the results including liberation. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.118**

Yajña-vidyā means karma. Mahā-vidyā means aṣṭāṅga-yoga. Guhya-vidyā means bhakti. Ātmā-vidyā means jñāna. Because you are the shelter of all of these, you are these forms, and give various types of liberation (*vimukti*) and various other results (*phala-dāyinī*).

The mūrti-śakti manifests the śrī-vigraha, the form of the Lord. This is called *vasudeva.* Śiva says:

*sattvaṁ viśuddhaṁ vasudeva-śabditaṁ*  
*yad īyate tatra pumān apāvṛtaḥ*  
*sattve ca tasmin bhagavān vāsudevo*  
*hy adhokṣajo me manasā vidhīyate*

**The pure antaḥ-karaṇa is called** ***vasudeva,*** **because there the Supreme Lord appears without coverings. In that antaḥ-karaṇa, I worshipped the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva, beyond the material senses, by offering respects SB 4.3.23**

The meaning is this. *Viśuddham* means it is devoid of any material portion since it is a function of the svarūpa-śakti. That which is sattva, which is especially pure, is called *vasudeva.* Why is this existence of the nature of *vasudeva*? It is so called because *(yat)* in that condition *(tatra*) Vāsudeva (*pumān*) manifests *(īyate).* Though beyond material vision, it is called sattva (for people’s understanding) in the first verse because of similarity to the well known sattva of this world.

However there is a second meaning. Vāsudeva means he who is perceived in *vasudeva* condition. He is famous as the supreme Lord. He is perceived in viśuddha-sattva. The meaning is explained by taking the conventional meaning of Vāsudeva.

Vāsudeva can also mean “he who illuminates the devatās (*vāsayati devam*).” Or *vasu* can mean “everything dwells in him (*vasati asmin*) and *deva* can mean he who shines or plays (*divyati*). Together, these two form the word Vāsudeva.

*dharma iṣṭaṁ dhanaṁ nṝṇāṁ:* the respectable wealth for human beings is possessing *dharma.* SB 11.19.39

Since *vasu* means wealth and wealth means dharma, Vāsudeva can mean “He is revealed *(deva)* by wealth of dharma (*vasu*) in the form of bhakti.”

Thus *vasudeva* means viśuddha-sattva. The vigraha, a self manifesting light, is revealed by knowledge of the Lord.

*kaivalyaṁ sāttvikaṁ jñāna rajo vaikalpikaṁ ca yat*  
*prākṛtaṁ tāmasaṁ jñāna man-niṣṭhaṁ nirguṇaṁ smṛtam*

**Knowledge concerning the jīva apart from the body is in sattva. Knowledge with various options of doubt is in rajas. Knowledge of material life is in tamas. Knowledge concerning me, however, is understood to be beyond the guṇas. SB 11.25.24**

In many places in scripture, knowledge of the Lord is proved to be beyond the guṇas. That is shown by the word viśuddha. It is clear that it is self manifesting, arising from the svarūpa-śakti. Being perceived in sattva also means that the Lord is perceived in the senses which are viśuddha-sattva.

The function of the svarūpa-śakti is described in SB 4.3.23 quoted above. Vāsudeva *(pumān*) appears (*īyate*) uncovered *(apāvṛtaḥ*). If he were material sattva, the Lord would merely be like a reflection. Upādhis on Brahman (material world including sattva) are said to be like a reflection of Brahman. 24 A reflection in a mirror is covered by dirt and then is uncovered by cleaning (but is still not the real form). This form in sattva is meditated upon intensely (*vidhīyate*) by me in my mind. Sattva becomes one with the mind. Otherwise it would not be possible meditate on the Lord with the mind.

“One should simply meditate using the mind. Why is sattva necessary?” It is necessary because (*hi*) the Lord is beyond *(adhoḥ*) knowledge of the senses (*akṣaja*). Instead of *manasā* sometimes *namasā* (by obeisance) is seen. And instead of *hi* the word *anu* is found. The Lord who is revealed by his svarūpa śakti is served (*anuvidhīyate*) with obeisances *(namasā*). The Lord is not revealed by the mind or obeisances. The Lord who is not seen by us is served by us using obeisances. This meaning makes sense.

Since vasudeva is the very form of viśuddha-sattva which reveals the form of the Lord, in a special manifestation there is personification of śakti as the wife of Dharma. Ānakadundubhi is Vasudeva. The form of his wife is understood to be the manifestation of an aṁśa of the Lord’s śakti which appears as śraddhā, puṣṭi etc. should be understood to be a manifestation of the śakti.

*turye dharma-kalā-sarge nara-nārāyaṇāv ṛṣī \|*  
*bhūtvātmopaśamopetam akarod duścaraṁ tapaḥ \|\|*

**Fourth, appearing in the wife of Dharma, he became Nara-nārāyaṇa, and performed severe austerities which give peace to the soul. SB 1.3.9**

*Dharma-kalā* here means the śakti of Dharma., The śuddha-sattva form called vasudeva is this woman who is śakti, since the result was the appearance of the Lord named Nāra-nārāyaṇa in her. She is named Mūrti:

*mūrtiḥ sarva-guṇotpattir nara-nārāyaṇāv ṛṣī*

**Mūrti was the source of the Lord who is the ocean of all auspicious qualities, Śrī Nara-Nārāyaṇa. SB 4.1.52**

In her there was the manifestation (*utpattiḥ*) of Bhagavān with all qualities (*sarva-guṇa*). She was called Mūrti because she was the cause of the manifestation of the form of eternity, knowledge and bliss called Bhagavān. By seeing the result to be manifestation of the Lord, it is concluded that she has the same śuddha sattva nature as Ānakadundubhi.

*vasudevaṁ hareḥ sthāna vadanty ānakadundubhim*

**Vasudeva, who provided the proper place for the appearance Kṛṣṇa, was also known as Ānakadundubhi (sound of drums). SB 9.24.30**

If he were not viśuddha-sattva then the phrase *hareḥ sthānaḥ* becomes meaningless. One should also accept that the appearance of Śrī and other śaktis takes palce by viśuddha-sattva, predominated by a special porition of hlādini or other element. They take three forms: the form of abundance ( of wealth, beauty etc.) of the Lord, the form that creates abundance of mercy, and aṁśas of that abundance of the Lord. They are of two types: without form, just pure śakti, and with form , as persiding deities situated along with the Lord’s form, appearing with him when he appears on earth.

Śukadeva speaks.

Lakṣmī

## 118.

This svarūpa-śakti with unlimited functions is Lakṣmī, the form situated on the Lord’s left side:

*anapāyinī bhagavatī śṛīḥ sākṣād ātmano hareḥ*

**Śrī, who never leaves the Lord's side, appears with him in this world as the representation of his internal potency. SB 12.11.20**

The commentary says, “She is the constant śakti of the Lord because she is the cit form of his svarūpa (*sākṣād ātmanaḥ*). She is non-different from him. Using the word *sākṣāt* differentiates her from māyā.

*vilajjamānayā yasya sthātum īkṣā-pathe ’muyā \|*  
*vimohitā vikatthante mamāham iti durdhiyaḥ \|\|*

**The ignorant jīvas, bewildered by māyā who is ashamed to stand in sight of the Lord, boast about “I” and “mine.” SB 2.5.13**

Her constant nature is described:

*paramātmā hari devas tac-chaktiḥ śrīr ihoditā \|*  
*śrīr devī prakṛtiḥ proktā keśavaḥ puruṣaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*na viṣṇunā vinā devī na hariḥ padmajāṁ vinā \|\|*

**The Lord, ātmā in all beings, arose here with his Śrī. She is called Devī, Prakṛti, and he is called Keśava. She is never without Viṣṇu and he is never without her. Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra**

*nityaiva sā jagan-mātā viṣṇoḥ śrīr anapāyinī \|*  
*yathā sarva-gato viṣṇus tathaiveyaṁ dvijottama \|\|*

**O brāhmaṇa! The mother of the universe, Śrī, is eternally the consort of Viṣṇu. Just Viṣṇu is everywhere she is also. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.8.17**

*evaṁ yathā jagat-svāmī deva-devo janārdanaḥ \|*  
*avatāraṁ karoty eṣā tathā śrīs tat-sahāyinī \|\|*

**Just as the Lord of the universe Janārdana incarnates in this world, his consort also incarnates with him. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.142**

*aparaṁ tv akṣaraṁ yā sā prakṛtir jaḍa-rūpikā \|*  
*śrīḥ parā prakṛtiḥ proktā cetanā viṣṇu-saṁśrayā \|\|*  
*tām akṣaraṁ paraṁ prāhuḥ parataḥ param akṣaram \|*  
*harir evākhila-guṇa akṣara-trayam īritam \|\|*

**Prakṛti who is inferior and eternal is insentient in form. Śrī is superior prakṛti, conscious and under the direct shelter of Viṣṇu. She is eternal and superior. The Lord endowed with all qualities is superior to that and eternal. Thus there are three eternal entities. Skanda Purāṇa.**

*kalā-kāṣṭhā-nimeṣādi- kāla-sūtrasya gocare \|*  
*yasya śaktir na śuddhasya prasīdatu sa me hariḥ \|\|*  
*procyate parameśo yo yaḥ śuddho’py upacārataḥ \|*  
*prasīdatu sa no viṣṇur ātmā yaḥ sarva-dehinām \|\|*

**May the pure Lord whose śakti is beyond the scope of time with its divisions of second be merciful to me. May Viṣṇu who is the soul of all beings, who, though one with her is called the lord of Lakṣmī to indicate some difference, be merciful to me. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.45-46**

Śrīdhara Svāmī explains, “Lakṣmī, the Lord’s śakti, is not subject to the thread-like (*sūtra*) time units, since she is the controller of all the activities in the universe. She is not dependent on time and thus is eternal, since she is non-different from the Lord’s svarūpa. Since she is non-different from him, she is said to belong the pure lord (*śuddhasya*).”

“If Lakṣmī is non-different from Viṣṇu why is he called the husband of Lakṣmī?” He is called the lord (*īśaḥ*) of Lakṣmī (*para mā).* Though he is pure, or one with her, he is called the Lord of Lakṣmī with the intention of indicating difference (*upacārataḥ*). The use of the word *yaḥ* a second time (*yo yaḥ*) indicates his fame, as in the following usage.

*eṣa prapanna-varado ramayātma-śaktyā*  
*yad yat kariṣyati gṛhīta-guṇāvatāraḥ*  
*tasmin sva-vikramam idaṁ sṛjato ’pi ceto*  
*yuñjīta karma-śamalaṁ ca yathā vijahyām*

**When I create this universe endowed with his power, may the Lord, who benedicts the surrendered souls and manifests attractive qualities when he appears in this world by his internal energy, engage my mind in his pastimes which he performs, so that I can avoid the sins involved in creating. SB 3.9.23**

*sākṣāt śrīḥ preṣitā devair dṛṣṭvā taṁ mahad adbhutam*  
*adṛṣṭāśruta-pūrvatvāt sā nopeyāya śaṅkitā*

**Lakṣmī, though requested by all the devatās, after seeing him in an astonishing form never seen or heard of before, did not approach the Lord out of fear. SB 7.9.2**

This concerns the appearance of Nṛsiṁha. She did not approach out of fear, caused by this form never before seen or heard of. Therefore it is correctly said that she never leaves his side. Sūta speaks the verse.

## 119.
