Thus the Lord has a form of eternity, knoweldge and bliss, is endowed with unlimited marvelous, inconceivable śaktis arising from his svarūpa, is without qualities and with qualities, is without difference, but possessing various differences, is without form but also with form, is all-pervading but also medium sized. All this is true. He is the ocean of unlimited contrary qualities. He has a complete self-manifesting vigraha arising from his svarūpa, beyond the gross and subtle material bodies. He has unlimited vigrahas with one chief form called Bhagavān with the same nature. He is endowed with Lakṣmī on his left side, the manifestation of his svarūpa-śakti, similar in nature to himself. His form exists in his own abode along with his associates and paraphernelia, special forms of his effulgence. He astonishes even ātmārāmas by his amazing qualities and pastimes which arise as the action of his svarūpa-śakti. He manifests in a general form as Brahman and as the taṭastha-śakti called jīva whose very life is in the Lord’s shelter. He possesses his shadow śakti which produces unlimited universes. This Bhagavān is described by scriptures whose meaning is realized by the wise. His nature is different from all other objects. The ignorant, who do not see the Lord, conclude that such a person cannot exist. This work was produced to create faith in persons desiring knowledge. On the one hand, Bhagavān is beyond the knowledge of the ignorant. But he can be known by the Vedas.

*ka iha nu veda batāvara-janma-layo ’gra-saraṁ*  
*yata udagād ṛṣir yam anu deva-gaṇā ubhaye*  
*tarhi na san na cāsad ubhayaṁ na ca kāla-javaḥ*  
*kim api na tatra śāstram avakṛṣya śayīta yadā*

**Everyone in this world has recently been born and will soon die. So how can anyone here know this topic, since Mahā-viṣṇu existed prior to everything else and gave rise to sense** ***devatās*** **and rulers of planets like Brahmā? When he lies down and withdraws everything within himself, nothing else remains—no gross or subtle matter or bodies composed of these, no force of time or scriptures. SB 10.8.24**

O Bhagavān (*bata*)! What person, recently born and soon to die (*avara-janmālayaḥ*) knows you, eternally perfect *(agrasaram*) in this world *(iha*)? The reason that the Lord is eternal and others are recent is given. This also gives the reason why the jīvas have no knowledge. From you (*yataḥ*) Brahmā (*ṛṣiḥ*) arose. Thus all others are more recent. When you took (*avakṛṣya)* the scriptures, Vedas, which reveal you to Vaikuṇṭha and took rest (*śayīta*), closing your eyes to the activities of the universe, at that time there was no method of knowledge for the jīvas sleeping along with you---because at that time there was not gross matter like ākāśa (*sat*) and there was no subtle matter like Mahat-tatatva. Nor was there a combination of gross and subtle--the body. There was no influence of time. Time is the subtle cause of disturbance of prakṛti. Thus there were no senses or prāṇas. That is the meaning. When after creation, when the jīvas take on the upādhi of bodies, the jīvas are not capable of understanding the Lord since they are contaminated under the control of time and karma. When there is destruction, and all these things do not exit, they cannot realize you since sādhana is absent because of deep sleep in great tamas and the Vedas have disappeared.

*na taṁ vidātha ya imā jajānānyad yuṣmākam antaraṁ babhūva*  
*nīhāreṇa prāvṛtā jalpyā cāsu-tṛpa uktha-śāsaś caranti \|\|*

**You do not know him who produced these creatures, who dwells within you. Surrounded by ignorance, uttering mantras to maintain themselves, the priests carry out their actions.** ***Ṛg Veda*** **10.83.7**

*yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manasā saha*

**Not understood by the mind, the Lord cannot be described by words. Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.4.1**

*ko addhā veda ka iha prāvocat \| kuta āyātā kuta iyaṁ visṛṣṭiḥ*  
*arvāg-devā asya visarjanenātha ko veda yata āvabhūva*

**Who knows clearly, who in this world can proclaim, from where the universe was born, from where this creation has come? The devas came after the creation. Therefore, who knows from where it arose? Ṛg Veda 10.129.6**

*anejad ekaṁ manaso javīyo*  
*nedaṁ devā āpnuvan pūrvam arśad \|*  
*tad-dhāvato’nyān atyeti tiṣṭhat*  
*tasminn apo mātariśvā dadhāti \|\|*

**The Lord is without fear, supreme, faster than the mind. The** ***devatās*** **cannot understand him completely, but he has understood everything since eternity. Standing in one place he surpasses all others who move quickly. Under him, Vāyu supports the movements of all living beings. Īśopaniṣad 4**

*na cakṣur na śrotraṁ na tarko na smṛtir vedo hy evainaṁ vedayati*

**The eyes, ear, logic, smṛti and the Vedas do not give knowledge of the Lord. (quoted by Madhva)**

Śrutis speak to the Lord.

## 120.

The wise can realize Bhagavān directly by bhakti. That is expressed in three verses.

*na paśyati tvāṁ param ātmano 'jano*  
*na budhyate 'dyāpi samādhi-yuktibhiḥ*  
*kuto 'pare tasya manaḥ-śarīra-dhī-*  
*visarga-sṛṣṭā vayam aprakāśāḥ*

**Even Lord Brahmā is to this very day unable to see you, who are superior to the jīva by meditation*****.*** **He cannot know you by logic. So what to speak of others like us, who have been created by Brahmā’s mind, body and intelligence, and who are ignorant. SB 9.8.21**

Brahmā *(ajanaḥ*) does not see you and does not understand you, who are superior to ātmā (*ātmanaḥ param*), not seen by extenal vision. He cannot see the Lord in realization by samādhi or understand the Lord mentally by logic. Brahmā cannot see you, so what to speak of others more recent (*kutaḥ apare*) seeing or understanding you? The reason they are recent is described. Our minds, bodies and intelligence have been created by Brahmā *(tasya*). Mind is sattva indicating devatās. Body is tamas indicating animals. Intelligence is rajas indicating humans. You are unmanifested to us (*aprakāśāḥ*). How can be see you?

## 121.

What others see is then described:

*ye deha-bhājas tri-guṇa-pradhānā*  
*guṇān vipaśyanty uta vā tamaś ca*  
*yan-māyayā mohita-cetasas tvāṁ*  
*viduḥ sva-saṁsthaṁ na bahiḥ-prakāśāḥ*

**Persons with material bodies, dominated by the three guṇas, see only objects composed of the guṇas in waking and dream states, and see only tamas in deep sleep. Their minds are bewildered by your māyā, with knowledge only of external objects. They do not know you, situated in them. SB 9.8.22**

Those who have material bodies do not know you, though you are situated in them (*sva-saṁstham)*. Rather they see the guṇas. Sometimes they see tamas. The three guṇas dominate their intelligence. In deep sleep there is tamas, while in waking or dream state, depending on intelligence, they see objects. They do not see you, who appear as ātmā to all the ātmārāmas, free of the guṇas. The reason for not seeing in all these cases is given. Because *(yataḥ*) their minds are bewildered by māyā they do not know you. Or they do not know you, by whose māyā their minds are bewildered.

Can you be known by reflecting with intelligence? Not at all. You are beyond our knowledge. But you are known by devotees. That is explained in the next verse.

*taṁ tvāṁ ahaṁ jñāna-ghanaṁ svabhāva-*  
*pradhvasta-māyā-guṇa-bheda-mohaiḥ*  
*sanandanādyair munibhir vibhāvyaṁ*  
*kathaṁ vimūḍhaḥ paribhāvayāmi*

**How can I, so bewildered, think of you, who are concentrated knowledge, who are realized naturally by sages like Sananda who have destroyed illusion and the conception of difference caused by the guṇas of māyā? SB 9.8.23**

How can I know you (*tvām*), the shelter of the superior śakti with astonishing functions? What is that svarūpa? It is a form of eternity, knowledge and bliss (*jñāna-ghanam*). Thus sahasra-nāma-stotra says *anirdeṣya-vapuḥ:* the Lord’s form is indescribable. The meaning is this. Since you are composed of knowledge, you should be knowable as the object of intelligent reflection. But overcome with the guṇas of māyā, I am incapable of reflection.

“What is the proof that I am made of knowledge and bliss?” You are directly realized and are the subject of reflection *(saṁvibhāvyam*) by sages who know bhāgavata-tattva such as Sananda, who have destroyed the illusion created by guṇas of māyā. The owls do not recognize the sun with its revelation of qualities but others realize its qualities. Similarly you cannot be known by persons with inexperienced vision, but are realized with your qualities by your wise devotees.

*parāñci khāni vyatṛṇat svayambhūs-*  
*tasmāt parāṅ paśyati nāntarātman \|*  
*kaścid dhīraḥ pratyag ātmānam aikśad-*  
*āvṛtta-cakśur amṛtatvam icchan \|\|*

**The creator created external senses. Therefore man sees external objects, not the inner Lord. A wise man, desiring immortality and turning his eyes inward, sees the Lord within. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.1.1**

*bhaktir evainaṁ nayati bhaktir evainaṁ darśayati \|*  
*bhakti-vaśaḥ puruṣaḥ bhaktir eva bhūyasī \|\|*

**Bhakti alone brings the Lord and bhakti alone enables one to see him. The Lord is controlled by bhakti. Bhakti alone is great. Māṭhara-śruti**

*yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyaḥ*  
*tasyaiṣa ātmā vivṛṇute tanūṁ svām*

**The Lord reveals his form to the person whom the Lord chooses. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.23**

Aṁśumān speaks to Kapila.

In this way Brahman and Bhagavān have been described.

Here ends Bhagavat Sandarbha, the second among the Sandarbhas which use Bhāgavatam as their source, and which contains the expert instructions of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, most revered members of the grand assembly of all Vaiṣṇavas, following the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya-deva, who descended to distribute his worship, in order to purify Kali-yuga.
